## [Strictly Causal Streaming Video Anomaly Detection with Theoretical Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24810) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a strictly causal streaming video anomaly detector that processes each frame in O(1) time and memory without buffering, using a diagonal linear state-space recurrence with a learned decay gate. It provides a closed-form relationship between the recurrence decay spectrum and detection delay, validated on UCSD Ped2 and CUHK Avenue, and reports real on-device latency on Apple M3 Pro hardware. This work addresses a critical gap in video anomaly detection by enabling real-time, edge-deployable processing without the latency and memory overhead of clip buffering. The theoretical link between decay and detection delay offers a principled way to tune models for specific latency requirements, potentially advancing practical surveillance and monitoring systems. The method achieves 67.9% and 70.2% frame-level AUC on Ped2 and Avenue, respectively, trailing prior non-causal SSM baselines in accuracy. The settling delay bound predicted from the learned base decay (57-59 frames) is far above the measured detection delay (1.6 and 18.4 frames), indicating that the event boundary gate, not the base decay, governs responsiveness. End-to-end latency on Apple M3 Pro is 0.74 ms and 0.77 ms per frame (over 1300 FPS).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Video anomaly detection (VAD) aims to identify unusual events in video streams, often using CNN or Transformer models that trade off accuracy for speed. State space models (SSMs) like Mamba have been introduced to VAD for faster inference, but existing methods still rely on buffering clips or windows, which introduces latency and memory overhead. This paper proposes a strictly causal streaming approach that processes frames one by one, with a theoretical model linking the recurrence decay to detection delay, and evaluates on edge hardware.

SPO++ corrects the advantage normalization mismatch in Single-stream Policy Optimization (SPO) by standardizing terminal-outcome advantages under the action-token measure, and reorganizes prompt evidence by policy event. It demonstrates improved online learning efficiency over SPO on ALFWorld and Math-TIR benchmarks. This addresses a fundamental flaw in SPO, a method designed to improve scalability and stability in agentic RL. The paper shows that trajectory centering does not center the token-weighted quantity used by the actor, and proposes standardizing advantages under the action-token measure. This paper introduces Evidence-Carrying Termination (ECT), a method that requires tool-using agents to provide typed certificates binding answers to trace evidence, achieving zero unsafe completions in controlled tests. This addresses a critical safety issue in AI agents, ensuring they only stop when their answers are verifiably supported, which could significantly reduce unsafe or premature terminations in real-world applications. In a locked static study across 48 synthetic tasks, ECT produced 0/288 unsafe completions versus 252/288 for the termination-critic core. ## [ToolRobustBench: Stage-Wise Benchmark for Diagnosing Tool-Calling Agent Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23635) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ToolRobustBench is a new stage-wise diagnostic benchmark for tool-calling agents that aligns four perturbation families with the tool-use pipeline and attributes failures to specific stages. Experiments on 15,456 instances across 7 models reveal substantial robustness degradation, with tool-output/observation perturbations being the dominant bottleneck. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM tool-use failures by providing fine-grained diagnostics beyond clean end-to-end accuracy. It offers valuable insights for improving agent robustness, benefiting the AI/ML and software engineering communities. The benchmark includes four perturbation families: tool-interface, user-intent, tool-output/observation, and runtime-environment perturbations, with 14 subtypes. Mixed-family experiments reveal non-additive failure patterns not explained by isolated single-family results, indicating cascade effects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-calling agents are LLM systems that select tools, supply structured arguments, and interpret returned feedback. Traditional clean end-to-end evaluation cannot identify where failures originate or how they propagate. Stage-wise benchmarks like ToolRobustBench align perturbations with pipeline stages to attribute errors to specific components, enabling more targeted improvements.

## [Failed Tool Calls in Transcripts Increase Repeat Failures in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23651) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.23651) demonstrates that recording failed tool calls and error messages in agent transcripts increases the likelihood of small language models repeating the same failure, contrary to the assumed corrective benefit. The study measured a negative corrective gain across six instruction-tuned checkpoints (135M-1.7B parameters) in simulated tool calling and MBPP program repair environments. This finding challenges a common assumption in agent design that error feedback is inherently corrective, and it has significant implications for how AI agents are built, especially for small language models used in cost-sensitive or private deployments. The result could lead to new best practices for handling failed tool calls in agent harnesses, potentially improving the reliability of AI agents in real-world applications. The study found that the probability of repeating a failed call rises from 0.06 to 0.54 over a fixed candidate set, and greedy decoding reproduces the failed call token-for-token on 19% of items after the failure versus 0% before. Counterfactual experiments show that the failed call's surface form accounts for 83% of the damage, while the semantic marking of failure has a small and inconsistent effect. Replacing the verbatim call with a runtime-generated description removes 76% of the inversion at no token cost, but an explicit 'do not repeat' instruction and deleting the failed attempt do not help.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Small language models (SLMs) are increasingly used in agentic AI systems due to their cost-effectiveness and privacy benefits, often defined as models with 10-30 billion parameters that fit within consumer RAM. Tool calling is a key capability that allows language models to invoke external functions and APIs, bridging the gap between language generation and real-world actions. Agent harnesses typically record failed tool calls and error messages in the transcript, assuming this provides corrective information to the model.

The paper introduces Pufibara, an agent harness that maintains persistent engineering state and associates simulation evidence with the candidate that produced it, and proposes the 232-task Modelica Agent Workflow Benchmark for evaluating end-to-end Modelica agent workflows. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent evaluation for physical system modeling, where correctness depends on physical consistency and scenario-dependent behavior, not just syntax and executability. The benchmark and harness could significantly impact simulation-driven engineering by enabling more reliable and efficient AI-assisted modeling. Pufibara makes submission an explicit agent action and uses a benchmark-owned evaluator outside the agent loop. With DeepSeek v4 Flash, Pufibara passes 202 tasks vs. 185 for Claude Code; with Claude Sonnet 5, it passes 202 vs. 187, while using 76.4%-82.5% lower logical-token totals and 6.1%-58.4% lower sequential runtime.

## [Elastic KV Cache Reclaims Idle Memory, but Chunked Prefill Already Suffices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23658) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes an elastic KV cache that dynamically lends reserved memory to the KV pool during decode and returns it before prefill, improving memory utilization without kernel changes. The mechanism is implemented in pure userspace on the CUDA virtual-memory path, with decommit in milliseconds and recommit in tens of milliseconds. This work addresses a common inefficiency in LLM serving engines, where reserved memory for worst-case prefill sits idle during decode-dominant phases. If effective, it could improve GPU memory utilization and throughput, but the paper's honest negative result suggests that chunked prefill already closes the gap, limiting practical impact. The elastic KV cache uses two physical handles mapped into one contiguous virtual range per layer, keeping the attention kernel unchanged and requiring no driver patch. It works with CUDA graphs and prefix caching, and never triggers an out-of-memory event, but a static commit of the same memory is unsafe and crashes on prefill bursts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM serving engines like vLLM partition GPU memory at startup into fixed regions, with the KV cache being the scarce resource that bounds concurrency and context length. The CUDA virtual memory management API (introduced in CUDA 10.2) allows fine-grained control over physical memory allocation and virtual address mapping, enabling userspace mechanisms like the one proposed. Chunked prefill is an existing technique that splits large prefills into smaller chunks, which can reduce peak memory usage and improve scheduling flexibility.

## [Audit Reveals On-Device LLM Miscalibration and Overconfidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23663) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new reliability audit of a deployed on-device language model reveals task-asymmetric miscalibration, with 69% confabulation on false premises and 18% over-refusal of benign inputs, alongside saturated, non-discriminative confidence scores (AUROC 0.47, ECE 70). The study also finds that confident-correct and confident-wrong outputs are surface-indistinguishable, with a classifier achieving only 0.55 AUROC. This matters because on-device models are shipped to hundreds of millions of devices without server-side moderation, and this audit shows that users and developers cannot easily tell when the model is wrong, undermining trust and safety. The findings highlight the urgent need for better calibration and oversight mechanisms in deployed AI systems. The audit introduces a model-agnostic protocol, a surface-indistinguishability test, and released code and frozen evaluation items. A black-box consistency wrapper, requiring no model access, reduces confident confabulation from 75% to 3% and improves selective accuracy from 43% to 83%, at a tunable cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: On-device language models are increasingly deployed directly on user devices, such as smartphones, without server-side oversight. Calibration refers to how well a model's confidence scores align with its actual accuracy; AUROC and ECE are common metrics for discrimination and calibration. Confabulation, a term preferred over 'hallucination' by some, describes when models generate plausible but false information.

A new arXiv paper (2608.23705) demonstrates that current automatic evaluation methods, including LLM-as-a-Judge, significantly misalign with human judgments of creativity in LLM-generated text. The study reveals that LLM judges systematically prefer AI-generated stories over human-written ones, and widely used automatic metrics show near-zero correlation with human assessments. This research highlights a critical flaw in how LLM outputs are evaluated, particularly for creative tasks, which could mislead model development and deployment decisions. It underscores the need for more robust, human-aligned evaluation frameworks as LLMs become more capable in creative domains. The study used the WritingPrompts dataset and collected human evaluations across 11 creativity dimensions, comparing them with automated metrics and LLM-as-a-Judge. The findings show that LLM judges favor stylistic characteristics of AI-generated text over human unpredictability, and correlation analyses reveal near-zero alignment for common automatic metrics.

## [EXAM^2: A New Benchmark for Multilingual and Multimodal Audio Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23758) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced EXAM^2, a benchmark for multilingual and multimodal audio understanding that spans six languages and multiple modalities, including speech, sound, music, mixed-audio, and visual images. It comprises 5,667 multiple-choice questions, 22,614 image instances, and 135,684 multilingual translations, and also proposes a fine-tuned model, Gemma3n-EXAM^2, which improves performance by up to 12.4% in multilingual settings and 21.7% in multimodal evaluation. This benchmark addresses the gap in existing LALM evaluations, which are largely English-centric and focus on single audio domains. By incorporating multilingual and cross-modal scenarios, EXAM^2 enables more realistic evaluation of scene-aware audio reasoning and paves the way for future research in multilingual and multimodal audio intelligence. The benchmark includes six languages and multiple modalities, such as speech, sound, music, mixed-audio, and visual images, enabling cross-modal comprehension evaluation. The proposed Gemma3n-EXAM^2 model is a lightweight fusion-model fine-tuned on EXAM^2-train, achieving significant improvements over a strong baseline, while state-of-the-art LALMs and multimodal LLMs still show substantial performance gaps.

## [TrustShiftProbe: Analyzing and Defending Staged Trust Attacks on MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23763) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces TrustShift, a novel staged trust attack on MCP servers where a compromised server behaves benignly initially and then switches to adversarial payloads after an interaction threshold. The authors propose TrustShiftProbe, a framework that includes a threat model, an attack engine, a defense called SHIELD, and a taxonomy of nine attack variants. MCP is a critical standard for connecting LLM agents to external tools, and TrustShift exploits a temporal evasion that bypasses predeployment static analysis and runtime middleware filters. This research highlights a significant security gap in AI agent infrastructure, with attacks achieving a 69.5% success rate across frontier models, underscoring the need for robust defenses like SHIELD. TrustShift attacks are categorized into nine variants across three execution mechanisms (structural violation, semantic corruption, scope expansion) and three adversarial objectives (disruption, exfiltration, and combination). SHIELD, a multi-tier zero-oracle runtime defense, reduces the mean attack success rate from 69.5% to 42.7% across frontier proprietary and open-weight models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an application-level protocol that standardizes how AI assistants connect to external tools and data sources, creating security boundaries at transport, protocol, and data layers. Traditional attacks like indirect prompt injection originate from user prompts, while man-in-the-middle attacks target the transport; TrustShift uniquely originates from the server-controlled tool channel, where the adversary is the trusted server endpoint itself. Staged trust attacks exploit the agent's tendency to build reliance on a server during a benign conditioning phase, suppressing skepticism before the server defects.

## [EmoTra-TTS Enables Smooth Intra-Utterance Emotion Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23791) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EmoTra-TTS is a new emotional text-to-speech system that enables smooth intra-utterance emotion transitions using multi-pass flow blending and frame-level VAD conditioning. It adds only +0.43% parameters with no latency overhead and achieves 30%-87% relative improvement on emotion transition quality. This addresses a key limitation in emotional TTS, where emotions are typically static per utterance, misaligning with the continuous nature of human affect. By enabling controllable intra-utterance transitions, it could significantly improve expressive speech synthesis for applications like audiobooks, virtual assistants, and interactive media. The system uses a multi-pass flow blending pipeline to synthesize frame-aligned transition audio, dual-stage VAD conditioning to guide prosodic planning and acoustic realization, and direction-magnitude decoupled injection to prevent content degradation. It achieves 64.4%-79.5% overall win rates in pairwise preference tests against four SOTA baselines and two commercial systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional emotional TTS systems condition on a single discrete label or static embedding per utterance, which fails to capture the dynamic nature of emotions. The Valence-Arousal-Dominance (VAD) model provides a continuous three-dimensional framework for representing emotional states, which EmoTra-TTS leverages for frame-level conditioning. LLM-based TTS systems may vary prosody implicitly but lack explicit control, which EmoTra-TTS addresses.

LUCAID, an agentic multimodal AI system for lung cancer precision pathology, was developed and clinically validated, integrating nine diagnostic modules covering the full routine workflow from tumor detection to structured report generation. In prospective clinical validation, it achieved 93.0% concordance with an expert-panel adjudicated reference standard, outperforming five experienced thoracic pathologists (68.3-81.1%). This is significant because it addresses a critical gap in precision oncology by providing a clinically validated, comprehensive AI system that covers the entire pathology workflow, potentially reducing interobserver variability and improving diagnostic accuracy. It could impact clinical practice by assisting pathologists in making more consistent and accurate treatment decisions for lung cancer patients. The analysis modules achieved F1 scores of 0.82-0.95 against large-scale expert ground-truth annotations. The system includes modules for quality control, tumor detection and segmentation, histological subtyping, tumor microenvironment profiling, tumor cellularity quantification, and predictive biomarker scoring (PD-L1, MET, TROP-2), and enables interactive querying and automated report generation.

## [ShardMeter: Predicting Sharded Training Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23840) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ShardMeter is a lightweight analytical model that predicts end-to-end runtime and identifies bottlenecks for sharded, distributed, and geo-distributed training of transformer models. It estimates per-GPU and per-island throughput, training cost, and wall-clock time across arbitrary hardware topologies. This addresses the impracticality of exhaustive benchmarking and manual tuning in large-scale AI training, enabling practitioners to quickly explore configuration spaces and choose near-optimal deployment plans. It provides valuable insights into diminishing returns and compute/communication transitions, which are crucial for efficient resource allocation in multi-cluster and decentralized training. The model accounts for tightly coupled factors such as model size, GPU memory, batch size, bandwidth, and sharding strategy. It reveals diminishing-return regimes as island size increases and quantifies transitions between compute- and communication-bound scaling, as well as cost-throughput trade-offs for decentralized training.

## [Systematic Security Analysis of Google's AP2 v0.2 Reveals High-Risk Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23858) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A systematic security analysis of Google's Agent Payments Protocol (AP2) v0.2 identifies vulnerabilities in pre-authorization phases and deployment architectures, extending prior work on AP2 v0.1. The analysis models 48 threats, with eight reaching the High band in at least one architecture, and provides proof-of-concept demonstrations for all. This analysis is significant because AP2 is a new protocol for LLM-driven payments, and the identified vulnerabilities could impact e-commerce and agent frameworks. The findings highlight that valid mandate signatures alone do not ensure user intent, which is crucial for trust in agent-mediated transactions. The analysis divides the transaction lifecycle into five phases and identifies five deployment architectures, using the MAESTRO framework to model four threat actors, eleven attack surfaces, eighteen adversary capabilities, and six attacker goals. The resulting catalog contains 48 threats across five attack families, scored with AIVSS, and a deployment-aware scanner was developed to map applicable threats.

**Background**: AP2 is an open protocol introduced by Google to enable LLM-driven shopping agents to authorize and execute payments on behalf of users. It builds on the Agent-to-Agent Protocol (A2A) and the Model Context Protocol (MCP), which are standards for agent communication and tool integration, respectively. The protocol uses signed Checkout and Payment Mandates to protect transaction data after signing, but pre-authorization interactions remain unprotected.

## [Revelation Control: A Theory for Decision-Focused Information Disclosure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23860) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel theoretical framework called Revelation Control, which formalizes how to choose priced interventions that reveal hidden state only when it can change a consequential decision. It defines decision-sufficient revelation and revelation depth, and provides a cost-adjusted factorization criterion for determining when additional information is decision-relevant. This work addresses a fundamental problem in machine learning and decision theory: how to efficiently allocate information-gathering resources in learning systems. It could impact interpretability, active learning, and decision-making in AI systems, offering a principled way to decide what information to acquire or reveal. The framework separates pure information value from productive reuse, embeds static Bayes refinement into state-dependent continuation value, and proves that bounded stop-flip risk alone cannot certify positive expected utility under unrestricted severity. Experiments on Qwen2.5-7B and Mistral-7B-v0.3 show that deeper future-learning probes have positive decision value and productive reuse yields strict equal-compute utility advantages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: In machine learning, models often need to decide whether to acquire additional information (e.g., through interventions or queries) that may improve future decisions. This is related to concepts like active learning, Bayesian experimental design, and decision theory. The paper builds on the idea of sufficient statistics and factorization criteria, extending them to a decision-focused setting where the value of information is measured by its impact on decisions.

## [Data-Driven Models Can Fail in Real-Time Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23885) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that data-driven models, including structured hybrid and neural ODE models, can accurately fit plant data yet yield incorrect economic optima in real-time optimization (RTO). The study uses a vinyl acetate monomer benchmark process to show that these models produce many 'phantom optima' and that the training optimizer itself can introduce errors. This finding challenges the common assumption that good predictive accuracy is sufficient for reliable economic optimization in process systems engineering. It highlights a critical gap between machine learning model validation and real-world decision-making, urging the community to adopt decision-oriented benchmarks before deploying data-driven models in RTO. The paper trains a structured hybrid model (combining known mass balances and thermodynamics with a neural-network closure) and a fully data-driven neural ODE model. Both models pass standard validation tests and show low prediction variance, yet their economic optima differ substantially from the plant's, and stochastic gradient training can drift from optimal weights even with noise-free data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Real-time optimization (RTO) relies on process models to find economically optimal operating conditions. Traditional first-principles models require deep process knowledge, so data-driven alternatives are attractive. Neural ODEs are a class of continuous-depth models where the hidden state dynamics are governed by a neural network parameterizing the derivative in an ODE. Hybrid models combine physical knowledge with machine learning to improve interpretability and data efficiency.

## [New Theory Frames Interpretation as Spectral Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23892) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.23892) introduces the abridged core of A Mathematical Theory of Interpretation (MTI), treating interpretation as observer-relative spectral measurement under an access structure. It defines Rational Entropy to measure residual uncertainty and shows that confusability can be a resource, reversing the usual zero-error role. This framework could provide a rigorous theoretical basis for designing interpretation methods in machine learning and information theory, with explicit access assumptions, guarantees, and failure modes. It may also influence philosophy of science by formalizing interpretation as a method-design problem. In the finite-effective regime, the zero set of Rational Entropy is classified, and pairwise confusability is equivalent to uniform atomic collapse. A four-condition certificate characterizes sharp, decodable, medium-faithful, and order-independent readout on a finite commuting code sector, returning typed obstructions when guarantees fail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Information theory, founded by Claude Shannon, quantifies information using entropy, which measures uncertainty. Spectral methods in mathematics and physics decompose operators or signals into eigencomponents, often used in data analysis and quantum mechanics. This paper merges these concepts to model interpretation as a measurement process, where an observer's access structure and utility determine what can be identified.

A new paper proposes learning the Kohn-Sham map with SE(3)-equivariant Fourier neural operators, enabling quasi-linear scaling density functional theory (DFT) without constructing Kohn-Sham orbitals. Trained on 8,504 molecules and solids, the model generalizes to out-of-distribution systems and converges SCFs at Kohn-Sham accuracy. This work addresses the cubic scaling bottleneck of traditional Kohn-Sham DFT, potentially enabling simulations of much larger systems on limited computational resources. It also demonstrates the power of neural operators in learning function-space mappings for scientific computing, which could impact computational chemistry and materials science. The model is a domain-invariant SE(3)-equivariant Fourier neural operator that predicts electron density from the Kohn-Sham potential on real-space grids. It achieves linear-scaling SCFs, demonstrated by converging magnesium dislocation densities with up to 82,500 valence electrons on a single GPU.

Neural operators learn mappings between function spaces, making them suitable for approximating the Kohn-Sham map directly.

## [Luce: Relightable 3D Asset Generation with PBR Gaussians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23943) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Luce introduces a voxelized multimodal Gaussian cloud with dedicated Gaussian primitives for geometry and PBR materials (albedo, metallic-roughness, normals), compressed into a material-aware latent space via a VAE and generated by a rectified-flow transformer from a single image. On Toys4K, it achieves state-of-the-art results, improving FID by 28% over the strongest baseline. This work advances image-to-3D generation by enabling relightable, PBR-compliant assets directly from a single image, which is crucial for seamless integration into game and VFX pipelines. The significant FID improvement and better CLIP alignment on AI-generated images suggest practical impact for content creation and digital asset production. The method uses a rectified-flow transformer conditioned on multi-layer features from a pretrained image encoder, preserving semantic and spatial details. It also outputs an optional textured mesh with tangent-space normal map, and on a new AI-generated image benchmark, it improves CLIP image-alignment score to 0.8519 vs. 0.8299 for the best baseline.

Gaussian splatting is a recent 3D representation that uses Gaussian primitives for efficient rendering, and rectified-flow transformers are a generative modeling approach that learns to transport noise to data distributions.

## [LLM Agent Harnesses Converge Despite Opposing Philosophies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23953) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A source-level multi-case study of three open-source LLM agent harnesses—LangChain's deepagents, Earendil's pi, and DeepSeek's dsh—reveals that despite deliberately opposing design philosophies, the mature harnesses have converged on a common architectural middle form comprising five recurring elements. This convergence suggests that agent harness design is maturing toward a standard set of architectural patterns, which could inform best practices and accelerate development in the AI engineering community. The identified absence of external verifiability highlights a critical gap that may become the next differentiator for provenance-sensitive applications. The five convergent elements are a commoditised loop, an append-only replayable session record, model quirks kept as data, progressive disclosure of context, and explicit extension seams. The study also found that one harness reused another's implementation in one seam, and that external verifiability—a tamper-evident record checkable without trusting the runtime—is absent across all harnesses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the surrounding code that turns a language model into an autonomous agent, handling context building, tool mediation, loop execution, and state persistence. Research has shown that the harness, not the model, often determines agent performance; for example, changing only the harness raised benchmark scores from 52.8 to 66.5 in LangChain experiments. The study's convergence analysis decomposes the observed patterns into parallel discovery, diffusion, and literal reuse.

## [NeuronGuard: Redistributing Safety Signals to Harden LLMs Against Jailbreak and Ablation Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23959) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NeuronGuard introduces a fine-tuning-stage defense that redistributes safety signals across a broader set of neurons, simultaneously hardening LLMs against both jailbreak and neuron-ablation attacks. The method dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons, applies KL-divergence regularization, and uses randomized gradient projection to preserve task utility, achieving near-zero attack success rates across multiple models and attack strategies. This work addresses a critical vulnerability in LLM safety alignment by targeting the common weakness of safety information concentration in sparse neurons. It provides a unified defense against both jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which is significant for deploying robust and safe LLMs in real-world applications. NeuronGuard provides a formal guarantee that it strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound. Experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.

**Background**: Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) aims to make outputs safe, accurate, and aligned with human values, but it remains brittle against attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment, both exploiting the concentration of safety-relevant information in a sparse neuron subset. Ablation studies, which remove components to study system behavior, are related to neuron ablation attacks that can cause catastrophic collapse in models like LLaVA and InstructBLIP.

## [RAGSentinel: Training-Free Defense Against RAG Document Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23965) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RAGSentinel introduces a training-free, label-free defense against adversarial document injection in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems, using geometric consensus on hidden-state shifts to filter poisoned documents. The method is proven to recover a poison-free majority-sized context under honest-majority and representation-separation conditions. This defense addresses a critical security vulnerability in RAG systems, which are widely used to ground LLM responses in external documents. By providing a training-free and label-free solution with theoretical guarantees, RAGSentinel offers a practical and robust defense that can be deployed in black-box settings, potentially improving the security posture of AI applications. RAGSentinel uses a surrogate encoder to measure query-conditioned hidden-state shifts, removes shared topic directions, and filters poisoned documents as geometric outliers from a robust majority consensus. Experiments across three QA datasets, three LLM families, and multiple poisoning attacks show low attack success rates while preserving competitive accuracy, even against adaptive attacks with full pipeline knowledge.

Existing defenses rely on instruction following, parametric knowledge, or text-level consistency, which adaptive attackers can imitate or optimize against. RAGSentinel leverages geometric properties of hidden-state shifts to identify and filter poisoned documents without requiring training or labels.

## [LLM Inference Degradation: A Supply Chain Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper argues that LLM providers' cost-saving strategy of degrading service during congestion is flawed because it ignores retry and churn costs, and proposes a supply-chain-inspired model for optimal inference allocation. This matters because it challenges the prevailing industry practice of throttling during peak demand, showing that such reactive policies can actually increase traffic and permanently degrade service quality, affecting both provider economics and user experience. The model uses three classical primitives: a newsvendor model for stockout cost, a geometric retry multiplier, and a two-regime transient queue with endogenous arrival rates. It shows that a cheaper model can save energy per satisfied answer while consuming more capacity per satisfied answer, and that reactive throttling can cross an ignition threshold, manufacturing more traffic than it sheds.

However, this paper argues that such accounting is flawed because it ignores the costs of retries and customer churn. The newsvendor model is a classic inventory management framework for uncertain demand, and queuing theory studies waiting lines, both of which are applied here to model inference allocation.

SAGE is a new multi-agent framework that reformulates Chinese ancient document understanding as evidence-grounded inference instead of direct answer generation. Experiments show that SAGE with Qwen3.5-9B surpasses much larger monolithic LVLMs on most evaluated metrics on the AncientDoc benchmark.

WebMCP-Phalanx introduces a dual-layer runtime architecture to enforce trust boundaries for browser-integrated LLM agents, addressing prompt injection and provenance issues in the emerging W3C WebMCP standard. The first layer provides a browser-native trust anchor with cryptographic capability credentials, while the second layer separates semantic inspection (Q-LLM) from privileged execution (P-LLM). This work addresses a critical security gap in the emerging WebMCP standard, which enables LLM agents to invoke tools exposed by web pages. By mitigating prompt injection and provenance spoofing, it enhances the safety and reliability of browser-integrated AI agents, potentially influencing future web security standards and agent design. The browser-native ownership mechanism reduces revocation and overwrite attack success from 100% to 0%, and the dual-agent runtime blocks all 80 prompt-injection attempts in tool descriptions, limiting tool-return attacks to 2 out of 80. However, under a white-box adaptive attacker, description-based filtering can be bypassed via malicious tool names, motivating a call-timing gate that delays invocation until all metadata is validated.

Attnlocate is a novel runtime framework that localizes behavior-guiding instructions in LLM agents by detecting attention activation traces, treating the localization as an object detection task. It achieves a mean IoU of 0.743, an average AUROC of 0.956, and a 0.934 true-positive rate at 0.067 false-positive rate across ten agent configurations from five LLM families. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in LLM agents—prompt injection—by providing a runtime detection method that works during model reasoning, unlike static defenses. It enhances AI safety and could be integrated into agent systems to prevent unauthorized tool calls, benefiting developers and users of LLM-based applications. Attnlocate uses a multi-head, multi-layer attention aggregation scheme to construct a token-level feature space, then deploys a 1-D U-Net with an anchor-free detection head to detect behavior-guiding spans. It dynamically adjudicates malicious invocation attempts based on the authority of the provider of the detected spans, and it transfers across unseen models and supports authority policy adaptation without retraining.

HSR first decomposes a target task into candidate skill sequences, evaluating each plan based on semantic plausibility and skill reliability. It then performs hybrid retrieval combining subtask-level language retrieval with behavior-feature reranking, followed by a two-stage pretraining and finetuning pipeline. ## [Circuit-Grounded Framework for Controllable Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24065) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a circuit-grounded framework that connects training-dynamics-based data valuation with mechanistic interpretability, using model-internal circuits as controllable interfaces to steer data generation. It introduces SAMS (Stage-Aware Mechanistic Scheduling) to schedule circuit-steered data according to the model's evolving optimization needs, demonstrating improved downstream performance and calibration on multiple-choice QA tasks. This work establishes a principled white-box paradigm for interpretable data generation, pioneering the use of mechanistic interpretability not just as an analytical tool but as a practical, controllable interface. It could significantly impact data quality and model training, offering a more transparent and precise alternative to heuristic prompt-based control. The framework conceptualizes data quality along three utility axes: learnability, challenge, and alignment. Experiments on multiple-choice QA tasks show that the approach yields precisely controlled data with greater diversity than prompt-based baselines, consistently improving downstream performance and calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability is a subfield of explainable AI that aims to understand neural networks by analyzing their internal structures, algorithms, and circuits, similar to reverse engineering software. Data valuation in machine learning studies the worth of data, often using training dynamics to identify influential or noisy examples. This paper bridges these two areas by using circuits as interfaces for data generation.

<a id="item-102"></a>
## [Bayesian Self-Escalation Enables LLM Agents to Ask for Help Mid-Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a Bayesian self-escalation mechanism for hierarchical LLM agents, allowing them to transfer control to a stronger model during reasoning based on learned competence estimates. It provides closed-form thresholds, dynamic programming characterization, and theoretical guarantees including exponential separation and regret bounds. This addresses a significant gap in current LLM agent systems, which typically decide delegation before reasoning or after completion, by enabling adaptive mid-reasoning escalation. It could improve efficiency and reliability of LLM agents, with potential impact on AI safety and resource allocation in hierarchical systems. The method models intra-generation delegation as a Bayesian optimal-stopping problem over a learned competence posterior, with sufficient statistics learned from labeled trajectories. Theoretical results include a time-varying threshold optimal policy, exponential separation at the Chernoff-information rate, and a finite-sample regret bound of 1/sqrt(n). A real-model validation on a Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B->7B code cascade (MBPP, 257 tasks) confirmed two of three pre-registered predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Hierarchical LLM agents use a tiered design where higher-level agents plan and delegate tasks to lower-level ones. Current systems typically decide delegation before reasoning (via a router) or after a response (via a verifier), but this paper explores a third regime: escalation during reasoning. Bayesian optimal stopping theory provides a framework for deciding when to stop and take action, and Chernoff information measures the exponential rate of error decay in hypothesis testing.

## [Cross-Script Safety Gaps in LLM Hate Speech Detection for Urdu](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24191) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This study reveals significant cross-script safety inconsistencies in LLM hate speech detection for Urdu, with a 'Missed-in-Urdu' rate of up to 9.9% across five major LLMs, and finds zero dedicated Urdu papers in nine years of WOAH proceedings. This matters because it exposes a critical blind spot in LLM safety evaluation for low-resource languages, showing that content moderation can fail to flag harmful content in Urdu scripts, potentially allowing hate speech to spread unchecked. It underscores the urgent need for multilingual safety benchmarks and more inclusive evaluation practices. The study tested GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Qwen-2.5, and Llama-3.1 across six datasets covering Nastaliq Urdu, Roman Urdu, English, and code-switched Urdu-English. Label instability between original-script and English-translation classification ranged from 15.9% to 31.6%, with smaller open-weight models showing higher instability and missed-harm rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Urdu is the world's tenth most spoken language with 246 million speakers, yet it is almost entirely absent from mainstream LLM safety evaluation. Nastaliq is the default Arabic script variant for Urdu, written right-to-left, and code-switching between Urdu and English is common in everyday communication. The ACL Anthology API provides access to metadata for all papers in the Anthology, which was used to confirm the absence of dedicated Urdu papers in WOAH proceedings.

## [CBPO: Disentangling Budget Allocation and Credit Assignment in RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24300) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Contrastive Branch Policy Optimization (CBPO), a method that separately handles rollout budget allocation and token-level credit assignment in reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). CBPO uses generation entropy to screen branch positions and path/node-level decay to distribute the budget, while defining Contrastive Branch Value (CBV) to rescale continuation advantages without changing their sign. CBPO addresses a known challenge in multi-turn LLM tool use by disentangling budget allocation and token-level credit assignment, which existing methods often conflate. It consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods on ten benchmarks across two model scales, offering a practical solution for fine-grained credit assignment in tool-integrated agent training. CBPO requires only outcome rewards and no process-level annotation. When multiple nodes are selected along the same trajectory, it partitions the trajectory into non-overlapping credit segments to avoid duplicated gradients on shared tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) enables language models to learn multi-turn interactions with external tools, but sparse outcome rewards provide no signal for identifying which intermediate decisions are responsible for success. Branch sampling induces local comparisons among alternative continuations, but existing methods tend to conflate allocating a fixed rollout budget and translating branch outcomes into token-level credit. CBPO disentangles these two problems and assigns a dedicated mechanism to each.

## [FARCA: Reliability-Aware Credit Assignment Reduces LLM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24350) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FARCA is a new policy optimization framework that transforms coarse-grained factual supervision into localized, reliability-weighted token-level training signals for reinforcement learning. It introduces counterfactual evidence attribution to compute reliability weights, improving factuality while preserving reasoning capabilities. This addresses a critical challenge in RLHF for LLMs: reducing hallucination risk from outcome-driven rewards. By improving credit assignment granularity and reliability, FARCA could enhance the factual reliability of LLMs across various applications, benefiting researchers and practitioners. FARCA aligns fact verification granularity with policy updates and uses counterfactual evidence attribution as a proxy for verification reliability. Experiments across multiple models and factual reasoning benchmarks show significant improvements in factuality without sacrificing general reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) trains LLMs using outcome-driven rewards, which can lead to hallucinations. Existing mitigation methods introduce process-level factual supervision, but coarse-grained aggregation and lack of reliability assessment cause noisy credit assignment. Credit assignment in RL is the problem of determining which actions contributed to a reward, and FARCA addresses this by providing fine-grained, reliability-weighted signals.

<a id="item-106"></a>
## [RACER: Region-Aware Repair of Backdoors in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24354) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RACER is a model-level framework that repairs backdoors in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by detecting and correcting layer-wise representation inconsistencies triggered by backdoor features. It requires only 100 clean samples and no knowledge of the trigger, attack objective, or even whether the model contains a backdoor. This addresses a critical gap in MLLM security: existing defenses are mostly inference-time filters that do not remove the embedded backdoor. RACER's model-level repair could significantly enhance the safety of MLLMs deployed in real-world applications, reducing the risk of malicious manipulation. RACER decomposes the fused representation into visual and textual token regions, normalizes their layer-wise inconsistency separately, and recomposes them using modality-aware weights over a deep-layer window. Evaluations on three open-source MLLMs across 36 backdoor settings show that RACER reduces average ASR to 1.1%, reaching 0% in 32 settings, while preserving clean-task utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate visual and textual inputs, but they can inherit backdoors from training pipelines, where triggers in images or text cause malicious outputs. Existing model-level defenses are designed for conventional classifiers and are ineffective for MLLMs, while inference-time defenses only filter suspicious inputs. RACER leverages the observation that backdoors cause abnormal layer-to-layer evolution in internal representations, termed layer-wise inconsistency anomaly, which is modality-dependent and concentrated in the token region encoding trigger features.

## [E(3)-Equivariant UQ for Tensor Prediction via SPD Covariances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24386) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework for E(3)-equivariant uncertainty quantification in symmetric rank-2 tensor prediction, ensuring positive-definite covariance matrices via matrix exponentiation and irreducible decomposition. The method is validated on ModelNet40 and Materials Project datasets. This work addresses a significant open challenge in geometric deep learning by providing rigorous confidence measures for tensor-valued predictions, which is crucial for applications in physics and materials science. It bridges the gap between equivariant neural networks and uncertainty quantification, potentially enhancing reliability in scientific ML. The covariance is decomposed into irreducible representations: Sym^2(ρ_c) ≅ 2×(l=0) ⊕ 2×(l=2) ⊕ 1×(l=4). The method maps from the flat Lie algebra sym(6) to the SPD manifold via matrix exponentiation, and introduces a Log-Euclidean Equivariant Scoring Objective (LE-ESO) based on the Multivariate Laplace distribution for robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: E(3)-equivariant neural networks are deep learning models that respect the Euclidean group symmetries (rotations, translations, reflections), making them suitable for physical data. Symmetric positive definite (SPD) matrices form a manifold where covariance matrices naturally reside, and matrix exponentiation ensures positive definiteness. Irreducible representations are fundamental building blocks in group theory used to decompose tensor spaces.

## [Forward-Looking Localization Improves SFT by Predicting Post-Tuning Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24482) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a forward-looking localization framework that estimates post-SFT interpretability states from pre-SFT parameters and the target dataset, using Taylor expansion to bridge the gap. It designs dual-granularity (neuron- and component-level) pipelines that guide parameter-efficient supervised fine-tuning (SFT) more effectively than retrospective interpretability methods. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of mechanistic interpretability—its retrospective nature—by enabling prediction of task-critical mechanisms before training. It bridges interpretability and optimization, potentially improving fine-tuning efficiency and alignment for large models, with implications for parameter-efficient tuning and AI alignment research. The framework models SFT as continuous parameter evolution and uses Taylor expansion to theoretically link post-tuning mechanistic objectives with pre-SFT dynamic gradients. Experiments show superior SFT guidance, robust performance, and temporal scalability across increasing model sizes, though the paper is a preprint and its impact is not yet established.

## [INCEPT: Invariance-Oriented EEG Foundation Model Outperforms on Broad Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24597) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced INCEPT, an invariance-oriented EEG foundation model trained on over 11,000 hours of unlabelled clinical EEG, which learns representation-level stability across correlated EEG observations. It achieves state-of-the-art results on a broad benchmark, ranking first on 26 of 30 linear-probing and 24 of 30 fine-tuning metrics across ten datasets. This work challenges the dominant reconstruction-centered paradigm in EEG foundation models, showing that invariance learning can better capture transferable neural representations. It could accelerate progress in brain-computer interfaces, neurological diagnostics, and neuroscience research by providing a reusable model that generalizes across diverse EEG tasks. INCEPT separates stable neural structure and subject-sensitive information from nuisance variability while preserving subject-, state-, and condition-discriminative details. It outperforms task-specific specialist encoders and other EEG foundation models across signal-level, brain-state, and brain-health tasks, with ablations confirming the benefits of invariance-oriented pre-training.

Foundation models, pre-trained on large unlabelled data, aim to learn reusable representations, but most existing EEG foundation models rely on reconstruction objectives, which may not capture the most transferable neural information. Invariance learning, as proposed in INCEPT, focuses on stable representations across correlated observations, potentially better reflecting underlying neural processes.

Microsoft introduced Maia 200, a second-generation AI accelerator that delivers 10,145 Tflop/s FP4 and 5,072 Tflop/s FP8 performance within a 750W TDP and 7 TB/s HBM bandwidth. It implements a new Software Defined Locally Accessed Dataflow (SDLA) architecture that shifts from thread-centric to data-movement-centric design. This architecture addresses the memory bottleneck and scalability challenges in large-scale AI inference, potentially reducing cost and energy consumption for running transformer-based LLMs. It represents a significant shift in accelerator design that could influence future hardware for high-performance computing and AI workloads. The paper introduces a taxonomy of data management inspired by Flynn's taxonomy to classify SDLA architectures. Maia 200 is optimized for Microsoft's inference workloads, supporting massive parallelism for AI inference, and achieves significant cost and energy savings compared to traditional architectures.

## [OPDVR: Combining On-Policy Distillation with Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces On-policy Distillation with Verifiable Reward (OPDVR), a method that seamlessly integrates on-policy distillation (OPD) and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) using a ReLU gating mechanism, without adding any hyperparameters. Experiments on six reasoning benchmarks show that OPDVR consistently outperforms standard OPD. OPDVR addresses a key challenge in LLM post-training by providing dense token-level guidance while ensuring task-level correctness, without the complexity of extra hyperparameters. This could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of RLVR and distillation, benefiting the broader LLM ecosystem. The method reformulates the implicit reward of sampled-token OPD based on trajectory correctness, then applies a ReLU gating to assign non-negative rewards to correct trajectories and non-positive rewards to incorrect ones. This transformation makes sampled-token OPD a proper RLVR method, compatible with any policy gradient algorithm like GRPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) uses rule-based verifiers to provide deterministic rewards for LLM outputs, but suffers from sparse task-level feedback. On-policy distillation (OPD) lets the student generate its own trajectories and receive teacher feedback, providing dense token-level guidance but ignoring trajectory correctness. Combining them is promising but existing methods introduce extra hyperparameters. OPDVR uses a ReLU gating mechanism, a common activation function that outputs zero for negative inputs, to align the distillation signal with task success.

## [FactoMap: Disentangling Representations via Factor-Space Topographic Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24762) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces factor-space structure and proposes FactoMap, a novel framework that learns disentangled representations by modeling factor-space geometry with topographic maps, overcoming limitations of Euclidean product coordinates. FactoMap transfers the lattice's periodicity, collapses, and non-uniform extent to the representation, enabling disentanglement of underlying factors. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in disentangled representation learning by showing that statistically independent factors need not be geometrically separable, and proposes a method that preserves factor continuity and enables disentanglement. It is likely to influence future research in representation learning and geometric deep learning, offering a new perspective on how to model factor spaces. The paper demonstrates that hue and scale produce effects that grow at different rates, yielding anisotropy that no fixed rescaling removes. FactoMap learns interpretable prototypes indexed by a factor-space lattice, and experiments show that matching this structure preserves factor continuity and enables disentanglement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning aims to separate underlying factors of variation into semantically meaningful variables. Topographic maps, inspired by biological self-organizing maps, preserve neighborhood relationships and can model such non-Euclidean structures.

## [LLMs Retrieve Financial Data but Fail to Use It in Judgments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24842) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies a 'retrieval-integration gap' in long-context financial analysis, showing that LLMs can accurately retrieve risk disclosures from 10-K filings yet fail to incorporate them into investment judgments, even as context length grows from 2,000 to 128,000 tokens. This finding challenges the common practice of evaluating AI analysts solely on retrieval accuracy, implying that such systems may ignore critical information in real-world decisions. It underscores that both model capability and workflow architecture determine AI analyst performance, urging a shift toward judgment-based evaluation and better workflow design. The gap persists across model families and judgment tasks, and even when real disclosures are removed from actual 10-K filings. Causal memory interventions reveal that compressed summaries and source-text lookup jointly transmit disclosures into judgments, while chunk-and-summarize pipelines evict relevant information; a targeted, structured restatement adjacent to the decision restores influence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as AI analysts to process financial disclosures and support investment decisions. However, typical evaluations focus on retrieval accuracy—whether the model can find relevant information—rather than whether that information actually influences the model's judgment. This paper introduces the concept of a 'retrieval-integration gap' to describe the disconnect between retrieving and using information, and explores how different workflow architectures (e.g., chunk-and-summarize vs. structured restatement) affect integration.

## [LAION-BVD: 10-Million-Hour Open Video Dataset for Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24845) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LAION-BVD introduces a large-scale open video dataset containing 80 million downloaded videos totaling 10 million hours, collected from 1.3 billion platform-specific URLs from CommonCrawl. The dataset is designed for multimodal pre-training across video, audio, and image modalities, with synthetic captions generated for extracted clips. This dataset significantly expands open access to multimodal video data at an unprecedented scale, potentially enabling new research and model training in multimodal learning. ## [Reinforcement Learning's Three Dogmas Challenged by Artificial Life](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11482) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper critiques the three core tenets of reinforcement learning (RL) — the environment as a Markov decision process, learning as policy optimization, and the agent as a maximizer of scalar reward — and proposes an alternative framework inspired by artificial life, emphasizing adaptation and open-endedness over reward optimization. This conceptual critique addresses significant theoretical gaps in RL, potentially influencing future research directions by shifting focus from reward maximization to adaptation and open-endedness. It could impact fields like AI safety, robotics, and autonomous agents by promoting more biologically faithful models of agency. The paper draws on open-ended novelty search as a complementary model of adaptation and goal-directed behavior, and grounds these evolutionary dynamics in thermodynamic theories of origin-of-life and agency. It argues that the artificial life community is uniquely positioned to concretize an alternative to the current RL paradigm.

ReflCtrl introduces a stepwise steering method that controls self-reflection in large reasoning models by applying interventions only at the start of each new reasoning step, reducing total reasoning tokens by up to 43.2% while preserving accuracy. This work addresses a key inefficiency in reasoning models by showing that self-reflection is often redundant, especially in stronger models, and offers a practical framework to reduce inference costs. It could significantly impact LLM deployment by lowering computational overhead without sacrificing performance. The authors identify a reflection direction in the model's latent space that separates reflection steps from non-reflection steps, and show that activation along this direction is strongly predictive of answer correctness. ReflCtrl outperforms conventional token-level steering at matched token budgets across math and general reasoning benchmarks.

A self-evolving scientific agent, driven by large language models and iterative code generation, autonomously designs and refines whitebox controllers for physical systems, demonstrated on a nonlinear fluid-structure interaction problem. SuperLocalMemory 4.0 introduces a governed, local-first memory operating system for AI agents, featuring multi-channel retrieval via reciprocal-rank fusion, bi-temporal recall, multi-scope isolation, role-based access, verified erasure, and a hash-chained audit trail. The paper also presents a negative result: ten mechanisms were implemented and reachable but ineffective, highlighting the distinction between implemented, reachable, and effective mechanisms. This work addresses the critical challenge of reliable memory management in AI agents, which is essential for long-term autonomy and trustworthiness. The negative result and proposed invariants could guide future research by clarifying that effectiveness requires independent oracles, potentially improving how agent memory systems are evaluated and designed. The reliability spine governs the primary write path with generation-fenced admission, verifiable memory transactions, and hash-checkable completion manifests. Eleven fault-injection scenarios, each repeated 200 times, upheld 2,199 of 2,200 scoped component properties. The paper also withdraws a previous overhead figure, reporting an 11.0 ms governed write with the envelope accounting for 70.6%, but the generation fence costs only 1.9 microseconds and the obligation ledger 42 microseconds.

## [Model Discovery Agent: LLM-Assisted Bayesian Experiment Design for Mechanistic World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Model Discovery Agent (MDA), which combines a novel nested sequential Monte Carlo algorithm (SMC^3) with LLM-driven hypothesis proposal and Bayesian experiment design to efficiently learn mechanistic world models from data. MDA achieves state-of-the-art performance on three existing benchmarks and introduces a new, harder stochastic single-neuron electrophysiology benchmark (HHbench). This work addresses a fundamental challenge in scientific discovery: efficiently identifying causal or mechanistic models from data, which is crucial for answering interventional questions. By integrating LLMs with Bayesian experiment design, MDA could significantly accelerate automated science and improve data efficiency in fields like physics, chemistry, and neuroscience. The SMC^3 algorithm uses three levels of nested sequential Monte Carlo over models, parameters, and latents, enabling robust inference. The LLM proposes new models when the current hypothesis space is insufficient, following M-open Bayesian inference, and the experiment designer maximizes the Value of Information. MDA sets new SOTA on DPbench, CHEMbench, and Boxing benchmarks, and performs well on the new HHbench despite its noise-robust Bayesian foundations.

Toby Ord's new paper on arXiv mathematically analyzes intelligence explosions, showing that singular growth (toward a vertical asymptote) is harder to achieve than recent economics-inspired models suggest. ## [Reconstruction Benchmark: LLMs Struggle to Recover Research Ideas from Bibliographies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16645) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Reconstruction, a blind benchmark that tests whether language models can infer a paper's core idea from its pre-publication bibliography alone. Across six scientific domains and 643 papers, seven frontier models achieved only 3-15% match rates, while a multi-agent pipeline raised this to 23-42%. This benchmark highlights significant limitations in current LLMs' ability to connect bibliographic references to underlying research ideas, which is crucial for literature review and research discovery. The multi-agent approach's success suggests that collaborative reasoning can substantially improve performance, offering a promising direction for future AI research tools. The benchmark employs a strict anti-leakage protocol, including temporal citation cutoff, anonymous reference IDs, and frozen per-paper bibliographies, to prevent prompt-time leakage. The multi-agent pipeline uses cross-model review and a Swiss tournament over aligned hypothesis slots, without external web search, achieving a 2.4x lift over the best single-model baseline.

<a id="item-129"></a>
## [CausalCache: Selective Screenshot Restoration Boosts GUI Agent Success](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22577) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CausalCache introduces a budgeted fidelity restoration method that selectively reactivates historical screenshots for long-horizon GUI agents, improving success by about 13 percentage points on OSWorld-Verified over summary-only memory. The method uses a history-gated key/value adapter that is bypassed when no history image is active, preserving current-screen processing. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in long-horizon GUI agents: limited context for historical screenshots. By showing that selective restoration outperforms recency-based allocation, it could influence future memory management designs in multimodal agents, potentially improving performance on real-world computer tasks. Under the official 15-step limit, CausalCache and Recent-4 are statistically indistinguishable, but in a 30-step diagnostic, CausalCache achieves 46.7% success versus 42.4% for Recent-4. Zero-shot on 117 MobileWorld tasks, CausalCache improves over Recent-4 from 30.2% to 36.8%, with gains concentrated on a cross-app memory-candidate split (30.6% vs. 19.4%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon GUI agents operate in environments like OSWorld, where they must complete tasks by interacting with desktop or mobile interfaces. These agents typically retain action histories as text, but only a few screenshots fit in the active context due to token limits. Budgeted fidelity restoration is a concept where every event remains summarized, but a fixed budget determines which events regain their archived screenshots. OSWorld-Verified is a benchmark for evaluating such agents on real-computer tasks, and MobileWorld is a similar benchmark for mobile tasks.

## [MobilePA-Bench: New Benchmark for Mobile Planning Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23035) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MobilePA-Bench is a new interactive, stateful, and tool-centric benchmark for evaluating mobile planning agents, covering 13 functional domains and 212 realistic mobile tools. It runs on an executable sandbox that maintains live application databases and returns structured feedback, enabling evaluation of sub-agent collaboration, memory usage, and skill usage. This benchmark addresses a critical gap between GUI-centric and static function-calling benchmarks, providing a more realistic evaluation environment for on-device LLM agents. It could significantly impact the development and evaluation of dependable mobile agents, as current frontier LLMs show unreliable performance under strict tool ordering, permission limits, and runtime errors. The benchmark evaluates a central planning agent along three advanced dimensions: sub-agent collaboration, memory usage, and skill usage. It pairs an interactive function-calling sandbox with evidence-based verification, serving as both a diagnostic benchmark and a foundation for agentic reinforcement learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Existing benchmarks for mobile agents fall into two categories: GUI-centric benchmarks that test screen manipulation but overlook background tool use and long-horizon planning, and static function-calling benchmarks that rely on offline API matching detached from real runtime constraints. MobilePA-Bench aims to close this gap by providing an executable sandbox that maintains live application databases and returns structured feedback, allowing for more realistic evaluation of tool-calling and planning abilities.

## [Apodex 1.1: Scaling Agentic Intelligence for Complex Work](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23283) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Apodex 1.1 introduces a framework that scales agentic intelligence along two dimensions: environment scaling and agentic coordination scaling, enabling sustained and verifiable progress on complex tasks. The 35B-parameter Apodex 1.1 Mini retains strong working capability in a locally deployable form. This framework addresses key challenges in scaling AI agents for real-world work, potentially impacting fields like finance, scientific research, and coding. By achieving leading performance with a smaller model, it suggests that efficient scaling of agentic intelligence is feasible, which could democratize advanced AI capabilities. Apodex 1.1 uses a shared execution harness and AgentOS to maintain task state and provenance across tools and agents. Training converts environment trajectories and coordination traces into reliable behavior, and the framework excels in complex professional work, finance, scientific research, mathematics, coding, and search.

## [MediSkill-Evo: Self-Evolving Clinical Agent with Process-Constrained Knowledge Banks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23397) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MediSkill-Evo introduces a self-evolving clinical agent that updates four typed knowledge banks (clinical, process, symbolic, and visual) under type-specific validation and scope rules, without fine-tuning the backbone. It uses a Process-Constrained Preference Harness to ground candidates in evidence and prioritize safer decisions, achieving significant improvements on multiple benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in evidence-grounded clinical interaction by enabling agents to convert experience into reusable process knowledge with explicit provenance and authority. It demonstrates substantial gains in diagnosis accuracy and safety, which could improve the reliability of AI-assisted healthcare and reduce critical failures in clinical settings. Evaluated on 300 MIMIC-IV-derived FullChain encounters, 180 hard-isolation conditions, and 100 multimodal NEJM image-diagnosis cases, MediSkill-Evo improves diagnosis accuracy by 7.81% and treatment-intent coverage by 70.67% over the best prior agent on Qwen FullChain, while reducing critical failures by 43.04%. Under stress, it improves the stress-process composite by 7.77% and required-action completion by 12.41%, with no controller-scored errors in safety checks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive clinical agents operate under partial observability, meaning they must make decisions with incomplete information. Existing agents often struggle to convert experience into reusable process knowledge with explicit provenance and authority. MediSkill-Evo addresses this by maintaining four typed knowledge banks that are updated under type-specific validation and scope rules, and using a Process-Constrained Preference Harness to ensure evidence-grounded and safe decision-making. The approach is evaluated on clinical benchmarks derived from MIMIC-IV and NEJM cases.

## [RL-Based Eulerian Headway Control Reduces Highway Congestion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02520) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a reinforcement learning-based Eulerian control system that regulates aggregate traffic density near bottlenecks via headway commands, leveraging adaptive cruise control (ACC) for improved reactivity and compliance. Two variants, time-headway and distance-headway control, were evaluated in large-scale simulations, improving traffic flow by up to 10.6% over human traffic and 6.7% over traditional variable speed limits (VSL). This work addresses limitations of traditional VSL and Lagrangian control methods by offering a deployable, scalable solution that does not rely on latent driver lane-change intentions. It could significantly improve highway traffic management and pave the way for practical CAV-based congestion mitigation. The system uses headway commands instead of speed commands, and the authors introduce a novel boundary-aware speed metric to address a flaw in simulation studies with dynamic vehicle entry and exit. Both variants outperform baselines in simulations across a range of traffic conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Connected automated vehicles (CAVs) with adaptive cruise control (ACC) enable new approaches to highway congestion mitigation. Traditional Eulerian variable speed limits (VSL) regulate traffic via roadside signs but suffer from infrequent updates and limited driver compliance, while Lagrangian strategies directly control individual vehicles but depend on drivers' latent lane-change intentions in multi-lane settings. This paper proposes an Eulerian control system optimized through reinforcement learning to overcome these challenges.

It also outlines future research directions and open challenges in LLM uncertainty quantification and calibration.

Researchers introduced Quasar, a novel programming language that separates internal code from external tool calls to enhance LLM code actions. It transpiles from a subset of Python and enables features like access control, autoparallelization, and conformal prediction. This innovation addresses limitations of Python for LLM code actions, potentially improving security, latency, and reliability of LLM-driven tool use. It could influence how LLMs interact with external tools in real-world applications. Quasar implements features by annotating external calls with effects and modifying internal code execution. The paper demonstrates access control with batched user queries, autoparallelization of external calls, and conformal prediction for uncertainty quantification.

## [STReason: A Modular Framework Combining LLMs and Spatio-Temporal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20073) ⭐️ 8.0/10

STReason is a novel training-free framework that integrates large language models (LLMs) with spatio-temporal models to perform multi-task inference and generate explanatory rationales without task-specific fine-tuning. It decomposes complex natural language queries into modular programs via in-context learning, executing them to produce both numerical solutions and detailed reasoning. This framework addresses a significant gap in spatio-temporal data mining, where existing models are often limited to narrow tasks and lack multi-task reasoning and long-form explanation capabilities. By grounding explanations in verified computational outputs, STReason suppresses hallucinations common in direct-prompt LLM approaches, potentially reducing expert workload and broadening applicability to real-world scenarios. The framework is evaluated on a new benchmark dataset with a unified evaluation framework and metrics designed for long-form spatio-temporal reasoning. Experimental results show STReason significantly outperforms advanced LLM baselines, especially in complex reasoning-intensive scenarios, and human evaluations confirm its credibility and practical utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Spatio-temporal data mining involves analyzing data that varies across both space and time, such as traffic flow, weather patterns, or disease spread. Traditional models often specialize in single tasks, while large language models (LLMs) excel at reasoning and language understanding but can hallucinate when directly prompted for analytical tasks. In-context learning allows LLMs to perform tasks from examples without updating parameters, enabling frameworks like STReason to combine the strengths of both approaches.

## [CytoNet: Foundation Model for Human Cortex at Cellular Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CytoNet is a new foundation model trained on 1 million unlabeled microscopic image patches from over 4,000 histological sections of nine postmortem brains, using a self-supervised approach based on co-localization in the cortical sheet. It supports multiple downstream tasks including area classification, laminar segmentation, and mapping of cortical subdivisions. This model provides a unified framework for scalable analysis of cortical microarchitecture, potentially accelerating neuroscience research by enabling automated analysis of large-scale histological data. It also offers evidence linking cytoarchitecture to macroscale functional organization, which could deepen our understanding of brain structure-function relationships. CytoNet was evaluated on over 2,000 sections from five additional brains excluded from pretraining, demonstrating generalization. The model uses modified ResNet-50 and hybrid ResNet-50–ViT backbones with a novel SpatialNCE loss, and was trained on the JUPITER supercomputer using 6.5 petabytes of data from 21 postmortem brains on 4,096 NVIDIA Grace Hopper Superchips in less than five days.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: The human cerebral cortex has a complex cellular architecture that is traditionally studied through histological images, but manual analysis is labor-intensive and difficult to scale. Foundation models, which are large pre-trained neural networks, have shown success in various domains by learning general representations from unlabeled data. Self-supervised learning allows models to learn from unlabeled data by creating pretext tasks, such as predicting spatial relationships, which is the basis of CytoNet's co-localization approach.
