## [CAIT Benchmark Reveals Language Bias in Open-Source MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07737) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces CAIT, a benchmark of 400 counter-intuitive visual scenes, and evaluates humans, proprietary models like Claude and Gemini, and 14 open-source MLLMs. It finds that open-source instruction-tuned models perform at chance level due to a strong language prior, unlike humans and proprietary models. This is significant because it reveals a critical limitation in open-source MLLMs: they rely on language priors rather than visual evidence, which can lead to failures in real-world applications where scenes contradict common sense. The findings highlight the need for better evaluation benchmarks and training strategies to improve visual grounding in open-source models. The CAIT benchmark includes scenes like 'a rabbit is chasing a tiger' where visual evidence contradicts common sense. The study shows that Chain-of-Thought reasoning can improve accuracy but slows responses and introduces overthinking, where models refuse to accept visual content that violates physical laws. Targeted fine-tuning and structured prompting can mitigate the language prior bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but they often suffer from biases introduced by language priors, leading to hallucinations. Counter-intuitive scenes, where visual evidence contradicts common sense, are challenging for these models because they may override visual input with statistically common text descriptions. This paper addresses the gap in evaluating MLLMs on such scenes.

This paper introduces a quantum-like extension of the Tug-of-War decision-making model (QTOW) that uses qutrits to represent a single internal state, and demonstrates violations of non-contextuality bounds via KCBS-type probe families. This work provides a novel theoretical framework linking quantum contextuality to cognitive decision-making, potentially offering a compact single-state representation for contextual operations. It could influence both quantum foundations and AI/ML architectures by highlighting a trade-off between intrinsic state transformations and external memory resources. The QTOW model uses a qutrit state, a state-disturbing generalized decision instrument, decision- and reward-conditioned norm-preserving feedback, and optional probing operations. The non-contextuality violation serves as a witness that the operation family cannot be embedded in a single non-contextual classical probability space, though classical reconstructions can avoid this by adding context labels or restricting probes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Qutrits are three-level quantum systems that can hold more information than qubits. The KCBS inequality is a non-contextuality inequality used to test quantum contextuality, which is the dependence of measurement outcomes on the context of other simultaneous measurements. The Tug-of-War model is a cognitive decision-making framework that describes how conflicting options pull an internal state, and this paper extends it to a quantum-like setting.

## [Anytime Pretraining with Weight Averaging Achieves Minimax Rates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03702) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces and analyzes anytime learning-rate schedules for LLM pretraining, showing that weight averaging (model merging) enables minimax convergence rates without knowing the training horizon in advance. Empirically, 150M and 300M parameter models trained with constant or 1/sqrt(t) schedules plus weight averaging match cosine decay performance across up to 32x Chinchilla scale. This work addresses a critical gap in continual and open-ended LLM training, where the total compute budget is often unknown. By providing a practical, horizon-free alternative to cosine schedules, it could simplify training pipelines and enable more flexible adaptive compute allocation, impacting both research and production systems. The theoretical analysis focuses on overparameterized linear regression, proving that anytime schedules with weight averaging achieve minimax rates, with polynomial decay rates determined by source and capacity conditions. The empirical evaluation uses 150M and 300M parameter models trained up to 32x Chinchilla scale, comparing constant and 1/sqrt(t) schedules with weight averaging against a well-tuned cosine schedule.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are often trained with cosine learning rate schedules that require knowing the total training steps in advance, which is not feasible in continual or open-ended settings. Anytime learning schedules aim to provide good performance at any point during training, without prior knowledge of the horizon. Weight averaging, also known as model merging, combines parameters from different training checkpoints to improve performance, and this paper shows it is key to achieving minimax convergence rates in this context.

<details><summary>References</summary>

## [Reinforcement Learning Enables End-to-End Tokenization Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13940) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel method for learning token boundaries in LLMs end-to-end using score function estimates from reinforcement learning, rather than straight-through estimates. The approach incorporates time discounting to reduce variance and demonstrates superior performance at the 100 million parameter scale. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in LLM training by making tokenization end-to-end learnable, potentially improving model efficiency and performance. It offers a theoretically grounded alternative to heuristic or straight-through methods, which could influence future LLM architectures and training pipelines. The method uses score function estimators, which directly optimize the discrete token boundary selection to minimize loss, providing tighter theoretical guarantees than straight-through estimates. Time discounting is shown to be necessary to reduce variance sufficiently for practical use, and the method outperforms prior straight-through estimates both qualitatively and quantitatively at the 100M parameter scale.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Tokenization is a hardcoded compression step in LLM training, converting text into discrete tokens. Prior work has attempted to learn token boundaries using straight-through estimates, which treat discrete boundaries as continuous, but this paper proposes using score function estimates from reinforcement learning, which directly optimize the discrete problem. Score function estimators, also known as REINFORCE, are a standard technique in RL for estimating gradients of expected rewards, though they often suffer from high variance, which time discounting helps mitigate.

## [VLANeXt: A Unified Recipe for Building Strong VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18532) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces VLANeXt, a new Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model that outperforms state-of-the-art methods on the LIBERO and LIBERO-plus benchmarks. It systematically dissects the VLA design space along three dimensions, distilling 12 key findings into a practical recipe for building strong VLA models. This work addresses the fragmentation in the VLA field by providing a unified framework and evaluation setup, making it easier for researchers to identify which design choices truly matter. The practical recipe and open-source codebase can accelerate progress in embodied AI and robotics, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The study starts from a simple VLA baseline similar to RT-2 and dissects design choices along three dimensions: foundational components, perception essentials, and action modelling perspectives. The resulting VLANeXt model demonstrates strong performance in real-world experiments, and the authors release a unified, easy-to-use codebase at https://github.com/DravenALG/VLANeXt.

## [ST-Lite: Training-Free KV Cache Compression for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00188) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ST-Lite introduces a training-free KV cache compression method specifically designed for GUI agents, using spatio-trajectory guidance to preserve critical KV pairs under memory constraints. It outperforms existing baselines across seven GUI benchmarks, achieving up to 2.35x decoding speedup at fivefold compression. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in deploying vision-language GUI agents under memory and latency constraints, which is crucial for real-world automation. By being training-free and effective, ST-Lite could enable more efficient and scalable GUI agents, impacting fields like UI automation and digital assistants. ST-Lite identifies three GUI-specific workload properties: high inter-frame visual redundancy, small UI-element spatial footprints, and near-uniform cross-layer attention sparsity. Its three components—Trajectory-aware Semantic Gating (TSG), Component-centric Spatial Saliency (CSS), and flat per-layer budget—each target one property, and the implementation is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache compression is essential for efficient inference of large language models and vision-language models, as the cache grows with sequence length and can become a memory bottleneck. Existing methods are designed for generic language workloads and fail to account for the unique structure of GUI interaction traces, leading to suboptimal performance. GUI agents use vision-language models to understand and interact with graphical user interfaces, often requiring long-horizon reasoning over multiple screenshots.

## [ADVERSA Framework Measures Multi-Turn LLM Guardrail Degradation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10068) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ADVERSA is a new automated red-teaming framework that measures how LLM guardrails degrade over multi-turn attacks, using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model and a structured compliance rubric. In a controlled experiment across three frontier models, it observed a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25. This framework addresses a significant gap in LLM safety evaluation by moving beyond single-prompt binary outcomes to continuous multi-turn trajectories, providing a more realistic assessment of guardrail robustness. It also highlights the importance of judge reliability and attacker-side confounds, which are often overlooked in red-teaming studies. The framework uses a fine-tuned Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct attacker model with QLoRA, and a triple-judge consensus architecture to measure judge reliability. It reports inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift, and attacker refusals as confounds; attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional LLM safety evaluations often use single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which do not capture how safety evolves under sustained adversarial interaction. Red-teaming involves simulating attacks to test model robustness, and multi-turn attacks are more realistic but less studied. QLoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that reduces memory usage, enabling fine-tuning of large models on limited hardware.

## [Omanic: A New Benchmark for Step-wise Multi-hop Reasoning Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Omanic introduces a 4-hop open-domain QA benchmark with step-level annotations, comprising 10,296 machine-generated training examples and 967 expert-reviewed human-annotated evaluation examples. It enables diagnosis of reasoning failures by decomposing questions into single-hop sub-questions with intermediate answers and graph topologies. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by moving beyond final-answer accuracy to pinpoint where reasoning breaks down, revealing later-hop bottlenecks and error propagation. It provides a valuable resource for improving multi-hop reasoning in LLMs and demonstrates transferable supervision gains across multiple reasoning benchmarks. Experiments with proprietary and open-source LLMs show Omanic is challenging, and step-wise analysis reveals a later-hop bottleneck, factual knowledge floor, and error propagation along reasoning chains. Fine-tuning on OmanicSynth transfers to six reasoning and mathematics benchmarks, yielding a 7.41-point average gain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop QA benchmarks assess a system's ability to perform sequential reasoning across multiple evidence sources. Traditional benchmarks often only measure final answers, obscuring intermediate failures. Step-level annotations provide a finer-grained evaluation, helping to identify specific reasoning errors and error propagation patterns.

## [PAuth: Task-Scoped Authorization for AI Agents via Natural Language Slices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces PAuth, a novel authorization mechanism that uses natural language task slices to grant AI agents precise, task-scoped permissions, addressing the overprivilege issue in current OAuth-based systems. It evaluates PAuth on AuthBench, a benchmark built on AgentDojo and cross-validated on OpenClaw, showing that all 100 benign tasks are authorized and all 634 adversarial calls are blocked. This work addresses a critical security gap in AI agent authorization, as current OAuth scopes only grant permissions over operators (e.g., TRANSFER) but not operations (e.g., transfer $100 to Bob), leading to overprivileged agents. PAuth could significantly enhance both security and usability for AI agents interacting with web services, impacting the AI/ML and security communities. PAuth uses natural language slices, a symbolic specification of expected calls inspired by program slicing, and signed envelopes that bind concrete values to symbolic provenance. It frees users from approving each call with concrete operand values, including intermediate results, enhancing usability while maintaining security.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: OAuth 2.0 is a widely used authorization framework that grants permissions via scopes, which define granular permissions like 'pets:read' or 'orders:write'. However, scopes are operator-level and cannot express operand-level constraints, leading to overprivilege. Program slicing is a software engineering technique that computes the set of statements affecting a point of interest, which inspires the concept of NL slices. Prompt injection is a security risk where malicious input manipulates LLM behavior, and PAuth aims to mitigate its impact even when agents are compromised.

This paper introduces NeuroAdapt-Bench, a systematic benchmark for evaluating test-time adaptation (TTA) methods on EEG foundation models under realistic distribution shifts. It evaluates representative TTA approaches across multiple pretrained models, tasks, and datasets, including extreme modality shifts like Ear-EEG. This work addresses a critical gap in EEG foundation model deployment by systematically benchmarking TTA under real-world shifts, which is essential for clinical AI and privacy-constrained settings. The findings highlight the limitations of directly applying existing TTA techniques to EEG, underscoring the need for domain-specific adaptation strategies. The evaluated TTA methods yield inconsistent gains and often degrade performance, with gradient-based approaches particularly prone to heavy degradation, while optimization-free methods show greater stability. The benchmark covers in-distribution, out-of-distribution, and extreme modality shifts (e.g., Ear-EEG) across heterogeneous datasets.

## [SIRA: Compressing Multi-Round Agentic Retrieval into a Single Action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06647) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Meta introduces Superintelligent Retrieval Agent (SIRA), a retrieval architecture that compresses multi-round exploratory search into a single corpus-discriminative retrieval action. SIRA achieves state-of-the-art average retrieval performance across ten BEIR benchmarks without using relevance labels or retriever fine-tuning. SIRA challenges the conventional agentic retrieval paradigm by eliminating iterative query reformulation, potentially reducing latency and improving recall in RAG systems. Its strong performance on BEIR and downstream QA suggests a more efficient and effective approach for knowledge-intensive applications. SIRA uses LLM-enriched vocabulary and corpus statistics to filter terms that are absent, overly common, or unlikely to create retrieval margin, then performs a single weighted BM25 call. The paper also introduces BrowseComp-Wikipedia, a hard-search benchmark of 232 queries over a 25.6M-document Wikipedia index, where SIRA outperforms multi-round Perplexity agents at every budget.

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented agents typically treat retrieval as a black box, issuing exploratory queries and reformulating until evidence emerges, which can be inefficient. SIRA instead compresses this process into a single action by predicting evidence vocabulary and using corpus statistics to validate terms, similar to how an expert navigates a database with strong priors.

## [Tournament-GRPO: Group-Wise Tournament Rewards for Long-Form RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26958) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Tournament-GRPO introduces a group-wise tournament-based reward framework for reinforcement learning in open-ended long-form generation, converting rubric-guided LLM judgments into relative rewards through repeated multi-round tournaments among same-query rollouts. On Deep Research Bench, it achieves a 4.52-point overall-score improvement over the strongest baseline. This work addresses a critical challenge in RLHF for long-form generation, where reliable reference answers and automatic metrics are often unavailable. By providing a more effective reward signal, it could improve the alignment and quality of LLM outputs in complex, open-ended tasks, influencing future reward design in RL. Tournament-GRPO compares candidates within groups, accumulates tournament outcomes, and normalizes them into group-wise rewards for GRPO training. Analyses show that tournament rewards provide a favorable effectiveness–efficiency trade-off and that tournament design affects training dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that updates a model's weights using comparative scoring of a group of outputs at once, commonly used for training reasoning models. In open-ended long-form generation, rubric-based methods typically rely on pointwise LLM-as-a-judge scoring, which can be difficult to calibrate and may saturate during optimization. Tournament-GRPO instead uses relative comparisons through tournaments to provide more discriminative and stable rewards.

## [Mamba-2 State Sink Causal Localization Is Non-Unique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00930) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that in Mamba-2, the causal effect of the state sink is not uniquely localized to top-activating heads, breaking the link between probe labels and causal circuits. This finding challenges a common assumption in mechanistic interpretability that top-activating units correspond to the actual circuit, which could impact how interpretability methods are validated and interpreted in state space models. The study shows that at Mamba-2 head granularity, a near-disjoint set of top-activation heads matches or exceeds the ablation effect of the specialists, and perturbing the Delta gate moves loss by at most 0.16 nats while suppressing head outputs moves it by 1.5 to 7.3 nats.

Mechanistic interpretability often uses probes to identify top-activating units as the circuit executing a computation.

SALIMORY, introduced in a recent arXiv paper, trains a single language model to manage cognitive memory for conversational agents, using a hierarchical stage-wise process reward and reward-decomposed contrastive refinement. This approach reduces memory-attributed failures by one-third and improves end-to-end accuracy by over 10% compared to state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses a critical challenge in building lifelong conversational agents that must maintain persistent memory across interactions. By providing a more effective training approach, it could significantly enhance personalization and user experience in AI companions, chatbots, and virtual assistants, potentially influencing future research in memory management for large language models. The SALIMORY architecture separates memory management into offline and runtime stages, with the offline stage building and maintaining structured memory. The framework introduces a hierarchical stage-wise process reward and reward-decomposed contrastive refinement to provide isolated supervision for distinct memory operations, including selective filtering, consolidation, and cue-driven recall.

Simply expanding context windows with raw retrieval degrades reasoning quality, and training memory agents with standard reinforcement learning creates a credit assignment bottleneck in multi-stage pipelines. SALIMORY addresses these issues by training a single model to manage a cognitively-structured memory, drawing on cognitive science principles.

## [Single Neuron Edit Reduces Repetition Loops in Gemma 4 LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13705) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that repetition loops in Gemma 4 instruction-tuned models can be substantially reduced by editing a single neuron, with one sign-inverted neuron in Gemma 4 E2B proving sufficient. Across all four Gemma models, detected loops dropped from 46/384 to 12/384 on held-out prompts and seeds. This work offers a targeted, training-free alternative to repetition penalties, which can distort valid repetition and degrade task performance. It highlights the potential of small-scale weight editing for model interpretability and debugging, and provides cross-family evidence with Qwen3.5 and LFM2.5, suggesting broader applicability. The methodology combines per-layer ablation with per-neuron or routed-expert attribution, then evaluates weight edits over complete generations. While edits are effective for E2B and E4B at longer generation budgets, remaining failures in 26B and 31B shift toward 'doom looping'—non-convergent self-correction over facts the model cannot recall.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Repetition loops are a known failure mode in large language models where the model gets stuck repeating the same tokens or phrases, often on long factual enumeration tasks. Neuron editing is a model intervention technique that modifies specific neurons to alter behavior without retraining, based on the locality hypothesis. The Gemma 4 models are a family of open-weights multimodal LLMs from Google, available in pre-trained and instruction-tuned variants.

## [RARM: Confidence-Gated Progress Reward Model for Robot Manipulation RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22027) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RARM is a lightweight visual comparator that converts a single successful demonstration into a dense, progress-aware reward for reinforcement learning in robot manipulation. It is trained once on general-purpose videos with a contrastive temporal objective, requiring no robot-specific data or task-specific reward labels. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in RL for long-horizon manipulation tasks by eliminating the need for task-specific reward engineering. It could significantly reduce engineering effort and improve success rates, especially in tasks like cloth folding where progress estimation is challenging. RARM matches rollout clips to reference clips and rewards only confident forward progress, suppressing uncertain matches that may produce false-positive rewards. It achieves the best overall success rates across 9 simulated tasks (LIBERO and MetaWorld) and 4 real-world tasks, with large gains on long-horizon tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for robot manipulation often struggles with reward design: sparse success rewards provide weak supervision, while hand-crafted dense rewards are tedious and generalize poorly. Progress-based reward models estimate how far an observation has advanced toward task completion, but existing methods often require task-specific demonstrations or labels. RARM uses a contrastive temporal objective to learn from general-purpose videos, making it broadly applicable without per-task engineering.

## [Unified Algebraic Framework for Classification Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04028) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a unified algebraic framework for classification performance evaluation, extending binary measures to multiclass, multilabel, ordinal, hierarchical, cost-sensitive, and soft-label settings using three aggregation operators (global, column-wise, row-wise) that correspond to micro, macro/weighted, and exemplar averaging. It provides theoretical characterizations, including the uniqueness of the product t-norm for soft ground truth and conditions for measure properties. This framework simplifies the extension of binary evaluation measures to complex classification scenarios, eliminating the need for measure-specific derivations. It has the potential to standardize evaluation practices across machine learning research and applications, impacting model selection and benchmarking. Key findings include that micro-averaging equals denominator-weighted macro-averaging only for aggregation-decomposable (linear-fractional) measures, and that for soft ground truth, the product t-norm is uniquely characterized by preserving marginal memberships. Additionally, under one-hot multiclass encoding, measures can have non-trivial performance floors even for completely incorrect classifiers, such as a label accuracy of 0.8 with zero correct predictions on 10 classes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Classification performance evaluation typically uses measures like precision, recall, and F1, which are defined for binary cases and extended to multiclass via micro, macro, or weighted averaging. Aggregation operators in fuzzy logic, such as t-norms, generalize conjunction and are used in soft-label settings. The framework builds on these concepts to provide a unified algebraic treatment.

## [Eluna: Graph-Guided Multi-Agent LLM System for Warehouse SOP Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08960) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Eluna is a production-deployed agentic system that encodes warehouse Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) as directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) with progressive disclosure, and uses asymmetric episodic distillation to train a smaller student model that matches or exceeds its teacher. On a 13-task benchmark and two production applications, it achieved 94% expert agreement on ticket processing. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agents for industrial settings: enforcing procedural compliance under strict time constraints while avoiding context overload. By demonstrating a production-grade system that improves efficiency and reliability, it paves the way for broader adoption of agentic AI in logistics and operations management. The system uses a graph-guided multi-agent framework where independent tasks are delegated to parallel sub-agents with persistent code execution and live data access. Asymmetric episodic distillation involves improving a strong teacher with episodic error memories, then fine-tuning a smaller student on corrected trajectories with memory stripped, internalizing corrections without inference-time overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Warehouse operations rely on Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) that encode complex decision logic across multiple systems. LLM agents often struggle with procedural compliance and context overload when given full SOP specifications. Progressive disclosure is a design pattern that reveals information gradually, reducing cognitive load, and is applied here to manage the complexity of SOPs. Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller model learns from a larger teacher model, and asymmetric distillation introduces imbalances to improve robustness.

EviPathBench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) on whole-slide pathology, focusing on evidence acquisition and multi-scale reasoning from gigapixel images. It includes 1,822 TCGA WSIs and 17,135 diagnostic paths annotated by ten pathologists, plus a private cohort of 190 breast cancer WSIs. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in pathology AI evaluation by testing models' ability to acquire evidence from whole-slide images, not just pre-cropped patches. The results reveal a significant gap between reasoning over curated evidence and acquiring it from WSIs, which could guide future research in medical imaging and VLM development. The benchmark evaluates four capabilities: image-to-text matching, text-to-image retrieval, diagnostic-region localization, and multi-scale reasoning. Leading open-weight models achieve over 93% accuracy in multi-scale reasoning and over 50% in cross-modal matching, but diagnostic-region localization remains challenging, with best mean intersection-over-union below 0.09.

GraphVid introduces a graph-conditioned image-to-video generation model that enables interactive control through structured interaction graphs, along with a new large-scale benchmark dataset called GraphVid-Bench. The model achieves competitive results with less training data and fewer parameters than prior motion-control methods. This work addresses a key limitation in controllable video generation: specifying precise multi-object interactions. By using interaction graphs instead of text or trajectory-based control, it offers a more intuitive and scalable interface, potentially advancing applications in content creation, simulation, and autonomous systems. GraphVid reduces FID by up to 39.9% and FVD by 37.6% compared to Motion-I2V, while improving PSNR from 9.87 to 15.98 and SSIM from 0.38 to 0.61. The model uses directed interaction scene graphs to represent motion control, and GraphVid-Bench provides structured relational annotations for training interaction-aware models.

## [LOCKS: Page-Local Spectral Summaries for Efficient Long-Context Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24555) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LOCKS introduces a method that assigns each page of the KV cache a compact spectral summary, enabling the selection of top pages without reading all keys. This approach reduces per-step KV reads by 10-25x and halves decode latency at 1M tokens on an H200 NVL, while maintaining quality close to the full cache. This is significant because long-context decoding is a major bottleneck in LLM serving, and LOCKS offers a practical solution that can be integrated as a drop-in plugin for vLLM. It enables efficient long-context inference without significant quality loss, which could benefit applications like document QA and long-form reasoning. LOCKS uses rank-r spectral summaries (r=8 gives a tenth of the cache size, r=2 gives a twenty-fifth) and estimates page attention mass via log-sum-exp. It selects top pages without reading candidate keys or values, and at a 2048-token budget matches FullKV aggregate quality at 100K+ context while attending to about 2% of tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: In transformer-based LLMs, the KV cache stores key and value vectors for all tokens, and during decoding, it is read in full at each step, which becomes a bottleneck for long contexts. Attention keys are often locally low-rank but globally high-rank, meaning that a shared low-rank basis may miss page-specific directions. LOCKS addresses this by giving each page its own spectral summary, which captures page-specific information more effectively.

## [MOSAIC: Fast Secure Outsourcing for AI Computations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29221) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MOSAIC introduces a novel matrix-multiplication masking protocol that enables secure outsourcing of AI computations from a trusted client to an untrusted server, achieving orders-of-magnitude speedups over prior work. It supports large transformer inference, such as 70B models, with perplexity comparable to quantization and matching full-precision BF16 on HumanEval. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in privacy-preserving machine learning by enabling efficient and secure outsourcing of large-scale AI workloads. It could pave the way for confidential AI in data centers, keeping the trusted computing base small while leveraging untrusted accelerators. The protocol introduces small noise to multiplication results, relaxing correctness to achieve optimal asymptotic client overhead, with security based on the decisional LWE and LPN assumptions. A key technical challenge is bounding error growth across transformer layers, addressed via an error-scaling mechanism using random Hadamard rotations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Secure outsourcing of computations often relies on homomorphic encryption or secure multi-party computation, which are computationally expensive. The Learning with Errors (LWE) problem and its decisional variant (DLWE) are foundational to many post-quantum cryptographic schemes, while Learning Parity with Noise (LPN) is another hardness assumption. Matrix multiplication is a core operation in AI workloads, and masking protocols aim to hide inputs and models from the server.

## [AeroDPO: Lightweight UAV Navigation Matches 7B Models via Automated Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07557) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AeroDPO demonstrates that a lightweight 2B model with high-fidelity perception can match the success rates of massive 7B baselines in UAV-VLN, and introduces a zero-cost automated Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) pipeline to overcome behavior cloning robustness issues, achieving a new state-of-the-art success rate of 49.16% on unmapped scenarios. This finding challenges the assumption that larger language models are necessary for UAV navigation, showing that perception quality is more critical than reasoning capacity. It enables real-time edge deployment of autonomous aerial agents, reducing latency and computational costs, and the automated preference optimization approach addresses a key limitation of behavior cloning without human annotations, benefiting the robotics and AI communities. The AeroDPO pipeline uses deterministic physical simulation state rollback to detect collisions, rewinds the environment to extract causal reasoning errors as rejected actions, applies decoupled privileged interventions to synthesize collision-avoidance preferred maneuvers, and uses an offline vision language inspector to filter visual ambiguities. This automated data flywheel boosts success rates to 49.16% on unmapped scenarios while drastically suppressing collision rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Navigation for Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAV-VLN) requires rapid and reactive control in complex 3D environments. Recent minimalist end-to-end paradigms rely on massive language models with billions of parameters, incurring prohibitive latency for real-world edge deployment. Behavior cloning, a common imitation learning method, lacks explicit negative feedback, leading to robustness flaws such as high collision rates in out-of-distribution scenarios. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a method that learns directly from preferences without training a separate reward model, and AeroDPO automates this process using simulation rollback.

## [MAPLE: Heterogeneous Expert Allocation Boosts MoE Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15299) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MAPLE proposes a plug-and-play framework that reallocates routed-expert budgets heterogeneously across layers of pretrained MoE LLMs without retraining, using sensitivity-guided closed-form allocation and genetic search. On DeepSeek-MoE-16B, it uses only 75% of experts yet surpasses the original 100% uniform baseline on ARC-E, ARC-C, and BoolQ, and reduces serving latency by 32.2% with 47.4% throughput improvement. This work challenges the conventional uniform expert allocation in MoE Transformers, showing that heterogeneous allocation can be more effective than simply activating more experts. It offers a practical, training-free method to improve efficiency and performance of existing MoE LLMs, which is significant for deployment in resource-constrained environments. MAPLE quantifies layer-wise sensitivity using three measures and derives an analytically optimal budget assignment, refined by a sensitivity-constrained genetic search. It outperforms uniform and pruning-based baselines under a 75% routed-expert budget across four MoE models, and implementation in SGLang demonstrates real deployment gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Transformers activate only a subset of experts per token, but typically fix the same number of routed experts across all layers, ignoring layer-wise redundancy. MAPLE addresses this by reallocating expert budgets based on sensitivity, which measures how much each layer's output changes with expert count. This approach is plug-and-play, requiring no weight modification or retraining, making it easy to apply to existing models.

## [First Mechanised Formalisation of Romanov's Triplet Logic in Rocq](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18445) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper presents the first mechanised formalisation of Romanov's Triplet Logic (TLS) in the Rocq proof assistant, including a verified filter (SVI) for the sliding-window fragment with explicit polynomial-time bounds and a correctness boundary. It also introduces VFR, an extracted OCaml prototype with a Python runtime and Docker packaging. This work bridges combinatorial SAT reasoning and formal methods, providing a verified decision procedure for a fragment of TLS and a sound filter for general 3-CNF. It demonstrates the feasibility of mechanically verifying complex combinatorial algorithms, which could influence future automated reasoning and proof assistant applications. The Rocq development comprises over 23,000 lines of code with 424 proved lemmas and no unproved assumptions. The paper proves that for general formulas, SVI non-emptiness is necessary but not sufficient for satisfiability, while for aligned structures, a full bi-implication holds, extended to systems of structures. It also formalises the grouped-window translation and provides a formal counterexample to its completeness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Romanov's Triplet Logic (TLS) is a combinatorial framework for reasoning about compatible paths through layered triplet structures, called Compact Triplets Structures (CTS), originally motivated by Boolean satisfiability. The Simple Vertex Intersection (SVI) filter is a key algorithm in TLS that checks for non-empty intersections of vertex sets. The Rocq proof assistant (formerly Coq) is used to mechanise the formalisation, ensuring correctness of the proofs.

## [Audit Finds LLM Agent Credit Signals Fail Against Causal Ground Truth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19760) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper audits step-level credit assignment signals in LLM agents against causal ground truth from executed replay in ALFWorld, finding that none of the tested signals (LLM-judge scores, outcome-conditioned logprob ratios, policy confidence) reliably outperform shuffled controls. The study introduces a distinction between step correctness and step contribution, showing that existing evaluations may be measuring the wrong thing. This challenges the reliability of common credit assignment signals used in training and evaluating LLM agents, which could impact the development of agentic reinforcement learning. The findings suggest that current methods may not identify causally important steps, potentially leading to inefficient training and misleading evaluations. The ground truth is structured: 30.5% of decision points exhibit a nonzero replay contrast, and measurability is model-dependent (13.1% vs. 26.8% of points lack policy-supported counterfactuals). Implicit credit correlates with policy fluency (median rank correlation +0.75), while outcome conditioning adds no causal information (partial correlation -0.004). A confidence-only router cuts judge cost by 13.1% per turn but recovers pivotal steps at chance level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly trained with step-level credit signals, such as LLM-judge scores or outcome-conditioned logprob ratios, to improve long-horizon tool-use tasks. ALFWorld is a text-based environment that parallels embodied worlds, allowing agents to reason and act in an abstract space. Executed replay is a causal method that re-samples the policy's alternatives at each decision point and rolls forward to measure the effect on the outcome, providing ground truth for step contribution.

## [Periodic Row-wise Muon Scales Diffusion Transformers Efficiently](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20818) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Periodic Row-wise Muon, a scalable optimization method for Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) that performs a full Newton-Schulz spectral update only once every K steps and applies a low-cost row-wise constrained update at other steps. It also co-designs a distributed implementation that reduces optimizer time by 46.9-54.3% and end-to-end step time by 15.7-24.3% while preserving Muon's generative quality advantage over AdamW. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in training large Diffusion Transformers, which are foundational to state-of-the-art image and video generation models. By making Muon's efficiency practical at scale, it could significantly reduce training costs and time for large generative models, benefiting both researchers and industry practitioners. The method reduces logical communication volume by 66.7% and reaches best generative quality with 33.7-64.8% less active training time. Across model scales from 1.3B to 15B parameters, Muon improves generative quality over AdamW by 12.9-19.1%, and Periodic Row-wise Muon stays within 0.5% of vanilla Muon on 1.3B-4B models while improving quality by 4.5% at 9B.

**Background**: Muon is a matrix-aware optimizer that balances updates across singular directions, improving training of large models. However, its standard implementation requires a 5-step Newton-Schulz iteration at every optimization step, which becomes computationally expensive at scale. Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are a class of generative models that combine transformer architectures with diffusion processes, and they are widely used in modern image and video generation systems.

<a id="item-170"></a>
## [CIVA: Efficient Critic-Induced Value-Subspace Attacks on World-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CIVA, a novel white-box, causal, online attack method that exploits a low-dimensional subspace derived from the victim's critic to efficiently and smoothly perturb visual world-model agents like DreamerV3. It achieves a 26.07% reward drop on DMC walker walk with low temporal variation (TempAbs 0.646), outperforming five recent methods. This work advances adversarial machine learning by addressing the unique challenges of attacking world-model agents, which are increasingly used in reinforcement learning. It highlights a practical vulnerability in AI systems, underscoring the need for robust defenses in safety-critical applications. CIVA first probes the frozen victim offline with critic-guided PGD and extracts a low-rank value-subspace via SVD. At test time, it optimizes only subspace coefficients, smoothed with an exponential moving average (EMA), and maps them back to pixels, keeping online optimization cheap and temporally coherent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Visual world-model agents like DreamerV3 act through a recurrent latent state rather than a single observation, making them robust to frame-wise attacks. Traditional attacks like PGD are iterative and can produce temporally inconsistent perturbations. CIVA leverages the critic's value function to find a low-dimensional subspace where perturbations are effective and smooth.

## [Visual Scaffolds Boost VLM Spatial Reasoning by 34 Points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SPaRC, a benchmark for grid-based visual spatial planning, and shows that adding lightweight input-side visual scaffolds improves VLM accuracy by up to 34.0 percentage points and complements GRPO-based training, yielding up to 4.6 additional accuracy points. This research highlights that visual presentation is a critical factor in multimodal reasoning, suggesting that prompt engineering and input design can significantly enhance VLM performance. It also provides a new benchmark (SPaRC) for evaluating spatial reasoning in VLMs, which has practical implications for AI development and deployment. The scaffolds preserve the visual modality while making spatial structure more accessible, and the gains are closely tied to reductions in grounding-related errors, while rule reasoning remains comparatively challenging. The analysis covers both end-to-end task solving and object detection, and the benchmark includes procedurally generated 2D grid pathfinding puzzles.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but their performance can be affected by how visual information is presented. ## [Training Knowledge Bases as Non-Parametric Models for RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21829) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes treating the knowledge base as a non-parametric model trained on question-answer pairs, where a curator agent edits the store based on gold answers. It introduces two contamination-free benchmarks, KBGym and PhantomWiki, and shows improved generalization on unseen questions. This approach addresses a significant gap in current RAG pipelines, where document stores are typically built unsupervised. By treating the store as trainable, it could lead to more efficient and accurate retrieval, potentially influencing future RAG system designs. The curated store's advantage grows with overlap: from parity when no key is shared to +0.176 F1 when both keys are shared, and 25% fewer actions at +0.294 F1 on trained questions. It matches HippoRAG's gains with 1,913 links versus 196,112, achieving 1.5x action saving and 2.1x accuracy gain per point of corpus covered.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) typically treats the document store as a frozen input, with offline pipelines building structure unsupervised. This paper instead treats the knowledge base as a non-parametric model, meaning its complexity grows with the data, and trains it using supervised question-answer pairs. The curator agent iteratively edits the store based on gold answers, allowing the store to adapt to the questions it will face.

<a id="item-173"></a>
## [Functional Compatibility Drives Persistent Neural Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22462) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies functional compatibility as a causal determinant of whether new learning persists in neural networks. By manipulating compatibility from identical neural states, the authors show that higher compatibility leads to more persistent learning across architectures and domains. This reframes the stability-plasticity dilemma from preventing forgetting to determining which new learning can coexist with existing function and persist. It provides a novel, experimentally manipulable factor that could guide the design of continual learning algorithms and improve their long-term performance. The study validates the finding across independent directions, convolutional and transformer architectures, vision and text domains, and a ten-seed replication. It also notes that learning rules and retention constraints determine how much compatible opportunity is retained, while nonlinear geometry limits the matched intervention at larger update norms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Neural networks often suffer from catastrophic forgetting when learning new tasks, a challenge known as the stability-plasticity dilemma. Continual learning aims to address this by enabling models to acquire new knowledge while preserving old capabilities. This paper introduces functional compatibility as a key factor in this process, offering a new perspective on how to achieve persistent learning.

## [Auditing Prefix Invariance in Hybrid Sequence Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formalizes prefix invariance for hybrid sequence models and introduces a lightweight, training-free audit that localizes causality leaks in two forward passes. The audit detected all 192/192 injected faults across eight checkpoints, while standard attention-mask inspection found none. This work addresses a critical but often overlooked issue in hybrid sequence models, providing a practical audit that outperforms the default mask inspection. It identifies real defects in existing models like Zamba2 and Nemotron-H, highlighting the need for causality verification beyond attention masks. The audit requires only two forward passes, no training or gradients, and yields a per-layer score localizing where causality breaks. Static/dynamic analysis of chunked-scan code in transformers found an inter-chunk axis error in Zamba2 and Nemotron-H, fixed via the reference implementation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Hybrid sequence models combine attention with state-space or other mechanisms to handle long sequences efficiently. Prefix invariance requires that representations at position t depend only on past inputs, a property essential for autoregressive generation. Attention-mask inspection is the default check but is incomplete because causality is a graph-level property that can be violated by scans, aggregations, or normalization even with correct masks.

## [VideoRover Unifies Video Reasoning and Deep Research for Open-World Video Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23329) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VideoRover is a new framework that unifies video reasoning and deep research, enabling open-world video agents to iteratively coordinate video cropping, multimodal search, and webpage browsing. It also introduces VideoRover-Bench, a benchmark stratified by video duration and research difficulty, along with an automated data curation pipeline that produces 26K SFT trajectories and 3K RL instances. This work addresses a significant gap by integrating two capabilities—active temporal perception and multi-step information seeking—that were previously developed in isolation. It could influence future research in video understanding and multimodal agents, providing a new paradigm for open-world video question answering. The VideoRover-8B-RL model achieves performance comparable to proprietary models in the direct-answer setting without tool use, while outperforming larger open-source models equipped with the same tool suite. Ablation studies and training dynamics validate the complementary roles of active video grounding, external retrieval, and long-horizon reinforcement learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Open-world video understanding often requires locating sparse visual evidence and acquiring external knowledge not present in the video or the model's parametric memory. Traditional approaches either focus on active temporal perception (e.g., Thinking-with-Videos) or multi-step information seeking (e.g., Deep Research), but rarely combine both. VideoRover bridges this gap by using tool results to guide subsequent actions, enabling a more holistic approach to video question answering.

## [Cross-Domain Data-to-Text Generation via Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23391) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a data-driven knowledge distillation (DDKD) approach with structure-preserving augmentation for cross-domain, multi-task data-to-text generation, achieving superior performance over fine-tuning and zero-shot inference without in-domain training data. The method is validated on five benchmarks, and the authors also construct QUINTD-5, a fivefold extension of QUINTD-1. This work addresses a significant challenge in data-to-text generation: generalizing across domains and tasks without in-domain training data. By enabling small models to outperform larger fine-tuned models on some domains, it offers a cost-effective alternative to large language models, potentially impacting real-world applications where labeled data is scarce. The method uses data-driven knowledge distillation (DDKD) and structure-preserving augmentation via structural subsampling and perturbation. Experiments at a constant model size of 1.7B parameters show DDKD consistently outperforms fine-tuning and zero-shot inference; the resulting small models outperform a much larger fine-tuned model on two of five domains and achieve comparable performance on the remaining three.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Data-to-text (D2T) generation converts structured data like tables, knowledge graphs, and time series into natural language text. Traditional approaches rely on task-specific training data or large language models' zero-shot capabilities, which may not generalize well across diverse domains. Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller 'student' model learns from a larger 'teacher' model, often used for model compression. Structure-preserving augmentation aims to create new training samples while maintaining the underlying data structure, which is crucial for structured data.

## [TimeCatch Benchmark Reveals VLMs' Temporal Blind Spot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23474) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TimeCatch, a new benchmark that evaluates vision-language models (VLMs) on temporal anomaly detection by swapping consecutive frames. The study found that VLMs perform near chance on temporal anomaly detection, despite strong performance on frame-level anomalies, and humans achieve near-ceiling performance on both tasks. This finding highlights a critical limitation in current VLMs' ability to reason about temporal consistency, which is essential for video understanding and multimodal AI applications. The TimeCatch benchmark provides a controlled tool for evaluating and improving temporal grounding in VLMs, potentially guiding future research and model development. The benchmark includes four synthetic and real-world datasets, and models are evaluated on both anomaly detection and localization tasks. Additional analyses across model scales, prompting strategies, sequence lengths, and visual similarity suggest that the failures cannot be explained solely by perception limitations or model capacity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both visual and textual data, often used for tasks like image captioning and video understanding. Temporal grounding refers to a model's ability to understand the order and consistency of events over time, which is crucial for interpreting videos or image sequences. Anomaly detection involves identifying unusual patterns, and in this context, temporal anomalies are created by swapping consecutive frames, while frame-level anomalies involve replacing a frame with noise.

<a id="item-179"></a>
## [BPCO: Stable Critic-Based RL Recipe for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23566) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Best Practice Critic Optimization (BPCO), a training recipe that combines DPPO, bounded value predictions, Monte Carlo value targets, unnormalized policy advantages, and length-adaptive GAE to stabilize critic-based RL for LLMs. Across mathematical reasoning tasks with models from 1.5B to 30B-A3B MoE, BPCO consistently improves a strong critic-based baseline and matches or exceeds group-based methods like GRPO while sampling only one response per prompt. This work addresses a critical instability issue in critic-based RL for LLMs, offering a reliable alternative to group-relative advantage estimation. It could reduce the computational cost of RL fine-tuning by eliminating the need for multiple response sampling, and improve learning with rubric-based rewards, benefiting the broader RLHF/alignment community. BPCO conditions the critic on reward-defining information (e.g., reference answer or grading rubric) that is hidden from the policy, since the critic is only used during training. Controlled experiments isolate the effect of each design choice, and the recipe also improves learning with rubric-based rewards. Code is available at https://github.com/QPHutu/golden_critic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to fine-tune large language models (LLMs) to align them with human preferences. Group-based methods like GRPO avoid training a critic by sampling multiple responses per prompt, while critic-based methods like PPO estimate token-level advantages from a single response but are often unstable. Generalized Advantage Estimation (GAE) is a technique to balance bias and variance in advantage estimation, and DPPO is a variant of PPO that addresses trust region issues in LLM RL.

OpenAI has published benchmark results for its custom inference chip, Jalapeño, showing it outperforms Nvidia's GB200/GB300 and Rubin in inference performance, delivering up to 2x tokens per watt at peak throughput. The chip, developed in just over a year by a small team, is set for mass deployment next year. This marks a significant milestone in AI hardware, as OpenAI's custom silicon now rivals and even surpasses Nvidia's offerings in inference, potentially reducing reliance on Nvidia and driving innovation in the AI accelerator market. Each Jalapeño chip features 216GB of HBM4 memory and delivers up to 13.4 PFLOP/s in mxfp4 precision. The chip was designed starting in October 2024, with the first version going into production in November 2025 and first chips ready in May 2026; the software stack reached competitive performance in under three months. The team behind it is only about 30 people.

telegram · ai_newz · Aug 26, 11:10

MXFP4 is a 4-bit floating-point format with block-level scaling, enabling efficient low-precision computation while preserving model accuracy. OpenAI's Jalapeño chip is an inference ASIC co-designed with Broadcom, part of a broader trend of AI companies developing custom silicon to optimize performance and reduce costs.

## [TRACE: Transition-Aware Framework Boosts Multi-Objective Materials Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRACE, a transition-aware residual control framework, treats evaluated edits as feedback units to improve multi-objective materials discovery with LLM agents. In a controlled comparison, it raised the macro-average hit rate from 18.13% to 25.96% over the LLEMA baseline. This advancement addresses a key limitation in LLM-agent-based materials discovery: the inefficient use of costly property evaluations. By enabling agents to learn from edit transitions, TRACE can accelerate the discovery of materials with multiple desired properties, potentially impacting fields like energy storage and catalysis. TRACE records parent-edit-child transitions with observed property deltas, aggregates transition evidence to estimate reusable edit effects, and ranks future edits by their predicted ability to reduce constraint violations while avoiding damage to satisfied objectives. The framework is designed for oracle-limited settings, where property evaluations are expensive and limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective materials discovery involves finding materials that satisfy multiple conflicting properties, such as high strength and low density. LLM agents propose candidate materials and their modifications, but traditional methods only store evaluated candidates and scores, missing the causal link between edits and property changes. TRACE introduces a transition-aware approach that leverages this causal information to guide more effective search.

## [STEP-KTODER: Function-Level Feedback Boosts Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23632) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STEP-KTODER introduces a framework for code preference optimization that defines steps as module-level functions and uses automatically generated unit tests to provide binary correctness labels. It outperforms outcome-only KTO and DPO on HumanEval(+), MBPP(+), BigCodeBench, and LiveCodeBench. This work addresses the underexplored area of process supervision in code generation, offering a practical method to improve code LLMs. By showing that execution-based labels are crucial, it highlights the limitations of LLM-as-a-judge and could influence future alignment strategies for code models. The framework combines function-level process supervision with outcome-level feedback on the full program, instantiating stepwise KTO. Analysis shows that LLM-as-a-judge annotations systematically over-predict function failures, corrupting positive step labels and degrading downstream preference optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Preference optimization methods like DPO and KTO align LLMs with human preferences using binary signals or preference pairs. In code generation, process supervision is challenging because there is no standard notion of a 'step', unlike chain-of-thought in math reasoning. STEP-KTODER defines steps as module-level functions in decomposed multi-function programs, enabling automatic unit test generation for supervision.

## [FLARE: A Framework for Economic Evaluation of AI in Healthcare](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23643) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FLARE, a systematic and uncertainty-aware framework that combines fuzzy logic, time-driven activity-based costing, and return on investment analysis to evaluate the financial and operational viability of AI adoption in healthcare. It was demonstrated through a case study on AI-assisted large vessel occlusion detection in the CT stroke pathway, identifying a break-even threshold of approximately 3,992 patients per year. This framework addresses a critical gap in healthcare AI evaluation by moving beyond accuracy metrics to consider economic viability, which is essential for real-world adoption. It provides a transparent decision-support tool for clinicians, administrators, and policymakers, potentially influencing how AI investments are assessed in healthcare. The case study showed that economic benefit depends on patient volume, verification time, infrastructure choices, and workflow design, not just algorithmic performance. Under expected assumptions, positive first-year return on investment was achieved at typical annual stroke volumes of about 5,000 patients.

Large vessel occlusion (LVO) detection is critical in acute ischemic stroke, as timely identification enables endovascular clot retrieval within a limited therapeutic window.

This paper proposes a normative framework for delegating scientific reasoning to LLMs, introducing constructs like content origin, verification, responsibility, and epistemic audit to ensure human accountability. As LLMs become routine in research, this framework addresses the critical need for human oversight and accountability, offering a vocabulary to distinguish responsible delegation from neglect of epistemic responsibility, which is vital for maintaining scientific integrity. The framework separates content origin O(g), verification V(g), responsibility R(g), accountable ownership M(g), and epistemic outcome E(g), and introduces the concept of an 'epistemic audit' as a structured record for transparency. It argues that the ethical boundary is set by verification and human ownership, not machine involvement degree.

## [MolEmb: MLLMs as General Molecular Embedding Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23646) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MolEmb is a lightweight framework that adapts multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to serve as general molecular embedding models by aligning molecular profiles with textual descriptions in a shared embedding space using a bidirectional contrastive objective. The paper also introduces MolCAR, a diagnostic benchmark for context-aware retrieval, and shows that context-aware molecular embedding is primarily a data property of the supervision. This work challenges the prevailing approach of using specialist single-view molecular encoders, suggesting that MLLMs can provide a more flexible and general route to molecular embeddings. It could impact computational chemistry and drug discovery by enabling context-conditioned representations that support property prediction, virtual screening, and cross-modal retrieval in a unified space. The bidirectional contrastive objective enforces alignment and separation of paired data in forward and reverse directions, using InfoNCE-style losses in a shared latent space. The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2608.23646v1) and introduces MolCAR as a diagnostic benchmark to evaluate context-aware retrieval, finding that the ability to condition embeddings on context is largely determined by the supervision data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Molecular embedding models convert molecules into vector representations that serve as foundational infrastructure for computational chemistry and drug discovery, supporting tasks like property prediction, virtual screening, and retrieval. Traditional encoders are often specialist models built around a single molecular view (e.g., SMILES strings) and produce unconditional vectors without a language interface. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) natively process images, text, and symbolic inputs, offering a potential basis for more flexible, context-conditioned embeddings.

## [Gated Activation Steering Reduces Sycophancy and Hallucination in Medical QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23666) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel framework that jointly steers LLMs away from sycophancy and hallucination in medical question answering using Inference Time Intervention (ITI). It learns separate steering directions for each behavior and applies them only when needed via behavior-specific gates, keeping model weights frozen. This is significant because sycophancy and hallucination are critical failure modes in clinical QA, where accuracy and robustness to user pressure are paramount. The gated approach improves robustness without degrading already-correct responses, potentially enabling smaller models to match the performance of much larger ones in clinical settings. The framework uses contrastive clinical pairs to learn steering directions for hallucination and sycophancy, applied to causally verified attention heads. In experiments with a 4-billion-parameter model, gated steering helped the model hold its ground under pressure in 551 out of 600 pressure trajectories, compared to the unsteered model caving in 570 cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Inference Time Intervention (ITI) is a technique that shifts model activations during inference along directions identified to enhance truthfulness, typically applied to a subset of attention heads. Activation steering modifies LLM internal activations to alter outputs without retraining. Sycophancy is the tendency of LLMs to tailor responses to what the user wants to hear rather than what is accurate, which is particularly problematic in medical contexts.

## [LLMs Fail to Learn Limit Order Book State, Causing Biased Forecasts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23706) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2608.23706) demonstrates that a large language model (LLM) trained on synthetic limit order book (LOB) data can generate valid sequences of LOB events with near-perfect accuracy, but its implicit world model fails to capture the underlying state of the LOB. This deficiency results in biased estimates and spurious predictability when the LLM is used to forecast future LOB events. This finding is significant because it challenges the assumption that LLMs can serve as reliable forecasting tools for financial markets, despite their ability to mimic surface-level patterns. It highlights a critical limitation in using LLMs for stochastic dynamics, which is essential for accurate financial modeling, and could influence how researchers and practitioners approach LLM-based forecasting in quantitative finance. The paper extends prior work on LLM world models from deterministic settings to the stochastic dynamics required for LOB. The authors use novel tests to probe the LLM's understanding of the LOB state, revealing that while sequence generation is valid, the model's internal representation is flawed, leading to biased forecasts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: A limit order book (LOB) is a record of all outstanding buy and sell orders for a financial asset, and its dynamics are inherently stochastic, driven by random order arrivals and cancellations. Large language models (LLMs) are trained on text data and can generate sequences that appear plausible, but their ability to learn the underlying state of a stochastic system is not well understood. This paper addresses that gap by training an LLM on synthetic LOB data and testing its world model, which is the internal representation of the system's state that the model uses to make predictions.

This paper proposes a formal auditing protocol that measures the robustness and fidelity of post-hoc explainers like SHAP and LIME, combining them into a single Trust Score. ## [SyPS Framework Measures LLM Sycophancy Prompt Sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SyPS, a framework that measures how variations in social cues within prompts affect LLM sycophantic behavior, and proposes the Sycophancy Prompt Sensitivity Score (SPSS) to quantify instance-level sycophancy variation across paired prompt variants. This work addresses an underexplored aspect of LLM evaluation—sensitivity of sycophancy to prompt variations—which has implications for model robustness and safety. By separating baseline sycophancy from prompt-induced shifts, SPSS enables more nuanced comparisons of model behavior, helping developers build more reliable and socially stable AI systems. SyPS constructs controlled prompt variants that preserve the underlying user situation while varying social cues such as user confidence, emotional framing, social consensus, or validation-seeking language. Empirically, the framework finds that validation-seeking and emotional-pressure cues often increase sycophancy, whereas counter-framing and anti-sycophancy prompts tend to reduce it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are known to exhibit social sycophancy, often validating or agreeing with users in socially sensitive contexts. Existing evaluations typically measure sycophancy under a fixed prompt formulation, leaving unclear whether such behavior is stable when the same underlying situation is presented with different sycophancy-relevant prompt variants. SyPS builds on existing social sycophancy evaluation settings to systematically vary these cues and measure sensitivity.

## [ICI-Time: Time Series Forecasting via Visual Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23855) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ICI-Time reframes time series forecasting as a visual inpainting task, converting data into area charts and using pre-trained vision transformers with in-context learning to complete missing patterns without fine-tuning. It demonstrates competitive performance across epidemiology, meteorology, and power systems. This approach bridges temporal and visual domains, potentially reducing the need for specialized temporal architectures and extensive domain-specific training. It could enable more adaptable and data-efficient forecasting across various fields. The method uses a consistent, invertible mapping between numerical and visual domains, representing temporal dependencies through spatial layout. It shows promising adaptability under limited-data settings, though it is not yet a major breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting traditionally relies on specialized architectures like RNNs or Transformers with domain-specific training. Visual inpainting is a computer vision task that fills missing image regions, and in-context learning allows models to perform tasks from examples without fine-tuning. Large vision models (LVMs) are pre-trained on vast visual data and can generalize to various tasks.

## [Granite.Trust Policy Tools: Shareable, Actionable Policies for GenAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23870) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IBM Research introduced the Actionable Policy schema, a YAML-based format for specifying content-based safety policies for generative AI, along with a synthetic data generation pipeline and enforcement tools. These are open-sourced under the Granite.Trust repository. This addresses a critical gap in GenAI safety by enabling organizations to define policies once and enforce them across the entire lifecycle, from model alignment to runtime monitoring. It promotes shareable, actionable policies that can adapt to diverse regulatory and organizational contexts. The schema supports exception-based policy governance, allowing policies to propose exceptions to track violations. The synthetic data pipeline generates policy-aligned training data for model alignment and testing, and the tools are available on GitHub.

## [Evolutionary Recurrent Decision Model Simulates Adaptive and Maladaptive Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23932) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Evolutionarily Recurrent Decision Model (ERDM), a reinforcement learning framework that simulates how evolutionary mismatch, bounded rationality, and satisficing produce adaptive and maladaptive behaviors. In a validity study, ERDM agents naturally exhibited strategies like learned helplessness, avoidance, healthy relationships, and aggression under varying adverse childhood experiences, without being hardwired. This work bridges evolutionary psychology and reinforcement learning, offering a computational tool to understand psychopathology as a result of bounded cognition under modern-ancestral environmental mismatch. It could influence future research in computational psychiatry and RL-based behavioral modeling. ERDM simulates agents across evolutionary recurrent environments including threat, prey/goal-pursuits, and alliances, with agents learning through competing rewards abstracted from survival metrics. The model's ecological validity is supported by alignment with empirical literature on adverse childhood experiences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary mismatch occurs when a previously advantageous trait becomes maladaptive due to rapid environmental change. Bounded rationality is the idea that decision-makers are limited by cognitive capabilities and time, leading to satisficing—choosing the first satisfactory option rather than the optimal one. ERDM integrates these concepts into a reinforcement learning framework to model behavior.

## [Twin-Prefix Framework Shows Longer LLM Oversight Reviews Are More Rejective, Not More Discriminative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23941) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the twin-prefix framework, a controlled, pre-registered instrument that isolates the effect of verification unit size on pre-execution LLM oversight. It finds that longer review windows make zero-shot monitors more rejective but not more discriminative, with informedness peaking at one or two actions across all six judges in both domains. This research addresses an under-measured aspect of AI control: the unit of verification in pre-execution oversight. By showing that more rejections do not imply better discrimination, it provides critical guidance for designing safer and more effective LLM oversight protocols, potentially impacting AI safety practices and deployment decisions. The twin-prefix framework generates gold plans with one injected, environment-accepted error and a clean twin differing in one write, judged at five nested lengths to tie verdict changes to the unit alone. Discrimination is scored using pre-registered informedness (catch minus false rejection), and the calibrated short unit recovers up to 0.95 informedness over eight-action review, with no tested label-blind policy consistently beating it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Pre-execution oversight is a core component of AI control, where a fallible LLM monitor reviews planned actions before irreversible execution. The unit of verification—how many actions a single review covers—is often taken as given, but its effect on fallible monitors has been unmeasured. Natural traces cannot isolate this effect because review length co-varies with error type and position, and catch alone is misleading since rejecting everything catches everything. The twin-prefix framework addresses this by providing boundary variation alone and a matched clean control.

## [Giraffe: Efficient Graphic Design Generation via Single-Token Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23970) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Giraffe, a novel architecture that maps hidden text representations to visual embeddings using a single [IMG] token per image, significantly reducing input length for graphic design generation. It employs two shallow MLP blocks with compression and expansion modules, trained with six loss functions, and demonstrates strong performance in image-to-design and text-to-design tasks. This work addresses a key limitation in current multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for media generation, where multiple tokens per image increase computational cost and limit efficiency. By using a single token, Giraffe could enable more efficient and scalable graphic design generation, benefiting applications in automated design and content creation. The architecture uses CLIP ViT-L/14 as the visual embedding space and consists of two shallow MLP blocks: one aids the other during training and is omitted during inference, resulting in a lightweight solution. The model is trained with six distinct loss functions and shows strong performance in both image-to-design and text-to-design generation tasks.

CLIP ViT-L/14 is a vision transformer that maps images and text to a shared embedding space, commonly used for such mappings. Graphic design generation requires blending thousands of tokens across text, images, and layout, making efficiency crucial.
