## [Auditable Rule-Based Argument Selection for Deliberative Polling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a transparent, user-configurable rule for argument selection in deliberative polling, replacing opaque learned rankers with a published rule over publicly recomputable evidence. Simulations over about 17,000 seed-paired runs show the rule performs competitively, with served slates falling only 0.035 short of a theoretical ceiling. This work addresses a critical gap in civic technology and AI governance by making the argument exposure mechanism auditable and contestable, which is essential for democratic legitimacy. It provides a practical alternative to opaque algorithms, potentially influencing how deliberative polls and similar platforms are designed in the future. The rule is a one-hop reversed endorsement flow parameterized by a relation-weight function, and it meets seven checkable criteria for a civic recommender. The paper also shows that the choice between ranking arms is a coverage-versus-mass tradeoff, not a fact, and that the weight function acts as a security control affecting completeness by up to 10%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Deliberative polling is a method of public consultation where a representative sample of citizens engages in informed deliberation on policy issues. In such polls, when submissions are numerous, a mechanism must select which arguments each voter sees, and this selection can significantly influence outcomes. Traditional approaches often use opaque machine learning rankers, which voters cannot audit or contest. This paper proposes a rule-based alternative that is transparent and user-configurable, addressing concerns about algorithmic accountability in democratic processes.

## [Conditional Memory Router Improves Scientific Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23982) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Knowledge Boundary-Aware Router that dynamically decides when and how to activate conditional memory in language models for scientific reasoning, based on input proxies. Experiments on biological and chemical benchmarks show it outperforms static and random routing by preserving beneficial memory while suppressing regressions. This work addresses a critical gap in scientific reasoning for LLMs, where memory augmentation can either help or hurt performance. By establishing selective memory allocation as a principle, it offers a practical method to improve reliability, which is valuable for AI applications in scientific domains. The router uses task-specific input proxies available before generation to determine memory activation, layer-stage nodes, and signal strength. It was tested on two backbone families and six task types, showing memory effects vary substantially across inputs, tasks, and injection locations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Conditional memory is an explicit lookup pathway that complements dense neural representations in LLMs, similar to retrieval-augmented generation but with a learned gating mechanism. The knowledge boundary refers to the limit of what a model already knows, and the router aims to activate memory only when it can repair missing associations without introducing distractions.

Experiments on real-world datasets show state-of-the-art performance.

## [Evidence-First Reflection Boosts Desktop GUI Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24015) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Evidence-First Reflection (EFR), a two-stage reflector that decouples action-induced visual difference extraction from outcome verification, improving desktop GUI agent accuracy by 7.11% and end-to-end task success by 5.94% and 4.95% on OSWorld-Verified and WindowsAgentArena benchmarks. Desktop GUI agents often struggle with dense interfaces where state changes are subtle, and existing reflectors make weakly grounded decisions. EFR's evidence-first approach enhances reflection reliability, potentially advancing desktop automation and AI agent capabilities in real-world applications. EFR uses Set-of-Marks annotations to identify action locations and candidate changed regions, then describes and filters action-relevant changes before making final judgments. This decoupled design reduces visual search complexity and reasoning burden, as demonstrated on OSWorld-Verified and WindowsAgentArena benchmarks.

**Background**: The Planner-Operator-Reflector (POR) framework is a common architecture for GUI agents, where the reflector compares pre- and post-action screens to verify outcomes. Set-of-Marks (SoM) is a visual prompting method that overlays marks on segmented image regions to enhance visual grounding in large multimodal models. EFR builds on these concepts to improve reflection in desktop GUI environments.

## [Relative Timestamps and Masked Training Enable Word-Level Timestamping in SpeechLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method for SpeechLLMs to predict word-level timestamps by using relative timestamps instead of absolute ones, combined with a hybrid fine-tuning strategy and a masked timestamp training objective. The approach significantly improves timestamp prediction accuracy while maintaining strong speech transcription performance. This work addresses a critical gap in SpeechLLMs by enabling fine-grained temporal alignment, which is essential for applications like automatic subtitling, audio-visual synchronization, and speech-based search. It transforms SpeechLLMs from content-only models into temporally-aware models, potentially broadening their applicability in real-world speech understanding tasks. The hybrid fine-tuning strategy involves full-parameter fine-tuning of the timestamp-augmented embedding layer and language model head, combined with LoRA fine-tuning of the decoder layers. The masked timestamp training objective prevents over-reliance on ground-truth timestamps, enhancing robustness against noisy real-world annotations.

Traditional timestamping methods often use absolute timestamps, which can be verbose and less generalizable. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that reduces the number of trainable parameters. Masked training objectives, such as masked language modeling, involve hiding parts of the input and training the model to predict them, which improves robustness.

## [AgentWorld: Personality-Aware Evaluation Framework for Agentic IR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24076) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentWorld is a new simulation framework for evaluating agentic information retrieval systems, integrating Big Five (OCEAN) personality-driven user populations, the pass^k consistency metric, and an adversarial Risk Analyser. It was introduced in a preprint on arXiv (2608.24076) and demonstrated across three experiments, including stress-testing that revealed trajectory brittleness and attack dominance. This framework addresses a critical gap in evaluating agentic AI systems, which are often tested with uniform, scripted users and miss natural personality diversity and adversarial brittleness. By incorporating personality variation and adversarial robustness, AgentWorld could lead to more reliable and trustworthy AI agents in real-world applications, impacting both researchers and practitioners in AI/ML and information retrieval. The framework includes the pass^k consistency metric with structured fault classification, partial-credit scoring, and dual-control handoff verification, as well as score-thresholded training-data export in six fine-tuning formats. The adversarial Risk Analyser uses Monte-Carlo rollouts under four perturbation types, quantifies risk via ΔP/ΔT scoring, Dempster-Shafer evidence fusion, and Shapley attack-category attribution, revealing that tool/infrastructure-layer attacks dominate (Shapley: 46% system, 38% action).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic information retrieval refers to AI agents that perform tasks like searching and analyzing data, often using tools. Traditional evaluation methods use scripted interactions with uniform users, which fail to capture real-world diversity and vulnerabilities. The Big Five (OCEAN) personality model describes human personality across five traits: Openness, Conscientiousness, Extraversion, Agreeableness, and Neuroticism. The pass^k metric measures consistency, complementing pass@k which measures capability; Dempster-Shafer theory is a method for combining evidence from multiple sources.

ACE introduces a self-correcting agentic canvas editor that operates on a hierarchical scene graph with 98 presentation-specific tools, paired with a content-aware router (CARE) that reduces input tokens by ~89%. It also includes a ground-truth-free instruction-following judge that provides natural-language critiques for self-correction. This work addresses two practical barriers to reliable LLM-agent deployment in document editing: layout preservation with legacy formats and the lack of unique ground truth for design tasks. By improving instruction following, speed, and cost efficiency, ACE could advance automated presentation and document creation tools. With a fixed backbone, ACE's scene-graph editor in a single turn matches an agentic HTML pipeline, and adding self-correction improves instruction following (IF 4.23 vs. 3.81) at 1.75x speed and ~44% lower cost. Blind raters preferred ACE overall (58.7% decisive win-rate) and preferred self-corrected output 81% of the time; 66% of cases halt after one pass, and strict-peak rollback removes all observed regressions.

## [QC-T2I-Bench: Question-Centric Evaluation for Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces QC-T2I-Bench, a question-centric evaluation framework that converts prompts into atomic questions organized via Davidsonian Scene Graphs (DSGs), enabling fine-grained, attributed, and cost-aware assessment of text-to-image models. It also proposes hierarchy-constrained question aggregation to handle dependencies and complexity, and demonstrates a training-free routing method that reduces GPU cost by 21.3% while matching ERNIE's performance. This framework addresses critical limitations in current T2I benchmarks, which often yield similar total scores despite different model strengths, and fail to attribute failures to specific aspects. By providing reliable ranking and fine-grained diagnosis, it enables better model selection and targeted improvements, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in the AI/ML community. The framework uses Davidsonian Scene Graphs to organize atomic questions and applies hierarchy-constrained aggregation to exclude downstream questions after a prerequisite fails, preventing simple and complex prompts from receiving equal weight. Experiments on English and Chinese prompts show joint completion drops from 80.7% for components with two capabilities to 37.2% for those with seven or more, and the cost-aware router matches ERNIE's 89.51-point estimate with 21.3% less GPU-s/MP.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image (T2I) models generate images from textual prompts, and evaluating their faithfulness is challenging. Traditional metrics like CLIP score summarize entire images and prompts into single embeddings, losing fine-grained details. TIFA introduced question-answering-based evaluation, but it often aggregates questions back to prompt-level scores, obscuring specific failures. Davidsonian Scene Graphs (DSGs) provide a structured way to decompose prompts into atomic questions with dependencies, improving attribution and reliability.

## [AHEAD: Step-Aware Adaptive Hindsight for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AHEAD introduces a step-aware framework that adaptively applies environment feedback to routine steps and LLM-generated corrective hints to error steps during agentic RL training, with minimal changes to the standard GRPO algorithm. It improves task success by +13.3 points on ALFWorld and +11.0 on WebShop at 7B scale over GRPO. This addresses a key limitation in existing self-distillation methods that apply uniform privileged information to all steps, ignoring the asymmetry between routine and critical error steps. By providing more targeted supervision, AHEAD can improve training efficiency and performance for multi-turn LLM agents, potentially accelerating progress in agentic RL applications. The method introduces minimal changes to the standard GRPO algorithm and is evaluated across ALFWorld, WebShop, and Search-based QA, across three model scales. It also reaches a given success rate in fewer training steps and solves tasks within tighter interaction budgets than outcome-only RL and prior self-distillation baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Training multi-turn LLM agents with reinforcement learning typically relies on trajectory-level rewards, which assign a uniform advantage to every step and cannot identify which decisions led to success or failure. Self-distillation methods provide finer-grained supervision by augmenting RL with privileged information, but existing approaches often apply the same type of privileged information to every step. AHEAD differentiates supervision based on step type, using environment feedback as a grounded dense signal for all steps and LLM-generated corrective hints for error steps.

## [RobustTests: Faulty-Code-Driven Test Synthesis for Robust Code RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24135) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RobustTests, a framework that uses faulty-code-driven test case synthesis and stepwise dense reward shaping to mitigate reward hacking in reinforcement learning for code generation. Experiments show that RL fine-tuning of Qwen3-32B with RobustTests achieves an absolute 3% performance gain on LiveCodeBench compared to baselines. This work addresses a critical limitation in RLVR for code generation by improving test case quality and reward shaping, which can reduce reward hacking and policy degradation. It offers a practical approach to enhance LLM code generation capabilities, potentially benefiting the broader AI coding ecosystem. The framework integrates validator agents with behavioral feature clustering to filter invalid and redundant test cases, and uses a stepwise dense reward function based on pass rates to address false negatives from hallucination noise. The augmented CodeContests dataset covers a broader spectrum of faulty code scenarios, enhancing diagnostic utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a technique used to enhance LLM code generation by providing rewards based on verifiable outcomes, such as test case pass rates. However, insufficient test coverage can lead to false positives, causing reward hacking where the model exploits loopholes in the reward function without truly solving the task. RobustTests aims to mitigate this by synthesizing better test cases and providing denser, more informative rewards.

## [AdaptRubric: Task-Adaptive Rubrics for GUI Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24174) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AdaptRubric, a coarse-to-fine framework for constructing task-adaptive judging criteria in GUI reward modeling. It improves F1 by 3.6 points over baseline average and yields a 4.23-point task-success gain in online reinforcement learning. This work addresses a critical limitation in GUI reward modeling: under-specified success criteria that reduce task adaptivity. By improving reward accuracy, it can enhance the training and performance of GUI agents, which are increasingly used for autonomous computer interaction. AdaptRubric operates in two stages: category-level coarse rubric retrieval, which routes instructions to a GUI task family and retrieves reusable criteria, and instance-level fine rubric generation, which surfaces compact cues for concrete values, scopes, and constraints. The framework consistently outperforms prior reward agents across offline evaluation and online RL optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: GUI agents use outcome reward modeling to judge whether an executed trajectory satisfies the success criteria implied by user instructions. Existing reward verifiers often under-specify these criteria, leading to non-adaptive judging that may transfer checks across tasks or overlook concrete constraints. Coarse-to-fine frameworks are a common technique in machine learning to first handle broad categories then refine to instance-specific details.

## [Paritok-4B: Intent-Conditioned Context Compression for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Paritok-4B is a 4B-parameter LoRA compressor that extracts intent-relevant spans from coding-agent trajectories, reducing context to 25.7% of its original size on SWE-bench Lite while retaining 86.5% of solve quality. It is trained by distilling a gpt-4.1-mini teacher over 67,074 real OpenHands trajectories, and the model, data, and scripts are open-sourced under Apache 2.0. This addresses a practical bottleneck in LLM-based coding agents, where re-sending large file reads and tool outputs dominates token costs. By providing an efficient, intent-conditioned compressor that preserves critical identifiers, it could significantly reduce operational costs and improve the feasibility of deploying coding agents at scale. The compressor is extractive, selecting spans rather than rewriting them, with 96.0% of emitted identifiers, paths, and numbers already present in the input. It is intent-conditioned, acting within a retained segment based on the agent's current task, and the 264 MB adapter self-hosts on a single 24 GB GPU with no per-token compressor fee.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents like OpenHands interact with large codebases by re-sending file contents and tool outputs to a frontier LLM each turn, leading to high token usage. General-purpose prompt compressors are trained on prose and often paraphrase identifiers, which is unsuitable for code where exact strings are needed. Paritok-4B addresses this by being extractive and intent-conditioned, and it is built on Qwen3-4B with LoRA fine-tuning.

## [BALIGN: Balanced Data Selection to Mitigate LLM Alignment Tax](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces BALIGN, a balanced data selection strategy that mitigates catastrophic forgetting during LLM alignment by filtering out high-risk preference samples based on three key data-centric features: reference model log-probability margin, token length difference, and TF-IDF similarity to general corpora. The alignment tax is a significant challenge in deploying LLMs, and BALIGN offers a data-centric solution that preserves general capabilities without sacrificing alignment gains. This could influence future data selection strategies in AI alignment, potentially reducing the trade-off between safety and performance. BALIGN aggregates three orthogonal features into a unified composite risk score and filters out high-risk samples. Extensive experiments on standard human preference datasets show it achieves the optimal Pareto frontier with minimal computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are often aligned to human preferences via methods like RLHF, but this can cause catastrophic forgetting of pre-trained capabilities, known as the alignment tax. Previous work focused on optimization or architectural fixes, while the role of data characteristics was underexplored. BALIGN addresses this gap by analyzing the preference optimization gradient to identify data features that drive parameter drift.

## [Constraint-Guided Mapping Boosts LLM Enterprise Data Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24218) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces constraint-guided mapping (CGM), a neuro-symbolic method that uses schema-grounded constraints to restrict LLM-generated candidate mappings, ensuring operational validity in enterprise entity alignment. CGM achieves a ~480x reduction in candidate space without dropping ground truth and improves F1 from 0.08 to 0.66. This work addresses a critical limitation of LLM-only matching in enterprise data integration, where structural and physical invariants are often violated. By making constraints a core part of the generation process, CGM offers a model-independent, cost-effective solution that could significantly improve data mapping accuracy and reduce expert effort in real-world enterprise settings. CGM operates in three stages: schema-grounded admissibility constraints, constraint-restricted candidate generation with cascade relaxation, and neural ranking with bounded LLM disambiguation. The method transfers across seven enterprise makes with macro F1 0.70 and lowers expert effort by ~7x versus spreadsheet workflows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Enterprise entity alignment involves matching records across different data sources, which often have semi-structured formats, implicit attributes, and unit or granularity mismatches. Traditional manual matching does not scale, while LLM-only approaches can produce fluent but operationally invalid correspondences. Neuro-symbolic methods combine neural networks' language understanding with symbolic reasoning's rigor, making them suitable for tasks that require both semantic understanding and strict logical constraints.

## [VTC-Bench: New Benchmark for Evaluating Multiple LLM Generations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24228) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces VTC-Bench, a five-domain benchmark, and Validated Task Coverage (VTC) as a metric to evaluate the coverage of distinct useful outputs from multiple LLM generations. The benchmark uses real-data tasks where output quality and task-relevant distinctness can be checked automatically without model-based judges. This work addresses a gap in LLM evaluation by focusing on multiple generations, which is common in real applications but often overlooked. It reveals that conventional single-draw evaluation can lead to different conclusions, potentially changing how models are compared and selected. The benchmark spans five domains and avoids model-based judges, ensuring reproducibility. The study shows that configurations with the best single-draw quality are not necessarily those with the best coverage, and simple variation measures do not reliably capture task-relevant coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM applications often need multiple candidate outputs for comparison or combination, but standard evaluation typically focuses on individual outputs or reduces multiple samples to a single success metric. VTC-Bench and VTC provide a framework to directly evaluate finite candidate sets as objects of interest, revealing differences in model behavior not apparent from conventional per-output evaluation.

## [STRIVE: Multi-Agent Framework Boosts Longitudinal Radiology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24237) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STRIVE introduces a multi-agent framework that decomposes longitudinal radiology report generation into specialized Diagnosis, Attribute, and Temporal Change Agents, with a Progression-Aware GRPO reward and two-stage verification. On the Longitudinal-MIMIC dataset, it achieves the best clinical efficacy and more than doubles the Longitudinal Change Concordance (LCC) over the strongest baseline. This work addresses critical limitations in existing LRRG methods, such as task interference and lack of error traceability, by making intermediate reasoning explicit and verifiable. It demonstrates a promising direction for applying multi-agent systems and reinforcement learning to medical AI, potentially improving the reliability and interpretability of automated radiology reports. The Temporal Change Agent is post-trained using Progression-Aware GRPO, a shaped reward that assigns partial credit to direction-preserving errors and scores direction reversals lowest. Verification occurs at two stages: a deterministic Consistency Gate reconciles agent outputs before report generation, and a Validation Agent checks whether the generated report is supported by the aggregated clinical evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Longitudinal radiology report generation (LRRG) involves identifying current findings and their changes relative to a prior study, which is crucial for tracking disease progression. Traditional methods often model progression states as independent labels, ignoring their ordered structure, and jointly handle multiple tasks in implicit representations, leading to task interference and limited error traceability. Multi-agent systems and reinforcement learning, such as GRPO, are increasingly used to decompose complex reasoning and provide verifiable rewards, as seen in related work on process-aware reinforcement learning.

## [Dual-Judge Protocol for Legally Grounded Vision-Language Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a dual-judge evaluation protocol for vision-language models in legally grounded tasks, pairing a 0-10 quality judge with a binary semantic-equivalence judge. The protocol was tested on UK traffic-sign interpretation, revealing a moderate association (point-biserial r = 0.644) and an asymmetric Type II error pattern affecting 8.0% of evaluations. This protocol adds a new evaluation signal for AI systems in legally accountable settings, where outputs must be justifiable against legal standards. It addresses a gap in existing legal-AI benchmarks and LLM-as-judge protocols, potentially improving AI accountability and trustworthiness in high-stakes domains. The study involved 4,680 evaluations across seven visibility levels and two occlusion modes. The quality judge aligned closely with human judgment (Pearson r = 0.81), while the equivalence judge was stricter in one direction; the protocol adds one LLM call per evaluation and releases prompts, occluded variants, and full results.

KnowChange is a novel framework that uses pretrained vision-language models to synthesize diverse change data for remote sensing change detection. ## [New Regularization Tackles Candidate-Set Interference in Continual KG Embedding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Matched Excess-Outranker Regularization (MEOR), a host-level objective that suppresses newcomer pressure exceeding a score-blind, structurally matched old-candidate reference. Experiments on ENTITY-ComplEx show MEOR improves historical current-universe MRR by 0.0057 over replay and reduces candidate-set interference by 0.0055. This work identifies and formalizes candidate-set interference as a distinct source of continual rank loss in knowledge graph embedding, which existing methods overlook. By providing a plug-in regularization that improves historical ranking without replacing the underlying architecture, it offers a practical enhancement for continual learning systems in dynamic knowledge graphs. MEOR uses a one-sided penalty that activates only when newcomer competition exceeds the matched reference, preserving the host learner's signal for legitimate new entities. It improves historical current-universe MRR over persistent calibration, matched maximum regularizer (MMR), and unmatched old regularizer (UOR), and shows gains in all ten reported FBInc-S and FBInc-L settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Continual knowledge graph embedding (CKGE) updates entity and relation representations as a knowledge graph grows, aiming to learn new knowledge while preserving old knowledge. Existing methods primarily focus on catastrophic forgetting, but adding new entities changes the candidate set for queries, causing historical answers to lose rank even if their scores are unchanged. MEOR addresses this by comparing newcomer pressure against a matched old reference, providing a new regularization technique for CKGE.

## [ReproAgent: Contract-Guided Pipeline Boosts Paper-to-Code Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24291) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReproAgent introduces a four-stage Prepare-Plan-Generate-Repair pipeline that uses a persistent implementation contract with two channels (implementation-requirement and reference-evidence) to guide AI agents in reproducing code from research papers. On PaperBench Code-Dev, it achieves the highest mean score among same-backbone scaffolds under both Claude-Sonnet-4.5 and Gemini-3-Flash. This work addresses a critical gap in scientific reproducibility by improving AI agents' ability to convert research papers into executable code, which could accelerate research validation and adoption. It also demonstrates the value of structured contract-based guidance over ad-hoc prompting, potentially influencing future agent design for complex coding tasks. The implementation contract binds both channels to work packages, which are projected into file-level contracts and consumed across generation and repair stages. End-to-end channel ablations and per-paper cases support the contribution of both channels, and code and experimental artifacts are publicly available.

ReproAgent's contract-based approach aims to preserve explicit content and retrieve implicit evidence from related repositories.

The system uses an outer-loop proposer that generates candidate harnesses based on prior programs, evaluation outcomes, and execution traces, then evaluates them end-to-end. Starting from uniform sampling, it surpasses weaker hand-crafted baselines, and starting from a stronger baseline yields further improvement; the selected harness transfers to additional long-video benchmarks without further search.

## [OPDSearch+: Teacher-Free Distillation for Search-Augmented Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24310) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OPDSearch+ introduces a novel distillation paradigm that eliminates the need for task-specific teacher fine-tuning in search-augmented reasoning. It uses a frozen off-the-shelf instruct model as the teacher, combined with a two-stage process of on-policy distillation followed by reinforcement learning refinement, achieving state-of-the-art results on 3B models across seven QA benchmarks. This work addresses a critical cost and stability issue in training small language models for search-augmented reasoning, potentially making such training more accessible. The insight that frozen teachers can reshape student policy distributions to enable superior RL convergence could influence future distillation and RL training methodologies. The method uses a per-position forward KL objective in stage one, transferring reasoning decomposition and evidence integration skills without task-specific teacher training. In stage two, RL refines the distilled student, achieving gains of 13.1% on HotpotQA and 8.5% on 2WikiMultihopQA over prior 3B RL baselines.

**Background**: Search-augmented reasoning combines internal reasoning with external retrieval to overcome knowledge limitations, but small models struggle with it. On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student on its own rollouts with teacher supervision, but traditional OPD requires expensive task-specific teacher fine-tuning and suffers from instability. OPDSearch+ avoids this by using a frozen teacher and RL refinement.

This paper benchmarks LLM-as-a-Judge evaluation by comparing human judgments with GPT-4.1 and GPT-5 on telecom and retail voice-agent conversations, across three evaluation configurations (p0, p1, p2). It examines reliability, calibration, and the need for human oversight. This research addresses the practical challenge of scaling voice-agent evaluation, which is crucial as voice agents become more prevalent. It provides empirical evidence on when LLM judges can replace human evaluators and when human oversight remains essential, guiding the development of hybrid evaluation pipelines. The study evaluates conversational quality and safety dimensions, and analyzes metric-level correlations, evaluator consistency, and systematic human-LLM disagreement. Results indicate that LLM-based evaluation is effective but its reliability is metric- and configuration-dependent, not uniform.

## [Dynamic Internal Field Governs Transformer Cognition: Certifiability, Not Superiority](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24319) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a homeostatic dynamic field on the module graph, governed by PDEs on the graph Laplacian, to modulate transformer computation. It proves a new discrete Schur-Cohn criterion for Verlet integration with velocity coupling, ensuring integrator stability, and shows that the field's physics type does not affect accuracy. This work offers a novel approach to adaptive computation in transformers by providing a certifiable compute governor, potentially enabling more reliable and controllable model inference. It distinguishes between capability and certifiability, suggesting that dynamic fields can modulate computation without enhancing cognition, which could influence future designs of adaptive reasoning systems. The paper's experiments include a twenty-seed preregistered deconfounding campaign, showing that the second-order effect is strong in one family (+0.087) but not detected in another (+0.014), indicating part of the original contrast was capacity, not order. A kill-gate test found no evidence for the field as an evidence accumulator (Delta AUC +0.0007), and a matched-interface GRU was indistinguishable or nominally exceeded the field in some cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers are neural network architectures that process sequences using self-attention mechanisms, and adaptive computation allows them to dynamically adjust the amount of computation per input. The Schur-Cohn criterion is a mathematical test for stability of discrete-time systems, ensuring all roots of a polynomial lie inside the unit circle. Verlet integration is a numerical method for integrating equations of motion, commonly used in physics simulations. This paper applies these concepts to create a homeostatic field that modulates transformer computation with certified stability.

## [SonarLLM: Native Sonar-Optical MLLM for Underwater Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SonarLLM, a multimodal large language model that treats sonar as a native perceptual modality, integrating a sonar-specific encoder, physics-aware feature enhancement, and reliability-aware hierarchical fusion. They also released SonarBench, a paired benchmark covering recognition, counting, VQA, and captioning under sonar-only, optical-only, and fusion settings. This work addresses a critical gap in underwater perception, where optical cameras degrade in turbid water but sonar remains robust. By enabling adaptive fusion based on sensing quality, SonarLLM could improve autonomous underwater vehicles' capabilities in murky environments, and SonarBench provides a standardized evaluation for future research. SonarLLM achieves 72.0% macro accuracy on sonar-only tasks, outperforming the strongest baseline by 34.4 percentage points, and 68.7% under fusion, exceeding the best baseline by 25.1 points. The fusion-over-optical gain increases from 6.0 to 36.0 points as turbidity rises, demonstrating the growing value of sonar under optical degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Underwater perception often relies on optical cameras, which capture rich appearance but fail in turbid conditions, and imaging sonar, which provides geometric information but has distinct range-azimuth structure and acoustic artifacts. Existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are built primarily on optical encoders, making them ill-suited for sonar data. SonarLLM introduces a sonar-specific encoder and reliability-aware fusion to dynamically weight modalities based on sensing quality, addressing this limitation.

## [OODA-Tool: Separating State from Action for Reliable Multi-Turn Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24368) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OODA-Tool, a typed closed-loop policy inspired by Boyd's OODA loop, which separates state preservation from action generation in multi-turn tool use. It consistently improves task success across Qwen3 models (0.6B to 14B) compared to direct function-calling and ReAct-style policies. This addresses the state-action competition problem in multi-turn tool use, a key challenge for AI agents that must track evolving task states. By decoupling state tracking from action generation, OODA-Tool offers a more reliable approach, potentially improving agent performance in complex, multi-step tasks. OODA-Tool routes each decision through controller-checked intermediate states: Observe reconstructs the task state, Orient determines execution warrant, Decide forms an admissible action structure, and Act realizes the external output. The gains are larger on smaller models and tasks where actions depend strongly on accumulated information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-turn tool use requires an agent to maintain an evolving task state while generating actions consistent with it. Traditional approaches like direct function-calling and ReAct-style policies couple state tracking and action generation in the same autoregressive trajectory, leading to state-action competition. The OODA loop, developed by military strategist John Boyd, is a decision-making model that emphasizes continuous observation, orientation, decision, and action, which inspired this work.

This paper introduces ViralRecipesTrans, a new dataset of procedural execution flow graphs extracted from cooking video transcripts, and formulates procedural stylometry as a graph learning and process discovery task. It also proposes a novel generative task of predicting a creator's exact structural execution graph for unseen dishes. This work bridges procedural knowledge, graph learning, and computational social science, offering a way to capture and generate personalized workflows. It could enable more personalized content generation and deepen our understanding of creator style in procedural domains. The study reveals a duality: traditional lexical classifiers overfit via semantic leakage, while discrete topological metrics capture rigid physical constraints of a creator's workflow. The proposed structured two-stage model achieves superior topological control via rigid Markovian priors, and an ensemble approach combines global semantic reasoning with localized topological footprints.

## [Behavior-Guided Online Probabilistic Forecasting for EV Charging Loads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24441) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel behavior-guided online probabilistic forecasting framework for EV charging loads, which explicitly models persistent station-specific patterns and recent behavioral changes. The method achieves significant improvements, reducing MSE and Pinball loss by 15.3% and 17.8% for 1-hour-ahead forecasts, and by 16.8% and 22.6% for 4-hour-ahead forecasts, compared to the best baselines. Accurate EV charging load forecasting is crucial for smart grid operations and EV infrastructure planning. This work addresses the challenges of behavioral heterogeneity and temporal variability, offering a practical solution that can improve grid stability and resource allocation as EV adoption grows. The framework constructs a dual-timescale behavior representation to distinguish long-term charging characteristics from recent behavioral states, and uses semantic encoding to guide drift-aware forecasting adaptation. A delayed-feedback mechanism ensures temporally consistent online updates when observations arrive across different forecasting horizons. Experiments were conducted on ten heterogeneous real-world charging stations.

## [Mahalanobis-Based Multi-Head Attention Achieves High Accuracy with 119K Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24462) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MHA-CSP, a novel attention mechanism that replaces dot-product with a Mahalanobis distance-based RBF kernel, enabling direct Tree Attention construction and cross-head collaboration. With only 119K parameters, it outperforms Transformer and GCN baselines on long-sequence state tracking tasks. This work demonstrates a new efficiency-performance trade-off for structured reasoning, showing that complex-valued state propagation with collaborative multi-head rectification can capture symbolic structures effectively. It could inspire more parameter-efficient attention mechanisms for long-context tasks. The Mahalanobis distance's positive definiteness allows direct construction of Tree Attention, with a LogSumExp correction that subtracts the log-sum of edge exponentials. Teacher forcing is applied only at the final hidden state, and the multi-head distance matrices are repurposed for attention meshing to boost accuracy and training efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Standard transformer attention uses dot-product similarity, which has quadratic complexity. Tree Attention is a topology-aware decoding method that reduces complexity by leveraging hierarchical structures. The Mahalanobis distance is a scale-invariant metric that accounts for data correlations, and RBF kernels map inputs into infinite-dimensional feature spaces.

The paper introduces HMGCLIP, a unified multimodal embedding framework that leverages a heterogeneous hypergraph to mine structure-aware hard negatives and aligns multi-granular semantics at relation and hyperedge levels. It also releases a new fine-grained e-commerce dataset and demonstrates superior performance over strong baselines on this dataset and the public MAVE benchmark. This work addresses a critical limitation of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in fine-grained attribute discrimination, which is essential for e-commerce tasks like distinguishing subtle material differences. By proposing a novel framework and dataset, it provides a valuable contribution to both the multimodal learning and e-commerce communities, potentially improving product search and recommendation systems. HMGCLIP uses a heterogeneous hypergraph as a structural prior to construct hard negatives and performs contrastive learning at both relation-level and hyperedge-level views. It also employs a dual-granularity inference mechanism that dynamically fuses attribute evidence for fine-grained and coarse-grained tasks, and the released dataset enables future benchmarking.

This paper proposes a novel reinforcement learning-guided evolutionary policy optimization framework, RLOSMEA, for heterogeneous agile Earth observation satellite scheduling with preference-adjustable weighted objectives. It decouples schedule decoding, population-based search, and online actor-critic operator control, allowing RL to select high-level search operators rather than constructing schedules directly. This work addresses a complex real-world scheduling problem in satellite operations, potentially improving efficiency and utility for heterogeneous satellite constellations. By combining reinforcement learning with evolutionary optimization, it offers a new paradigm that could inspire similar hybrid approaches in other combinatorial optimization domains. The framework uses assignment-based indirect encoding with decoder-based equivalent-cost evaluation to handle satellite-dependent constraints while integrating task gain, energy saving, and load balance into a scalar utility. Experiments on heterogeneous AEOS scenarios show RLOSMEA achieves higher overall weighted utility and more stable convergence than representative metaheuristic baselines, with sensitivity and learning-behavior analyses confirming robustness.

## [PhysMLLMs: Injecting Spatial Priors for Video MLLM Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24574) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PhysMLLMs introduces a training-stage architecture that injects physics-inspired spatial continuity priors into video multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Its core mechanism, Global Representation Prior Alignment (REPA-Global), distills global visual representations from a frozen DINOv2 teacher using an offline embedding cache and a scheduled distillation plan, improving spatio-temporal consistency in referring segmentation and grounded reasoning without adding inference cost. This work addresses a critical limitation in video MLLMs: spatio-temporal inconsistencies such as jitter, drift, and identity switches, especially for small, occluded, or fast-moving targets. By improving temporal stability without sacrificing image-level grounding or general multimodal capability, PhysMLLMs could enhance applications like video editing, autonomous driving, and embodied AI, and may inspire further research into prior injection methods for video understanding. The method uses an offline embedding cache to store DINOv2 features and a scheduled distillation plan to gradually align the student model's global representations. Experiments show improvements on video segmentation benchmarks, with larger gains on challenging cases, while maintaining comparable performance on single-frame referring segmentation and general VLM benchmarks. Code is available at the provided GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) extend LLMs to understand and reason about video content, enabling tasks like language-guided video segmentation. However, they often suffer from spatio-temporal inconsistencies because training lacks explicit spatial priors. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision model that learns robust visual features without labels, and representation alignment (REPA) is a technique that distills representations from a strong teacher to guide training. PhysMLLMs combines these ideas to inject spatial continuity priors into video MLLMs.

This method addresses a key challenge in mechanistic interpretability by enabling scalable, domain-wide analysis of neuron behavior. It could significantly speed up interpretability research and improve the reliability of neuron-level analyses in large language models. RACE scores every neuron at every layer for functional consistency with respect to a target-domain observation set. Perturbation experiments confirm its superior domain specificity, and token-distribution-level results verify the association between selected neurons and the target domain.

## [AtlasNav Tackles Evidence Blindness in Direct Corpus Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24764) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of Evidence Blindness in direct corpus interaction (DCI) by LLM agents and proposes AtlasNav, a persistent multi-view corpus-navigation framework. On BrowseComp-Plus, AtlasNav achieves 92.05% strict accuracy while reducing online inference cost by 30.21% compared to the prior state of the art. This work addresses a critical limitation in current RAG and agentic search approaches, showing that how a corpus is represented significantly impacts evidence realization under finite interaction budgets. It could influence future agentic search systems by promoting persistent corpus organization over per-query reconstruction. AtlasNav organizes the corpus once into a Corpus Atlas, allowing each query to navigate adaptively rather than reconstruct shared structure. It also performs well on PhantomWiki under controlled 10K-1M scaling and transfers competitively to heterogeneous enterprise knowledge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Direct Corpus Interaction (DCI) is an agentic retrieval paradigm where an LLM agent searches raw corpora using general-purpose terminal tools like grep, file reads, and shell commands, without relying on embedding models or vector indexes. Evidence Blindness refers to the progressive silent loss of required evidence during multi-stage interaction, where evidence may fail to surface, remain unopened, or fail to expose its decisive fragment. Traditional RAG systems rely on pre-computed indexes, while DCI keeps the full corpus accessible but often lacks reusable structure, leading to inefficient online reconstruction.

<a id="item-239"></a>
## [CES-PK: Verifying LLM KGQA Answers with Lightweight Symbolic Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24824) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Constrained Entity Selection under Partial Knowledge (CES-PK), a novel problem formulation for verifying LLM-generated candidate answers in knowledge graph question answering (KGQA) using lightweight symbolic constraints without full semantic parsing. The method employs three-valued constraint semantics (satisfied, violated, unknown) to handle incomplete knowledge graphs and is instantiated over the Hetionet biomedical KG, showing precision improvements while preserving recall. This work addresses a significant gap in LLM-based KGQA by balancing robustness and formal guarantees, offering a complementary approach to brittle semantic parsing and ungrounded LLM reasoning. It could improve the reliability of LLM-powered QA systems in domains like biomedicine, where knowledge graphs are incomplete and complex. The CES-PK framework uses type, relation, and exclusion constraints derived from the question to filter invalid candidates and provide symbolic evidence for valid ones. Experiments on Hetionet show that precision improves by filtering invalid candidates, while recall is preserved by retaining candidates whose constraints are not explicitly violated, and satisfied constraints help rank remaining candidates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph question answering (KGQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge graphs. LLMs are increasingly used for KGQA but often produce ungrounded or hallucinated answers. Traditional methods rely on full semantic parsing into executable queries like SPARQL, which is brittle with complex schemas or incomplete KGs, while LLM-reasoning approaches lack formal guarantees. This paper proposes a middle ground: verifying LLM-generated candidates with lightweight symbolic constraints, using three-valued logic to handle open-world assumptions.

## [Dual-Dimensional LLM Framework for Item Similarity Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24825) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a dual-dimensional LLM-based framework (AISA) that analyzes incidental content similarity in large-scale assessments through Structured Decomposition and Semantic Relatedness, showing better alignment with psychometric indicators than traditional metrics like BLEU or cosine similarity. This framework addresses the growing problem of incidental content redundancy in automatically generated assessment items, which can threaten test validity. By improving similarity detection, it supports better item bank curation and adaptive testing, potentially enhancing the quality and fairness of large-scale assessments. The framework operationalizes similarity through two dimensions: structured decomposition (analyzing structural elements) and semantic relatedness (capturing meaning). Psychometric validation shows LLM-derived metrics align more closely with construct-irrelevant local dependence indicators and yield more coherent item parameter groupings. Simulations in CAT demonstrate that LLM-based similarity constraints improve estimation stability and reduce bias with minimal efficiency trade-offs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large-scale assessments increasingly use automatic item generation (AIG) to create test items, but this can lead to incidental content redundancy where construct-irrelevant elements like wording become repetitive. Traditional text similarity metrics such as BLEU or cosine similarity fail to capture both structural and semantic nuances. This study leverages large language models (LLMs) to better analyze item similarity, addressing a key challenge in educational measurement.

## [FedV-KGQA: Privacy-Preserving Multi-Hop QA over Vertically Partitioned Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24846) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedV-KGQA is a new framework that enables multi-hop question answering over knowledge graphs vertically partitioned across organizations, where each silo owns disjoint relations but shares entities. It uses local graph enrichment and knowledge graph embeddings to keep raw triples and relation parameters within each silo, eliminating the need for inter-silo communication during runtime. This work addresses a critical challenge in federated learning and knowledge graph question answering: answering multi-hop questions when facts are distributed across organizations without centralizing sensitive data. By preserving data privacy and avoiding inter-silo communication, FedV-KGQA could enable collaborative QA systems in domains like healthcare and finance where data sharing is restricted. The framework introduces a topic entity anchoring mechanism that grounds questions in the correct graph neighborhood without runtime inter-silo communication. It was evaluated across 12 model configurations on three benchmarks, showing strong performance close to centralized systems, generalization to 3-hop reasoning, and robustness to embedding perturbations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph question answering (KGQA) typically relies on a centralized knowledge graph, but real-world data is often distributed across organizations due to governance and data sovereignty constraints. In vertically partitioned knowledge graphs, organizations share entities but own disjoint sets of relations, making multi-hop reasoning difficult without sharing raw data. Knowledge graph embeddings (KGE) learn low-dimensional representations of entities and relations, enabling reasoning tasks while preserving semantic meaning. Multi-hop question answering requires integrating scattered evidence across multiple reasoning steps, which is challenging in distributed settings.

## [HetSkills: Unified Latent Space for Heterogeneous Skill Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HetSkills is a novel framework that progressively learns heterogeneous skills in a unified latent space for physics-based character control, integrating motion tracking, text-to-motion generation, and task adaptation. It introduces motion intuition distillation and a task-guidance module to prevent shortcut learning and enable dynamic action adjustment based on language instructions. This framework addresses the challenge of integrating diverse skills from multiple data sources and supervision forms into a single controller, which is crucial for long-horizon tasks in animation and robotics. By enabling seamless skill expansion without retraining, it could significantly improve the adaptability and efficiency of physics-based character control systems. HetSkills starts with a tracking skill that establishes a shared motion decoder, reusable across tasks. The motion intuition distillation grounds text-to-motion generation in language semantics, while the task-guidance module dynamically adjusts actions based on high-level instructions, preserving natural motion and enabling continuous skill expansion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-based character control involves simulating characters with physical dynamics, requiring controllers to generate realistic motions. Traditional methods often train separate controllers for different skills, which is inefficient. Unified latent spaces aim to share representations across tasks, but integrating heterogeneous skills remains challenging. HetSkills builds on this idea by using a shared executable interface and distillation techniques to align diverse supervision forms.

## [New Framework Models Visuospatial Complexity in Embodied Active Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23572) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a holistic framework for analyzing multimodal complexity in embodied perception, grounded in embodied cognition and active vision theories. It expands visual complexity into categories including quantitative, structural, dynamic, auditory, and interactional attributes, and demonstrates its application in everyday driving scenarios. This framework provides a human-centered semantic template for explainable computational analysis of visuospatial complexity, which could advance research in human-robot interaction, computer vision, and cognitive science. It also offers practical guidance for creating and evaluating benchmark datasets that centralize cognitive human factors, potentially improving AI systems' ability to interpret dynamic 3D environments. The framework categorizes complexity into quantitative, structural, dynamic, auditory, and interactional attributes, and discusses applications for benchmark dataset creation and evaluation, particularly in driving. It lays the foundation for automated methods that interpret complexity in 3D dynamic environments from a human-centered perspective.

## [Aesthetic Scorers Show Fidelity Preference, Not Demographic Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23593) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper audits four image aesthetic scorers (LAION-Aesthetics, PickScore, ImageReward, HPSv2) using pixel-level interventions on skin tone and body type, finding that apparent demographic preferences are largely explained by fidelity preference. The study reveals that synthetic-image audits are misleading, as results do not transfer to real images. This finding challenges assumptions about demographic bias in aesthetic scorers, which are widely used in text-to-image systems to filter training data and guide generation. It underscores the need for real-data validation and careful methodology in AI fairness audits, potentially impacting how these models are evaluated and deployed. The study uses CIELAB L* shifts on skin and non-skin regions as placebo arms, showing the fidelity penalty is operator-dependent and holds for all operators only for LAION-Aesthetics. On 1470 real faces, LAION-Aesthetics reverses its synthetic preference for darker skin, and amplification becomes non-significant; population-stratified analysis shows fidelity-penalty asymmetry is not robust across groups after FDR correction except for HPSv2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Aesthetic scorers are models that predict the aesthetic quality of images, often used in text-to-image pipelines to filter training data or guide generation. The CIELAB color space defines L* as perceptual lightness, allowing precise manipulation of skin tone. Previous audits often relied on synthetic images, but this study shows such audits can misjudge bias direction and magnitude, emphasizing the importance of real-data validation.

## [REFINE: Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Evidence-Guided Code Refactoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REFINE is a new tool-agnostic, evidence-aware multi-agent LLM framework for Java file-level code refactoring. In evaluations on 450 Java files from 15 open-source systems, it reduced code smells by up to 72.79% across three LLM configurations (GPT-5.5, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, Claude Opus 4.8). This approach addresses a practical challenge in automated code refactoring: ensuring that LLM-generated changes reduce targeted quality issues without breaking behavior. By combining static analysis with multi-agent planning, REFINE shows promise for safer, more effective refactoring, potentially improving developer productivity and code maintainability. REFINE integrates static-analysis-guided smell identification, smell-informed planning, LLM-based transformation, automated re-analysis, preservation checks, and structured reporting. Compared to a direct-prompt baseline, it achieved higher median smell reduction with smaller edits and fewer public-method removals, but broader quality improvements were inconsistent and residual risks (e.g., assert/fail-call changes) remain, so outputs should be treated as candidates requiring further validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Code smells are indicators of poor design or implementation that can hinder maintainability. Traditional refactoring relies on manual effort or rule-based tools, while LLMs offer new possibilities but risk introducing errors. Multi-agent LLM systems decompose complex tasks into specialized subtasks, and static analysis provides evidence about code structure and quality issues, which can guide the refactoring process.

## [Rebuild-Dossier: Mechanically-Enforced Specs for Agentic App Rebuilds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces rebuild-dossier, an open-source, MIT-licensed tool that locks an application's exact interface before code is written and enforces one-test-at-a-time building via automated checks. It reveals model-tier failures in test compliance, showing that a passing test suite does not certify correctness when tests can be gamed. This addresses a known limitation in AI agent pipelines where multi-agent rebuilds can lose to simpler approaches. By mechanically enforcing specs, it offers a more reliable way to rebuild applications, which is valuable for AI/ML engineering and software modernization. In a small comparison, the compliant agent failed a held-back test while the rule-breaking agent passed everything, demonstrating that tests can be gamed. The tool's automated check mechanism was found to be crucial; when it wasn't running, the approach lost to simply giving the model source and one instruction. Claims are verified at three levels: agent report, automated log, and actual files, catching errors including a bug in the logging code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic app rebuilds involve AI agents rewriting or modernizing existing applications. Prior work (AgentModernize) found that once a model is strong enough, a multi-agent rebuild pipeline loses to the simplest approach: giving the model the original code and one instruction. Rebuild-dossier aims to improve reliability by locking the interface and enforcing tests mechanically, rather than relying on written instructions alone.

## [Macro-Operator Generation and Predicate Selection for TAMP Operator Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23629) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a system that automatically generates macro-operators from causally linked action pairs in demonstration data and prunes predicates not referenced by learned operators, achieving up to a 4.6x planning speedup across four TAMP domains and solving a long sequential task that the baseline cannot. This work addresses a key bottleneck in TAMP by automating operator learning and introducing macro-operators, which can significantly improve scalability for long sequential manipulation tasks. It demonstrates that macro-operator discovery not only accelerates planning but can determine solvability in practice, potentially advancing the deployment of TAMP systems in real-world robotics. The system discovers causally linked action pairs where one action produces exactly the condition required by the next, and converts each pair into a macro-operator. It also prunes predicates that no learned operator references, shrinking the symbolic state at each search node; the benefit grows with task length.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) combines symbolic task-level planning with motion-level reasoning, relying on manually crafted symbolic operators that are a bottleneck. Recent work learns operators from demonstrations, but existing methods learn actions in isolation and fail to capture recurring multi-step structures, making search intractable on long tasks. Macro-operators, sequences of primitive operators, act as shortcuts in the search space, and predicate selection reduces the state space evaluated at each node.

## [LLMs as Causal-Edge Classifiers: Recall-Dominant and Poorly Calibrated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically evaluates 12 instruction-tuned open-weight LLMs on causal edge classification across six benchmark graphs, five prompting strategies, and four confidence sources, revealing that LLMs are recall-dominant, often confuse relatedness with directness, and are poorly calibrated. This work provides crucial insights into the reliability of LLMs as causal priors, highlighting that they should be used as soft priors rather than direct evidence. It underscores the need for better calibration methods and caution when integrating LLMs into causal discovery pipelines. The study finds that models misclassify 40.0% of indirect and 36.0% of reversed non-edges as direct edges, with 80.8% and 84.6% of these false positives receiving verbalized confidence of at least 80%. Logit-based confidence often collapses near 1.0, while cross-prompt and cross-model agreement offer better calibration, though not statistically significant after Holm correction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Causal discovery aims to infer causal structures from data, and LLMs are increasingly used to provide prior causal knowledge. This paper evaluates LLMs as direct causal-edge classifiers, a task that requires distinguishing direct from indirect causal relations and correctly orienting edges. The study uses a language-only pairwise protocol, where models judge whether a direct causal edge exists between variable pairs.

## [When Knowledge Distillation Promotes Overfitting and How to Avoid It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23752) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates intermediate block-wise knowledge distillation, showing that on classic datasets, distilling only the last block is often best, while fine-grained, data-scarce settings benefit from intermediate supervision. It proposes a student design based on homogeneous blocks and provides empirical evidence across eleven datasets. This work clarifies when and how intermediate layer distillation helps, guiding practitioners in designing compact models without sacrificing accuracy. It addresses an underexplored aspect of knowledge distillation and offers practical insights for few-shot fine-grained classification. The study uses an EfficientNet-B0 teacher and homogeneous student blocks, exploring configurations of varying granularity. It employs explainability analyses including attention maps, Centered Kernel Alignment, and Grad-CAM to guide supervision placement.

**Background**: Knowledge distillation (KD) transfers knowledge from a large teacher network to a smaller student network, traditionally applied only at the final output. Intermediate block-wise KD provides supervision at multiple layers, which may help in data-scarce scenarios but can also cause overfitting. This paper studies the trade-off across classic and fine-grained datasets.

## [AI Evaluators Bias Which Items Reach Expert Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23766) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study on arXiv (2608.23766) shows that computational evaluators in AI-assisted item generation introduce biases in representation and selection, affecting which items reach expert review. Across two in-silico studies of 32,000 Big Five items, changing representation led to different items surviving, with inclusive primary forms sharing only 6 of 40 items across embedding configurations. This matters because computational evaluators are often treated as neutral technical steps, but they can systematically bias the content psychometricians review, potentially undermining test validity. It highlights the need for transparency and revisability in AI-assisted measurement design, affecting psychometrics and AI evaluation fields. The study used two linked in-silico studies with 32,000 selected Big Five items, following fixed source populations from semantic representation through structural evaluation and candidate-form construction. Broad agreement in semantic geometry concealed local differences, and eligibility policies filled all content cells but presented different wording, with inclusive primary forms sharing a median of only 6 of 40 items across embedding configurations.

## [SAIL: Disentangled Skill Representations for Predictive Human Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23776) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SAIL (Skill Abstraction with Interpretable Latents), a method that models human skill as an interpretable, multi-dimensional latent variable inferred from naturalistic behavior. SAIL uses counterfactual subskill swaps to learn disentangled skill embeddings that are robust to performance fluctuations and support skill-informed behavior prediction. This work addresses a key challenge in AI systems that collaborate with or assist humans: understanding persistent, compositional skills rather than transient states. By enabling interpretable and transferable skill representations, SAIL could improve AI coaching, personalized assistance, and human-robot collaboration across domains. SAIL represents each individual with a persistent skill embedding that controls a blend between expert and novice bases, trained using counterfactual subskill swaps for disentanglement. The method demonstrates strong predictive performance and improved behaviorally grounded disentanglement over baselines in racing and baseball, while also enhancing downstream AI coaching performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning (DRL) aims to separate underlying factors of variation in data into semantically meaningful variables, which aids interpretability. Latent variable models relate observable variables to unobserved latent constructs, commonly used in psychometrics and NLP. Counterfactual training involves generating alternative examples to improve model robustness and causal reasoning. SAIL combines these ideas to model human skill as a persistent, interpretable latent variable.

## [Youth-Centered Validation Needed for LLM-Based Student Talk Measures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23780) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new position paper argues that current validation methods for LLM-based measures of student discourse are insufficient and calls for re-contextualizing conversations and engaging youth in the research process. The paper includes a case study of multilingual eighth-grade students that reveals misalignments between student interpretations and LLM-based measures. This matters because LLMs are increasingly used to assess student talk at scale, and inadequate validation could lead to inequitable outcomes for marginalized youth. The paper's call for epistemic shifts and youth involvement could influence future research and practice in AI and education. The case study employed participant observations, interviews, focus groups, and member checks with four focal students in an eighth-grade math classroom. Findings showed that students contested both LLM classifications and the coding scheme, highlighting the need for youth as epistemic authorities.

## [RwF: Continual Learning for Image Restoration Without Forgetting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23799) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Restoring without Forgetting (RwF), a continual learning framework for multi-degradation image restoration that handles sequentially arriving degradations without catastrophic forgetting. RwF learns lightweight adapters for each new degradation and uses an unsupervised routing mechanism at test time, achieving significant PSNR improvements over naive fine-tuning. This work addresses a practical limitation of current all-in-one image restoration models, which assume simultaneous access to all degradations during training. By framing restoration as a continual domain-incremental learning problem, RwF enables field-deployed systems to adapt to new degradations without retraining on all historical data, which is often costly or infeasible due to privacy or storage constraints. RwF improves final average PSNR over naive sequential fine-tuning by 15.25 dB and 11.83 dB on Restormer and NAFNet backbones, respectively, across a five-domain sequence. It transfers to eleven real-degradation benchmarks (3,465 images) with 89.5% routing accuracy and only a +0.94 dB oracle PSNR gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Catastrophic forgetting is a well-known problem in neural networks where learning new information causes abrupt loss of previously learned knowledge. Domain-incremental learning is a continual learning paradigm where the label space stays fixed but input distributions change over time. All-in-one image restoration models aim to handle multiple degradations within a single network, but typically require simultaneous access to all degradation types during training.

## [Bandit-Driven Prompt Selection Cuts LLM Essay Scoring Costs by 78.4%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a multi-armed bandit (MAB) framework for dynamically selecting LLM prompting strategies during automated essay scoring (AES). On IELTS Writing Task 2 essays, the approach achieves comparable accuracy to exhaustive grid search while reducing LLM calls by 78.4%. This work addresses a critical operational concern in deploying LLMs for educational assessment: cost. By transforming prompt selection from offline hyperparameter optimization into an online learning task, it enables educational technology platforms to balance assessment validity with operational expenses, potentially making LLM-based scoring more accessible and scalable. The framework treats each prompt type as an arm in a MAB controller, testing four grading recipes: multi-step vs. single-step assessment, with vs. without calibration examples. The multi-step approach with examples achieved the highest accuracy, and the study also produced the first cost-reliability learning curves for essay scoring by tracking token usage and latency alongside agreement metrics.

The multi-armed bandit (MAB) framework is a sequential decision model that balances exploration and exploitation to optimize rewards, making it suitable for dynamically selecting the best prompt strategy during inference.

This paper proposes a hierarchical O-RAN control framework for tethered UAV-mounted 5G base stations, coordinating slow aerial control with fast per-user scheduling. It introduces a permutation-equivariant DeepSets Soft Actor-Critic (D-SAC) scheduler and demonstrates significant improvements in eMBB and URLLC performance. This work addresses a critical gap in O-RAN-enabled UAV communications by jointly optimizing UAV placement and radio resource allocation across different timescales. It could improve the efficiency and reliability of on-demand 5G coverage in dynamic environments, benefiting emergency communications and temporary network deployments. The framework uses a Non-Real-Time RIC rApp for slow control of UAV placement and slice budget, and a Near-Real-Time RIC xApp for fast per-user scheduling. The D-SAC scheduler treats users as an unordered set and is trained in a Sionna RT ray-traced channel, achieving up to 17% improvement in eMBB SLA satisfaction and up to 42% in URLLC on-time delivery.

A new gaze-informed transformer framework uses radiologists' 3D eye-tracking data during CT interpretation to predict their expertise level, achieving an ROC-AUC of 0.91 and F1 score of 0.86 on a held-out test set. The model integrates fixation patterns into volumetric feature learning via a learnable log-space bias in self-attention and gaze-weighted pooling of patch embeddings. This work demonstrates that incorporating visual search behavior into transformers can support objective, process-based expertise assessment in radiology, potentially improving training and quality assurance. It extends gaze-informed AI beyond 2D imaging to volumetric CT, opening new avenues for human-centered medical AI. The model uses a DINOv2 ViT-B/14 backbone and was trained on 182 CT reading sessions from five radiologists with varying experience levels. ## [New Resilience Metrics for Embodied Agents Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23839) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel resilience evaluation framework for Embodied Agents Systems (EAS), defining three new metrics: Rebound, Stability, and Graceful Extensibility. The framework is applied to 400 household tasks across 10 EAS, revealing process-level distinctions hidden by traditional outcome metrics. This work addresses a critical gap in EAS evaluation by shifting focus from outcome-centric metrics to process-level resilience, which is essential for reliable deployment in open-world environments. The proposed metrics and optimization guidance can improve human-agent trust and system robustness, impacting AI safety and robotics research. The resilience evaluation layer transforms execution processes into assessments for diagnosis and optimization. Key findings include a recovery cost difference of ΔC_rec=25.2 among successful episodes, increased instability, and task-family degradation, with metrics-guided optimizations reducing recovery cost and improving stability.

## [New Detector Fights Slopsquatting in AI Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23897) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers proposed a two-layer detector to prevent supply chain attacks from hallucinated Python package names in code generated by local LLMs. The detector combines a deterministic PyPI existence check with a Random Forest classifier, embedded in a LangGraph state machine that retries at escalating temperatures and falls back to a stronger model. This addresses a novel and timely security issue in AI code generation, as slopsquatting exploits LLM hallucinations to compromise software supply chains. The proposed pipeline offers a practical defense, with a user study showing high satisfaction and adoption intent, potentially improving security for developers using AI assistants. The detector's first layer performs a deterministic PyPI existence check, while the second uses a Random Forest classifier trained on ten features from package name and PyPI metadata. An import name reconciler resolves cases like 'import cv2' versus 'pip install opencv-python' without security bypass. Across 300 curated prompts, the pipeline produced hallucination-free code on 76% of runs, with intra-model retries recovering about a quarter of failures and cross-model fallback recovering a further 16.5%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Slopsquatting is a type of cybersquatting where attackers register non-existent package names that LLMs may hallucinate, tricking developers into installing malicious packages. This paper addresses the risk in local coding LLMs, which may hallucinate package names more frequently than commercial models. The proposed detector is embedded in a LangGraph state machine, a graph-based state management system that handles retries and fallbacks.
