## [RefineRank: Joint Box Refinement and Ranking for Surgical Spatio-Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23928) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RefineRank introduces a compact trainable module, RefineNet, that refines and ranks candidate boxes from a frozen open-set detector using language and regional features from a frozen medical vision language model. On the MedVidBench Official Rankings (Verified), it achieves 0.421 STG mIoU, the highest displayed STG score. This work addresses a key trade-off in surgical spatio-temporal grounding: vision language models understand question context but produce imprecise coordinates, while open-set detectors provide localized boxes but lack question-aware confidence. By reconciling these at the candidate-box level without retraining backbones, RefineRank offers a practical and effective solution that could improve surgical video understanding and assist in clinical settings. In a controlled evaluation on separate training and evaluation videos, coordinate correction raises the candidate oracle upper bound from 0.6772 to 0.7302, and ranking the joint pool of original and refined candidates by RefineNet scores improves STG mIoU from 0.2719 to 0.4534, whereas separately trained selectors over the same pool reach at most 0.4186. The code is available at https://github.com/linzhe001/RefineRank.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Surgical spatio-temporal grounding (STG) requires locating, at each video time specified by a procedural question, the object that the question asks about. MedVidBench is a large-scale benchmark for medical video understanding, containing 531,850 video-instruction pairs across 8 medical sources. Open-set detectors can identify objects from arbitrary categories, while vision language models (VLMs) can understand complex question semantics but often lack precise spatial localization.

## [QML Enhances NV-Center Magnetometry Under Information Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23934) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates the use of quantum kernel models for magnetic field estimation in NV-center quantum sensors, comparing their performance with classical machine learning models under measurement-induced information loss. The study demonstrates that QML-based sensing improves significantly when coherent quantum states are directly available, providing a theoretical upper bound on sensing performance. This work addresses a critical challenge in NISQ-era quantum sensing, where extracting reliable information from noisy, finite-shot data is difficult. By highlighting the importance of integrating quantum sensors with QML models, it offers a pathway to enhance magnetic field sensing under realistic constraints, impacting fields like biomedical imaging and materials science. The study formulates magnetic field sensing as a supervised regression task, comparing classical models trained on measurement-based data with quantum kernel models trained on pre-measurement coherent quantum states. Results show that QML performance improves with coherent quantum-state information, but not significantly with model complexity or learning paradigm, underscoring the need for tightly integrated pipelines.

Quantum kernel methods combine classical kernel methods with quantum models, providing a framework for such learning tasks.

## [STAIN-FL: Stealthy Backdoor Attack in Federated Video Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23952) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STAIN-FL introduces a stealthy targeted backdoor attack framework for federated video anomaly detection, using contextual triggers like low-light scenes and crowd density, combined with gradient masking to evade detection. Evaluated on UCF-Crime with non-IID settings, it achieves over 50% backdoor accuracy while keeping clean accuracy drop below 2%. This work highlights a realistic security threat in federated learning for surveillance, where compromised clients can inject persistent backdoors using natural triggers, potentially causing missed detections of anomalies. It underscores the need for robust defense mechanisms in federated video analytics, especially in safety-critical applications. The attack uses anomaly-to-benign label manipulation and gradient masking over least-updated coordinates to preserve clean accuracy. Under FedAvg, the sparse backdoor persists above a 25% backdoor-accuracy threshold for an average of 336 post-attack rounds, showing high persistence.

## [LLMs Fail to Judge Cross-Language Code Equivalence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces PolyHuman, a dataset of human-written programs in C++, Java, and Python, and evaluates LLMs' ability to judge functional equivalence across languages. The study reveals that current LLMs, including GPT-o4-mini, Claude-Opus-4.7, and Gemini-3-Flash, systematically misclassify non-equivalent code as equivalent, especially on harder problems. This work challenges the assumption that LLMs truly understand program semantics, highlighting a critical gap in cross-language code understanding. The findings have implications for code generation, translation, and automated software engineering, where reliable equivalence detection is essential. The study manually analyzed 81 cases of systematic disagreement and found a difficulty-dependent breakdown in equivalence judgment, model-specific sensitivity to programming language (e.g., GPT-o4-mini is more conservative on Python), and partial reliance on similarity-based cues. GPT-o4-mini also showed substantial run-to-run instability under identical settings, indicating inconsistent capability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Functional equivalence means two programs produce identical outputs for all valid inputs. Existing evaluations of LLM code understanding often focus on single-language settings or use synthetic code, which may not reflect real-world semantic reasoning. PolyHuman addresses this by using human-written programs across three languages, providing a more realistic benchmark for cross-language equivalence detection.

## [SCOUT: Hybrid Tool Discovery for Enterprise MCP Gateways](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23992) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SCOUT, a system that reframes tool exposure as a context-selection problem, injecting only relevant tools via two MCP meta-tools (tool_search and execute_tool) that use hybrid retrieval combining BM25 and dense vector search. In production at PayPal, SCOUT reduced MCP tool-token consumption from 140.2k tokens (70.1% of context) to 1.3k tokens (0.8%), a 99% reduction. This addresses two critical challenges in enterprise LLM agents: context window saturation and tool discoverability among thousands of tools. By reducing token consumption by 99%, SCOUT can significantly cut inference costs and improve response accuracy, making it a practical solution for large-scale MCP deployments. SCOUT uses Reciprocal Rank Fusion to combine BM25 sparse matching with dense vector search, and is backed by zero-downtime catalog update pipelines. Because SCOUT is surfaced as standard MCP tools, it is model-agnostic and requires no client-side modifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 to standardize how LLMs integrate with external tools and data sources. In enterprise settings, a proxy MCP server aggregates many backend servers, creating a chokepoint for security and governance, but this leads to context saturation when all tool schemas are loaded. Traditional prompt caching reduces reprocessing costs but does not free context capacity or improve accuracy, motivating selective tool exposure.

IterCAD introduces an iterative framework that reformulates orthographic-view-to-CAD generation as a progressive program repair process, where the model repeatedly analyzes intermediate CAD results and decides to REVISE or STOP. This approach addresses a key limitation of one-shot generation methods, which often produce non-executable or geometrically inconsistent CAD code. Experiments on the CADExpert benchmark show that IterCAD consistently improves code executability and geometric fidelity over strong one-shot baselines. The framework closes the loop between visual understanding, geometric verification, and code refinement, progressively correcting structural and parametric errors.

IterCAD leverages the executability of CAD code to enable iterative repair, similar to program repair in software engineering.

## [ChorusTIC: Training-Free Multivariate Time Series Classification via In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24033) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ChorusTIC introduces a classification-native foundation model that performs in-context multivariate time series classification across varying channel configurations without any task-specific training or parameter updates. It combines episode-consistent Random Subchannel Slot Concatenation with a shared dual-axis encoder to handle heterogeneous channel configurations. This work addresses a significant gap in time series foundation models, which typically require task-specific classifier fitting. By enabling training-free classification across heterogeneous channel configurations, ChorusTIC could streamline deployment in healthcare, sensing, and industrial monitoring applications, reducing computational costs and improving efficiency. ChorusTIC is pretrained solely on synthetic labeled episodes with sparse temporal or cross-channel rules, and it uses leakage-protected in-context learning to predict query labels. Evaluations on the complete UEA-30 and UCR-128 archives demonstrate strong full-context and low-label performance without target-specific classifier fitting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Time series classification is crucial in fields like healthcare, sensing, and industrial monitoring. Traditional approaches often require training a task-specific classifier on each dataset, and multivariate channels are frequently encoded independently. In-context learning allows models to make predictions based on provided examples without updating parameters, which is a key capability for foundation models.

## [Design-to-Plan: LLM Multi-Agent Framework for Manufacturing Process Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24039) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Design-to-Plan, a novel LLM-based multi-agent framework that coordinates specialized agents for end-to-end manufacturing process planning from 3D CAD models and 2D engineering drawings. It achieves 100% success across downstream agents, Tool F1 scores of 95.9%-97.6%, and a 60%-68% reduction in token usage. This framework addresses a significant gap in manufacturing process planning by integrating 3D CAD and 2D drawings into a full reasoning chain, rather than isolated subtasks. It demonstrates that structured LLM-based multi-agent coordination can bridge design representations and manufacturing knowledge, enabling scalable, efficient, and traceable automation. The framework uses an orchestrator to coordinate agents for 3D feature recognition, 2D drawing analysis, 2D-3D context fusion, knowledge retrieval, process sequencing, tool selection, and report generation. It combines deterministic modules with LLM reasoning agents, and is evaluated on 300 benchmark cases with ReAct-enabled agents, achieving 90% source detection accuracy in conflict analysis.

**Background**: Manufacturing process planning transforms design information into manufacturing decisions, but existing approaches often focus on isolated subtasks. LLM-based multi-agent frameworks leverage the collective intelligence of multiple specialized agents to handle complex tasks. This paper builds on that concept, integrating 3D CAD and 2D drawings with domain-specific rules.

The paper introduces the LongAudioQA dataset and the Graph-based Retrieval-Generation Agent (GRGA) model, which models heterogeneous audio features into a multi-dimensional graph and uses agent planning for retrieval and answer generation. This addresses the scarcity of QA datasets for long-form audio meeting understanding and mitigates acoustic information loss in existing Speech LLMs. This work fills a gap in long-form audio meeting understanding by providing a dedicated QA dataset and a novel graph-based approach that preserves acoustic and speaker attributes. It could advance speech LLM research and improve applications like meeting summarization and question answering, benefiting industries that rely on meeting analytics. The GRGA model integrates acoustic information (e.g., voice, tone) and speaker attributes (e.g., role, gender) into a unified multi-dimensional graph structure. The LongAudioQA dataset is used for both automatic and human evaluation, and the approach is validated on three datasets.

## [VisCache: Training-Free Visual KV Cache Pruning for Efficient VLLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24063) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VisCache introduces a training-free, plug-and-play framework for visual KV cache pruning in Vision Large Language Models (VLLMs). It combines lightweight keyframe selection with PruneKV, a layer-wise adaptive pruning algorithm that uses a parabolic budget allocation and asymmetric key-value updates, achieving up to 2.35x speedup with only 19-28% KV cache retention. This work addresses the critical bottleneck of long-context VLLM inference, which is often prohibitively expensive due to massive visual KV cache overhead. By establishing a new Pareto frontier between efficiency and performance, VisCache could enable more practical deployment of multimodal AI systems in real-world applications. VisCache consists of two synergistic stages: first, a lightweight VLM filters temporal redundancy by selecting semantically informative keyframes; second, PruneKV performs surgical KV compression with a parabolic layer-wise budget allocation and an asymmetric update mechanism that prunes keys while fusing values. The method is training-free and plug-and-play, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Large Language Models (VLLMs) process visual inputs as tokens, generating Key-Value (KV) caches during inference that grow with sequence length, leading to high memory and computation costs. Existing KV compression methods often apply uniform pruning across tokens and layers, which can cause information loss. Keyframe selection and layer-wise adaptive pruning are emerging techniques to address these inefficiencies, as seen in related works like KVTP and DepthKV.

## [OrbitALIF: Efficient Spiking Federated Learning for Onboard Cloud Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24073) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OrbitALIF, a novel federated learning framework for cloud removal in LEO satellite constellations, using a compact 2.30M-parameter spiking neural network (SNN) with adaptive gated fusion and spectral-spatial hybrid attention modules. It enables onboard training and inference, achieving 0.287 mJ per inference on neuromorphic hardware, a 72.3x energy reduction compared to an equivalent ANN. This work addresses practical challenges in satellite Earth observation, such as limited contact windows and bandwidth, by enabling decentralized training and inference directly on satellites. It demonstrates the potential of combining SNNs and federated learning for energy-efficient edge AI in space, which could inspire further research in on-orbit machine learning. The framework uses a decentralized federated learning strategy that shares model weights via inter-satellite links, avoiding the need to download data to ground stations. The SNN backbone is compact (2.30M parameters) and includes an adaptive gated fusion module (AGFM) and a spectral-spatial hybrid attention module (SHAM), achieving competitive cloud removal quality with significant energy savings.

Federated learning enables collaborative model training across decentralized clients without sharing raw data, which is beneficial for privacy and bandwidth constraints. LEO satellite constellations provide high-resolution Earth observation but face challenges like cloud coverage and limited ground contact, making onboard processing attractive.

## [Study: LLMs Underuse Minimal Responses in Counseling Dialogues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24080) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study systematically analyzes minimal responses in counseling dialogues across multiple datasets, finding they are common in human conversations but underrepresented in LLM-generated ones. The study also shows that even strong commercial LLMs struggle to determine when minimal responses are appropriate, and LLM-based evaluation may undervalue them. This research highlights a critical gap in AI-driven counseling systems, which often favor content-rich responses over interactional cues like backchannels. Addressing this gap could improve the quality and empathy of AI-based mental health support, making it more aligned with human counseling practices. The study uses a two-stage filtering method based on utterance length and content, followed by contextual verification with an LLM. It evaluates current LLMs in manually curated contexts and finds that counseling-specific models trained on synthetic data perform poorly, often producing longer, more information-rich responses instead of minimal ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Minimal responses, such as backchannel cues and brief empathic statements, are essential in counseling to convey attentive listening and encourage clients to continue. Existing dialogue systems and evaluation frameworks often overlook these interactional cues, favoring explicit, content-rich replies. This study addresses this gap by systematically analyzing minimal responses across languages and datasets, and evaluating LLM performance in generating them.

## [PARTAB: Partition-Aware Reasoning Boosts LLM Table Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24082) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PARTAB is a new framework that constructs a structured evidence interface between LLMs and tables, using semantically coherent, row-linked table regions and hierarchical selection over column groups and row-level partitions. It consistently outperforms full-table prompting and several recent methods on benchmarks like WikiTableQuestions and TabFact. This addresses a key scalability issue in LLM table reasoning, where performance degrades with larger and more complex tables. By improving evidence localization and reducing context, PARTAB could enable more reliable and efficient table understanding in real-world applications, benefiting AI research and NLP practitioners. PARTAB represents query-relevant evidence as semantically coherent, row-linked table regions and performs hierarchical selection before composing evidence for answer generation. It shows strong performance on WikiTableQuestions and TabFact, while remaining competitive on numerical reasoning, and provides larger benefits on complex tables.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown strong capabilities in table reasoning, but their effectiveness degrades as tables grow in size and complexity due to irrelevant context and difficulty localizing evidence. Existing approaches typically reason over the full table or a single reduced view, which can obscure important row-column relationships. PARTAB addresses this by constructing a structured evidence interface that organizes query-relevant evidence into coherent regions, enabling more scalable and accurate reasoning.

## [MatReplace: Reference-Free Benchmark for Interior Material Replacement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MatReplace, a novel reference-free benchmark for evaluating material replacement in interior scenes, with four verifiable dimensions and three conditioning tracks. It reveals that named-material rendering is largely solved by leading closed-source editors, but grounding materials from pixel references remains an open challenge. This benchmark addresses the lack of a public evaluation standard for a commercially relevant task, enabling fair comparison of material replacement methods. It highlights a critical gap between language-based and visual-based material understanding, guiding future research in computer vision and interior design tools. The benchmark defines three tracks: instruction-only, instruction plus region mask, and material reference image. Results show that reference-image conditioning degrades performance across all model families, with some models repainting the reference image itself, and expert ratings validate the ranking with Kendall's tau = 0.68.

**Background**: Material replacement is a common operation in interior design, where the material of a selected surface is changed while preserving geometry, surroundings, and illumination. Traditional reference-based metrics are inadequate because the task is one-to-many, and they favor the style of the reference generator. This benchmark provides a reference-free evaluation approach to address these challenges.

## [Frequency-Domain Evidence Boosts LLM Time-Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an evidence-augmented zero-shot time-series anomaly detection framework that adds global and local frequency-domain evidence computed via FFT to LLM inputs. Experiments on AnomLLM with models like InternVL2-LLaMA3-76B, Qwen2.5-VL-72B-Instruct, Gemini-2.5-Flash, and GPT-4o show improved detection of spectral anomalies on the TSB-AD-U benchmark. Existing LLM-based TSAD methods primarily rely on time-domain evidence, leaving spectral structure implicit, which limits detection of anomalies like shifted periodicity. This work demonstrates that explicit frequency-domain evidence can complement time-domain inputs, potentially improving zero-shot anomaly detection across various domains and models. The framework preserves indexed de-seasonalized observations while adding compact frequency-domain evidence at two resolutions: global (sequence-level periodic context) and local (time-localized spectral departures). Evaluation on the TSB-AD-U subset with multiple strong models shows consistent improvements over LLM-based TSAD baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series anomaly detection (TSAD) aims to identify unusual patterns in temporal data, which can be pointwise deviations or changes in recurring structure. Large language models (LLMs) have been increasingly used for zero-shot TSAD by converting time series into text or images, but they often miss spectral anomalies. The Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) converts time-domain signals into frequency components, revealing periodic patterns. Benchmarks like TSB-AD provide standardized evaluation for TSAD methods.

## [PonderPounce: Using MLLM Native Context as Robot Episode Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PonderPounce introduces a novel robot control architecture that reuses a pretrained multimodal large language model's (MLLM) native causal context as episode memory, eliminating the need for purpose-built memory modules. The System2 MLLM (Ponder) generates internal cognition tokens that are asynchronously passed to a System1 VLA (Pounce), and both are trained end-to-end. This approach could simplify VLA model design by leveraging existing MLLM capabilities, potentially improving performance on long-horizon tasks and reducing the need for complex memory mechanisms. It demonstrates a promising direction for integrating System1/System2 reasoning in robotics, which may influence future robot learning research. On RoboMME, PonderPounce achieves 60.83% with 9B parameters and 50.04% with 0.8B parameters, outperforming FrameSamp+Modul (44.51%) and current-observation π0.5 (17.93%). With 9x data, it reaches 75.54% versus 57.88% for FrameSamp+Modul. Optimized serving achieves p50 latencies of 78ms for cognition refresh and 25ms for action-model invocation, supporting 20Hz action playback.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models combine visual and language understanding to control robots, but they often lack effective memory mechanisms for long-horizon tasks. System1/System2 reasoning, inspired by cognitive psychology, distinguishes fast intuitive responses from slower deliberate reasoning; in AI, System1 models are reactive while System2 models perform deeper reasoning. PonderPounce leverages this by using a System2 MLLM to maintain episode context and generate cognition tokens that guide a System1 VLA.

## [TransPhy: Visual In-Context Learning for Physically Grounded Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24119) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PhysVICL-74, a benchmark with 74 physically grounded transformation rules and 5,240 image pairs, and proposes TransPhy, a framework that decomposes physically grounded visual in-context learning into physical-rule induction and transition-aligned rendering. TransPhy improves physical-rule adherence, query consistency, and unseen-rule generalization over existing methods. This work addresses a gap in visual in-context learning by supporting physically grounded transformations, which depend on material properties, geometry, and interactions, rather than just appearance-level changes. It provides a benchmark and method that could advance research in physically aware image editing and multimodal AI. PhysVICL-74 includes 74 transformation rules and 5,240 source-target image pairs, forming nearly 75K training and evaluation contexts. The benchmark splits evaluate novel-instance transfer and unseen-rule generalization separately, and TransPhy uses token-wise mixture-of-experts adaptation guided by localized transition cues.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Visual in-context learning (VICL) allows vision models to adapt to new tasks at inference time by conditioning on a few input-output image pairs without updating parameters. Existing VICL methods focus on appearance-level relation transfer, but physically grounded transformations require understanding of physical rules and scene context. PhysVICL-74 and TransPhy aim to bridge this gap by providing a benchmark and a framework that explicitly models physical rules and transition-aligned rendering.

## [Syn2RealTrack: Decomposing Synthetic-to-Real Gap for Multi-View Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24130) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Syn2RealTrack introduces an online multi-view multi-target tracking pipeline that separately addresses camera calibration, object shape priors, and object census assumptions to bridge the synthetic-to-real gap in warehouse scenes. It achieves a 3D HOTA of 52.0118% on the AI City Challenge 2026 Track 1 evaluation server. This work is significant because it challenges the common practice of treating the synthetic-to-real gap as a single monolithic problem, instead decomposing it into three separable issues with targeted solutions. This could lead to more robust multi-camera 3D perception systems in real-world applications like warehouse automation, without requiring retraining of feature extractors. The pipeline recovers lens distortion from images alone when calibration is absent, fuses detections across views using a visibility-weighted part-based descriptor that abstains on occluded parts, measures person height in closed form from calibration, and pairs a closed-world cardinality prior with a causal filter to remove phantom boxes. The code will be released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-camera 3D perception systems for warehouse scenes are often trained on synthetic data and evaluated on real environments, leading to a synthetic-to-real gap that degrades performance. This gap typically affects ground-plane localization and cross-camera identity association. The paper argues that this gap enters the pipeline at three distinct points, each requiring a different local remedy, rather than being a single deficiency for one domain-adaptation module to absorb.

This paper details the successful migration of a live production customer support recommender from a gradient-boosted multiclass model (CatBoost) to a pairwise-binary deep recommender, achieving performance parity at conversation start and outperforming at later stages. This migration provides actionable insights for practitioners facing dynamic catalogs and multimodal data, showing that deep pairwise-binary models can replace tree-based classifiers in production without sacrificing quality. It also highlights techniques like negative sampling and attention pooling that can be reused in similar systems. The paper evaluates multiple architectures including two-tower models, DeepFM, and variants, and loss functions such as contrastive loss. It uses attention pooling over transcript chunks, benchmarked against TF-IDF and sentence-embedding baselines, and incorporates negative sampling and noise injection to enhance learned representations.

## [PlaceSeek: Human-Centered Geospatial Retrieval via Semantic Grounding and Affective Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24133) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PlaceSeek is a new framework that retrieves urban outdoor places from natural-language queries by decomposing them into functional and affective sub-intents, using a Semantic Grounding Module to verify physical evidence and an Affective Alignment Module with a LoRA-adapted vision-language model to re-rank results. Evaluated on 31,956 street-view locations in Milan, it achieves 88.0% Precision@5, a mean match score of 3.39/4.0, and 0.920 nDCG@5, outperforming CLIP, fine-tuned CLIP, SigLIP, and a VQA-based baseline. This work addresses a gap in existing POI-centric geospatial retrieval systems by modeling both verifiable visual evidence and human perceptual preferences, enabling more intuitive and human-centered search for urban places. It could impact urban computing, human-computer interaction, and next-generation geospatial retrieval systems, benefiting users who search for places based on activities or feelings rather than just categories. The framework uses a Semantic Grounding Module to verify physical evidence in street-view images and an Affective Alignment Module with a LoRA-adapted vision-language model trained on human urban perception judgments. This paper introduces a modular training pipeline for zero-shot cross-city object detection, featuring multi-dataset pre-training with class-agnostic objectness distillation and a novel Grayworld augmentation. The pipeline uses RF-DETR, a real-time transformer-based detector, and operates within 16GB GPU memory. The Grayworld transformation is a color normalization technique that removes chromatic shortcuts, forcing the model to rely on shape priors; objectness distillation decouples vehicle geometry from semantic taxonomies.

## [LLM-Guided Action Evaluation Improves Industrial Process Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24156) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LCAE, a method that uses a large language model to normalize industrial documents into a frozen action-observation-direction-delay relation basis, which then guides action evaluation in actor-critic systems for operational decisions in industrial processes. This approach addresses a practical challenge in industrial reinforcement learning by embedding document semantics into policy learning, potentially improving decision-making efficiency and safety in process control. It could reduce the need for extensive online interactions and make policies more interpretable. The LLM and embedding model are only used offline to create frozen semantic artifacts; the deployed policy relies solely on these artifacts and visible numerical history. The method proposes a falsifiable hypothesis: if documented relations are correct and recent history reflects their contextual strength, this representation should provide a better decision bias than raw action coordinates.

The paper introduces Tlow, a flow-based item tokenizer that transforms semantic embeddings into a latent space with a standard normal distribution, enabling efficient and independent tokenization. It also proposes a novel codebook guidance to align codebook space with token embedding space, and reports significant improvements in offline and online experiments, including a 10.32% global CTR increase on WeChat. Tlow addresses key limitations of existing item tokenizers like RQ-VAE and OPQ, which suffer from low decoding efficiency or struggle with dimensional correlations and distribution complexity. By achieving dimensional independence and distributional simplicity, Tlow can improve recommendation efficiency and cold-start handling, potentially benefiting large-scale recommendation systems. Tlow uses normalizing flows to map embeddings to a standard normal distribution, allowing independent tokenization. The codebook guidance aligns codebook space with token embedding space, enhancing semantic distinctiveness. Experiments on four public datasets and online tests on WeChat show improvements, with code available at https://github.com/wjjln/Tlow.

## [Metadata-Aware Latent Diffusion for Conditional Cardiac MRI Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24342) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a metadata-conditioned latent diffusion model for cardiac MRI synthesis, integrating three strategies: Metadata-Free Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG), Contrastive Batching, and Inverse-Frequency Sampling. The framework was fine-tuned and evaluated on 59,058 short-axis CMR images from the UK Biobank, achieving a Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) of 37.47, improving by 57.04% over the same model without these strategies and by 28.68% over a previous text-conditioned baseline. This work addresses data scarcity and phenotype underrepresentation in medical imaging by enabling conditional synthesis of cardiac MRI that adheres to clinical metadata. It demonstrates the potential of generative foundation models for clinically meaningful image generation, which could aid in data augmentation, model training, and understanding disease-specific imaging features. The combined approach achieved an FID of 37.47, with the main gain driven by Metadata-Free CFG, but at a modest reduction in paired similarity, indicating a trade-off between population-level realism and exact image reproduction. Subgroup analyses showed improved alignment across demographic and acquisition-related metadata, with disease-specific conditioning being the most challenging task.

**Background**: Latent diffusion models (LDMs) perform diffusion in a compressed latent space, enabling efficient high-quality image generation and are widely used in models like Stable Diffusion. Classifier-free guidance (CFG) is a technique that controls adherence to conditioning signals without requiring a separate classifier, commonly used in text-to-image models. The Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is a standard metric for evaluating the quality of generated images by comparing feature distributions. This paper introduces multilevel extensions of envy-based fairness notions for tree-structured agent hierarchies, and proves that a multilevel weighted round-robin algorithm (MWRR) guarantees these notions under identical preferences. 这项工作将经典公平分配扩展到分层多智能体系统，这在人工智能和资源分配中日益重要。它提供了理论保证和实验见解，可为复杂组织结构中公平分配机制的设计提供参考。 The paper proposes three adaptations of envy-based notions (e.g., WEF1) and shows they coincide under identical preferences. ## [Realistic Missingness Protocol Improves Wearable Imputation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24436) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a realistic missingness evaluation protocol for deep imputation models on wearable device data, using data from a person with epilepsy recorded on a Garmin smartwatch. The protocol reduces BRITS's severe-gap MAE by 43% when trained with matched missingness patterns. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating deep imputation models for wearable data, as traditional random-point holdout protocols incorrectly assume missingness is independent across features and time. By exposing how evaluation designs obscure true model capabilities, it establishes transferable steps toward better imputation strategies for future multi-sensor wearable datasets. The protocol mines contiguous missing-run templates from training data, stratifies them by per-feature gap-length quantiles, and injects them as block masks with preserved co-missingness structure. The study extends BRITS with time-of-day encoding and a circadian harmonic channel, and finds that no single model dominates: linear interpolation is optimal for slow-moving features over short gaps, extended BRITS achieves lower MAE on dynamic cardiac features in moderate and severe gaps, and SAITS better preserves the ground-truth distribution by Jensen-Shannon distance despite higher MAE.

**Background**: Wearable device data enables continuous health monitoring but suffers from structured missingness, where features sharing a physical sensor drop out together. This paper systematically evaluates the robustness of supervised ensembles of uncertainty quantification (UQ) signals for LLM hallucination detection across four LLMs, nine datasets, and three generation regimes. It finds that supervised ensembles outperform the best individual scorer in 30 of 32 settings, with gains from as few as 100 labeled instances. Hallucination detection is critical for reliable LLM deployment, and this study provides practical guidance on when supervised UQ ensembles are beneficial. It shows that ensembles are robust across models, datasets, and generation regimes, making them a viable approach even with limited labeled data and under distribution shift. The study evaluates three axes: sample efficiency, in-domain dataset transfer, and generation regime dependence. Sampling-based black-box ensembles are nearly as effective as full ensembles, while single-generation white-box ensembles offer limited benefit.

The adapted AST enables parallel computation, and the pipeline is validated on canonical HSCM motifs and benchmark scenarios with known ground truth before application to STAR kindergarten mathematics outcomes.

## [FraudBench: Protocol-Sensitive Benchmark for Financial Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24551) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FraudBench introduces a protocol-sensitive benchmark for evaluating adversarial robustness in financial fraud and credit-risk detection, covering four public datasets and testing neural, tree-based, and ensemble models under three matched protocols. It demonstrates that robustness conclusions are highly dependent on the evaluation protocol, with post-hoc filtering yielding only 3.7 feasible-flipped examples on Lending Club data versus 2,832.3 under in-attack projection with mutability masking. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating adversarial robustness for financial tabular data, where domain constraints and attacker capabilities are often ignored. By showing that protocol choice can alter model-family rankings and robustness conclusions, it urges the community to adopt deployment-aware evaluation practices, potentially influencing future research and industry standards. FraudBench evaluates three protocols: unconstrained attacks, post-hoc feasibility filtering, and deployment-aware constraint-integrated attacks. Results on IEEE-CIS show that feasibility and attacker capability are separate axes, and black-box evaluation reveals that protocol choice can alter model-family rankings, emphasizing the need to report predictive degradation and attack feasibility jointly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial robustness in machine learning typically focuses on vision and NLP, but financial applications rely heavily on tabular data with domain-specific constraints, class imbalance, and asymmetric attacker capabilities. Traditional evaluation often treats constraints as post-hoc validity checks, which can lead to misleading robustness conclusions. FraudBench argues that robustness is not only a model attribute but also a protocol attribute, and proposes a protocol-sensitive benchmark to address this issue.

StrokeGuard is a novel multi-agent guided system that standardizes mobile FAST-based prehospital stroke screening for non-clinical users, using a dual-channel agent mechanism to separate formal assessment from procedural support. In a simulated prehospital scenario, it improved the MATES-9 total score by 10.83 points (23.8% relative increase) over a paper FAST-style form. This system addresses a critical gap in home and community stroke assessment, where non-clinical users often struggle with inaccurate descriptions and incomplete symptom observation. By improving the standardization and usability of FAST screening, StrokeGuard could enable earlier detection and treatment of stroke, potentially reducing disability and mortality. StrokeGuard employs a dual-channel agent mechanism, state-machine control, and stage-local fallback recovery to guide users through the assessment. Stage-specific scoring is delegated to constrained pretrained video assessment modules, and evidence source records are integrated with structured report generation. COCI is a novel AI framework that uses large language models and semantic mapping to extract structured metadata from Calls for Papers (CfPs), integrating the results with scholarly knowledge bases such as OpenAlex, DBLP, TIB ConfIDent, and the AIDA Dashboard. This addresses a significant gap in scholarly communication, as CfPs are typically unstructured and isolated from knowledge graphs. By enabling systematic analysis of non-publisher-based academic events, COCI can improve discoverability and integration of grey literature in the Semantic Web and digital libraries. <a id="item-294"></a>
## [Progressive Growth Biases Neural Networks Toward Flat Minima](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24568) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides a theoretical analysis of progressive grow-and-optimize strategies in neural network training, showing that they bias optimization toward flatter minima through a volume effect induced by frozen constraints. The authors prove that basin accessibility under frozen constraints can be characterized by an explicit effective curvature, and they validate their predictions in toy landscapes and a ResNet/CIFAR-100 setting. This work offers a theoretical explanation for why progressive growth improves generalization, a widely observed but poorly understood phenomenon. It also highlights nuances in the flatness-generalization connection, which could inform future algorithm design and deepen our understanding of deep learning optimization. The paper models progressive growth as progressive constraint relaxation, starting from a low-dimensional submodel and expanding trainable parameters by unlocking nested random subspaces while freezing the orthogonal complement. Empirical results show that while progressive subspace growth reliably produces flatter solutions, curvature reductions do not universally translate into improved test performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Deep neural networks often generalize well despite highly nonconvex and overparameterized loss landscapes, a phenomenon often linked to the geometry of the minima found by stochastic optimization. Progressive growth, where a network is trained starting from a small architecture and gradually expanded, has been empirically observed to improve training and generalization. This paper provides a theoretical framework to understand this bias, using concepts like basin accessibility and effective curvature.

## [findr: Transparent and Fair Credit Risk via Semi-Structured Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24582) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces findr, a semi-structured deep regression framework for binary credit risk modeling that combines an interpretable logistic regression component with an orthogonal neural residual, and incorporates a Wasserstein-based in-processing fairness penalty. It is evaluated on eight public credit datasets and a simulation study, showing it can match logistic regression for linear signals while recovering much of the predictive gain of neural models for nonlinear patterns. This work addresses a critical tension in credit risk modeling between predictive accuracy and interpretability, offering a practical framework that makes performance, fairness, and interpretability trade-offs explicit. It is relevant to the machine learning and finance communities, as it provides a way to build models that are both accurate and auditable, which is increasingly important for regulatory compliance. The framework decomposes the logit into an interpretable structured component and an orthogonal neural residual, with orthogonalization separating coefficient-based effects from residual nonlinear variation. It includes diagnostics that measure the structured component's contribution to logit variation, decision agreement, and local directional consistency, and uses an in-processing Wasserstein penalty to mitigate group disparities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Credit risk models are used by financial institutions to assess the likelihood of default. Traditional logistic regression is favored for its interpretability, but it may miss complex nonlinear relationships. Flexible neural networks can improve accuracy but often act as black boxes, requiring post-hoc explanations that may not reflect the actual decision rule. Semi-structured deep regression combines interpretable statistical models with deep learning components, aiming to get the best of both worlds.

## [Borg: Agent-Driven Simulation Framework for LLM Serving Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Borg, a composable simulation framework that models LLM serving systems as a unified dynamic graph, and uses a harnessed coding agent (Synthesizer) to automatically evolve the simulator from natural-language feature requests. Borg achieves 2.51% average throughput error against a vLLM-based real system, and is up to 284.96x and 23.19x faster than LLMServingSim2.0 and Vidur, respectively. Borg addresses the widening gap between rapidly evolving LLM serving systems and slow, human-driven simulator development. By enabling agent-driven, composable simulation, it allows researchers and engineers to quickly adapt simulators to new workloads and mechanisms, potentially accelerating systems research and reducing the cost of exploring the design space. Borg represents the entire serving workflow, including control decisions, as a unified dynamic graph, and uses a Synthesizer agent with simulator-specific guardrails and fidelity validation to lower natural-language feature requests onto this abstraction. The framework's extensions follow a vLLM-based real system with 2.51% average throughput error, compared to 6.03% for existing simulators, and it simulates up to 284.96x and 23.19x faster than LLMServingSim2.0 and Vidur, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: System-level simulation is crucial for exploring the design space of LLM serving systems, as real deployments are costly and often infeasible. However, modern LLM serving evolves faster than human-driven simulator development can track, and emerging workloads like agentic workflows and disaggregated serving no longer fit monolithic simulation pipelines. Borg introduces a composable simulator infrastructure that uniformly expresses the complete serving workflow as a dynamic graph, enabling agent-driven evolution of the simulator.

## [KENDO: A Unified Kernel Ensemble Framework for BO and Active Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24721) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces KENDO (Kernel ENsemble Disagreement-aware Operator), a unified framework that integrates Ensemble Gaussian Processes (EGP) with disagreement-aware acquisition strategies for Bayesian optimization (BO) and active learning (AL). It instantiates KENDO-BO and KENDO-AL, achieving up to 5x and 27x speedups respectively over baselines. This work addresses model misspecification in BO and AL without expensive MCMC sampling, offering a practical and efficient alternative. It could improve hyperparameter tuning and active learning pipelines, especially in resource-constrained settings, and provides a unified theoretical perspective for both tasks. KENDO uses a kernel ensemble with adaptive Bayesian weighting and disagreement-aware acquisition, and extends to multi-objective optimization via random scalarization. Experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks show competitive or superior performance with reduced computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian optimization and active learning often rely on Gaussian processes (GPs) with a single kernel, which can be misspecified and lead to suboptimal performance. Fully Bayesian treatments using MCMC are accurate but computationally expensive. Ensemble Gaussian Processes (EGP) combine multiple GP learners with different kernels to improve robustness, and disagreement-aware acquisition strategies leverage model disagreement to guide sampling.

This paper demonstrates that parameter-efficient self-supervised adaptation of EEG foundation models, updating only 9% of parameters, can achieve substantial performance gains over linear probing under fixed computational budgets. The method was evaluated on three clinical EEG datasets (TUAB, TUEV, CHB-MIT) using two models (BIOT and CBraMod). This work addresses the practical challenge of deploying EEG foundation models in resource-constrained clinical settings, where full fine-tuning is often infeasible. By showing that minimal parameter updates can yield up to 20x AUCPR improvements, it offers a cost-effective pathway for clinical AI adoption. The study found that under a fixed compute budget, peak performance requires only 20-50% of available unlabeled data. Additionally, when total window count is fixed, performance remains invariant to patient count, suggesting that overall temporal window diversity is the key factor.

A new mixed-methods study analyzed millions of AI-related news articles and social media posts, along with 57 interviews with AI professionals conducted in 2021 and 2023, to identify how AI is framed in public discourse. The study proposes a framework of three primary debates: the method of AI development, the mind of AI systems, and the morality of AI use. This research provides a systematic understanding of how AI is collectively understood, which is crucial for technologists and policymakers navigating the transformative era of AI. By highlighting the framing dynamics, it underscores how these frames can shape beliefs, values, and actions regarding AI development and regulation. The study employed computational text analysis of millions of articles and posts, combined with semi-structured interviews with AI professionals before and after the AI surge. The framework identifies debates between top-down expert systems and bottom-up emergent capabilities (method), passive tool versus humanlike 'digital mind' (mind), and decisions to slow down or speed up AI development (morality).

## [RAT: A Unified Bayesian Model for RAG Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24753) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Bayesian evaluation framework that jointly models retrieval success, abstention behavior, and answer correctness in RAG systems, factorized according to the pipeline's information flow. The framework is applied to 27 RAG configurations across three datasets, three retrievers, and three generators, revealing behavioral differences that are not captured by marginal metrics. This work provides a more nuanced evaluation method for RAG systems, enabling practitioners to identify which component (retrieval, abstention, or generation) causes failures, thus guiding targeted improvements. It also addresses the annotation allocation problem, showing that retrieval-success annotations are more informative than task-success annotations, which can help reduce annotation costs. The model extends to incorporate LLM-as-a-judge annotations as calibrated noisy observations, allowing practitioners to combine limited human judgments with cheaper automated assessments within a unified probabilistic model. The paper provides an information-theoretic explanation for why retrieval-success annotations are more informative than task-success annotations for estimating policy adherence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) combines a retrieval component with a generative language model to produce answers grounded in external knowledge. Evaluating RAG systems typically focuses on end-to-end correctness, but this ignores how errors propagate through the pipeline. Bayesian evaluation frameworks offer a principled way to model uncertainty and decompose performance by component, providing deeper insights than simple accuracy metrics.

## [Score-Based Ideal Observer Approximation via Denoising Score Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24768) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a score-based ideal observer (SIO) that reformulates the Bayesian Ideal Observer test statistic using score functions, estimated by a denoising convolutional neural network trained only on signal-absent images. The SIO can approximate the IO for signal-known-exactly detection tasks with arbitrary additive signals without per-image posterior sampling or signal-specific retraining. This work could simplify the computation of the Ideal Observer, which is the theoretical upper bound for binary detection tasks, and improve generalization across different signals and tasks. It has potential impact on medical imaging and other detection applications where accurate and efficient observers are crucial. The method uses denoising score matching to train a neural network on signal-absent images, and the resulting score model can be applied to arbitrary additive signals without retraining. Numerical studies on a stochastic lumpy-background model demonstrate that the SIO closely approximates the IO performance.

Denoising score matching is a technique to estimate the score function by training on noise-corrupted data.

This paper introduces a topology-guided actor-critic algorithm for policy synthesis in continuous-state stochastic systems under linear temporal logic (LTL) specifications. It proposes a generalized optimal backup order based on the topological order to mitigate reward sparsity and proves optimality and convergence in the tabular case. This work bridges formal methods and reinforcement learning, enabling the synthesis of policies that satisfy complex temporal specifications in continuous systems, which is crucial for safety-critical applications. The theoretical guarantees and modular learning approach could improve the reliability and scalability of RL in such domains. The algorithm evaluates policies by solving a constrained optimization problem with the augmented Lagrangian method, providing hyperparameter self-tuning. It uses modular learning, dedicating a separate neural network for each automaton state to avoid spurious ordinal relationships, and validates the approach on CartPole and a Dubins car motion-planning case study.

The paper introduces COPE, a test-time collaboration framework where small and large language models alternate as planner and executor, exchanging lightweight plans to solve tasks efficiently. It achieves performance comparable to large proprietary models while drastically reducing inference API costs. COPE addresses the practical cost-performance trade-off in LLM deployment, enabling cost-efficient inference without sacrificing performance. This could make advanced LLM capabilities more accessible for applications where frequent use of large models is prohibitively expensive. COPE is training-free and few-shot-free, requiring only inference-time prompts and no task-specific model training. It uses a multi-stage cascade where small and large models exchange plans, and has been evaluated on benchmarks covering mathematical reasoning, code generation, open-ended tasks, and agent tasks.

<a id="item-307"></a>
## [UCO: Multi-Turn RL for Adaptive Teaching with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08873) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces UCO (Unidirectional Cognitive Optimization), a multi-turn interactive reinforcement learning method that enables LLMs to adaptively teach by perceiving students' cognitive states and distinguishing genuine understanding from echo responses. It uses two synergistic reward functions—Progress Reward and Scaffold Reward—to capture cognitive advancement and identify each student's Zone of Proximal Development. This work addresses a critical gap in using LLMs for education: current methods cannot adapt teaching strategies in real time based on students' evolving cognitive states. By enabling LLMs to act as intelligent tutors that genuinely assess understanding, UCO could significantly improve the effectiveness of AI-driven personalized education. UCO was evaluated against 11 baseline models on the BigMath and MathTutorBench benchmarks, outperforming all models of equivalent scale and achieving performance comparable to advanced closed-source models. The code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/Mind-Lab-ECNU/UCO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in education, shifting from answer providers to intelligent tutors. Traditional supervised fine-tuning only learns surface teaching patterns without dynamic adaptation, while existing reinforcement learning approaches evaluate teaching effectiveness solely based on correct outputs, failing to distinguish genuine understanding from echo responses. UCO introduces a multi-turn interactive reinforcement learning paradigm that addresses these limitations by using reward functions based on cognitive progress and the Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD), a concept from educational psychology referring to the gap between what a learner can do independently and what they can achieve with guidance.

## [New Task DKGF Enriches Domain Knowledge Graphs with General Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10485) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel task, domain-specific knowledge graph fusion (DKGF), and proposes ExeFuse, a neuro-symbolic framework based on a Fact-as-Program paradigm, to systematically integrate relevant facts from general knowledge graphs into domain-specific knowledge graphs. The authors also construct new datasets to establish the first standardized evaluation suite for this task. This work addresses a critical gap in knowledge graph research by enabling the systematic enrichment of domain-specific knowledge graphs (DKGs) using comprehensive general knowledge graphs (GKGs), which can significantly improve the completeness and utility of DKGs across various applications. The proposed task and framework could influence future research on knowledge fusion and integration, benefiting fields like NLP, AI, and systems that rely on structured knowledge. The DKGF task faces two main challenges: high ambiguity of domain relevance and cross-domain knowledge granularity misalignment. ExeFuse uses neuro-symbolic execution to infer logical relevance beyond surface similarity and employs target space grounding to calibrate granularity, achieving superior fusion performance in extensive experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Domain-specific knowledge graphs (DKGs) are structured representations of knowledge in a particular field, but they often have limited coverage compared to general knowledge graphs (GKGs) like Wikidata or DBpedia. Existing methods to enrich DKGs rely on extracting knowledge from unstructured data or internal reasoning, but they do not systematically leverage the wealth of information in GKGs. Knowledge graph embedding and completion are related techniques that aim to represent and infer missing facts, but they do not address the specific challenges of cross-domain fusion.

## [CoMMa: Decentralized Multi-Agent Framework for Oncology Decision Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09159) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoMMa introduces a decentralized LLM-agent framework for oncology that enforces data decentralization and uses contribution-aware aggregation to replace stochastic reasoning with deterministic embedding projections, enabling interpretable and privacy-preserving decision support. This addresses critical privacy and interpretability limitations in existing multi-agent oncology systems, potentially enabling broader clinical adoption by allowing hospitals to collaborate without sharing raw patient data. It also advances decentralized AI in healthcare, aligning with trends toward federated and privacy-preserving learning. CoMMa uses agent-specific finetuning to enhance role specialization and a contribution-aware aggregation mechanism that approximates each agent's marginal utility via deterministic embedding projections. It was evaluated on real-world multidisciplinary tumor board datasets from large academic hospitals in North America and East Asia, as well as public datasets, demonstrating strong performance and generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM frameworks have shown promise in oncology decision support, but most assume centralized data access and rely on prompt-based assignment, which is impractical in privacy-sensitive clinical settings. Decentralized approaches allow agents to operate on partitioned data, but coordination and interpretability remain challenges. Contribution-aware aggregation, inspired by concepts like Shapley values, weights agent contributions by their measured value, improving fairness and model quality in heterogeneous environments.

## [PHMForge: New Benchmark Evaluates LLM Agents on Industrial Prognostics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PHMForge is a new evaluation environment with 99 SME-authored scenarios and 39 MCP-native tools to assess LLM agents on industrial prognostics, aiming to disentangle protocol fluency from reasoning and tool use from retrieval. The strongest configuration reaches 80.8% pass@1, and an ablation shows that replacing MCP with text-based RAG collapses Remaining Useful Life pass-all-3 from 100% to 20% on the battery class. This benchmark addresses critical gaps in existing LLM agent evaluations by focusing on safety-critical industrial asset management, where reliable tool use and reasoning are paramount. It provides a standardized way to measure and improve LLM agents' performance in real-world prognostic tasks, potentially accelerating the adoption of AI in predictive maintenance and reducing costly failures. PHMForge covers eight industrial asset classes, including rotating equipment, aero-engines, and lithium-ion cells, using public datasets like NASA PCoE and C-MAPSS. It reports Krippendorff's alpha between 0.74 and 0.82 on a 30-scenario sample, and the ablation reveals that static retrieval (RAG) is structurally limited for prognostic computation compared to MCP execution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Prognostics and Health Management (PHM) is a discipline that uses data and models to predict the remaining useful life of industrial assets, enabling condition-based maintenance. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that provides a consistent way for AI agents to connect with tools and data. C-MAPSS is a widely used NASA dataset for simulating aircraft engine degradation, often used in PHM research.

## [EngiAI: Capability-Based Evaluation Framework for Engineering Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19743) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EngiAI, a capability-based evaluation framework for tool-connected LLM agents in engineering design, and presents empirical results comparing proprietary and open-source models on the EngiBench Beams2D and Photonics2D problems. This framework addresses the lack of standardized evaluation in the rapidly growing field of engineering agents, enabling fair comparisons and diagnosis of failures. The performance gaps between proprietary and open-source models highlight the need for capability-specific assessment rather than end-to-end success alone. The framework separately evaluates workflow execution, retrieval-assisted parameter selection, HPC orchestration, and training-code authoring. On Beams2D, proprietary models complete 96-97% of workflow tasks versus 55-78% for open-source models; on Photonics2D, workflow completion drops to 20-53%. Gemini-3-Flash completes all HPC pipelines, while GPT-5-mini completes 50-70%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Engineering-agent systems use LLMs to automate tasks like simulation, design optimization, and HPC orchestration. However, differences in tasks, tools, and success criteria make it hard to compare systems. Capability-based evaluation breaks down skills into separate dimensions, providing a more granular view of strengths and weaknesses.

## [Compression Calculus: A New Framework for Intelligence as Atomic Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Compression Calculus, a formal framework for understanding intelligence as atomic compression and compositional reuse, and validates it on large software corpora under an open-world concept model. This framework offers a fresh theoretical perspective on intelligence and AI, potentially influencing how we design and evaluate generative systems by emphasizing persistent conceptual structures over raw parameter storage. The empirical evaluation shows substantial evidence-to-concept consolidation in two large projects, but a smaller third project remains below the target threshold. The reduction is driven primarily by within-corpus recurrence rather than cross-source recurrence, and consolidation depends on corpus scale, evidence-unit definition, and concept identity.

**Background**: The paper builds on the idea that intelligence involves reducing complexity by organizing information into reusable units. It also discusses the 'meaning gap' in generative systems, where LLMs act as dynamic fusion engines that navigate persistent conceptual structures rather than storing them directly.

## [AI Proof Audit Reveals PDF Extraction Artifact, Not Math Error](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14673) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An independent audit of an AI-generated mathematical proof from OpenAI's 'Ten Advances in Mathematics and Theoretical Computer Science' initially flagged a polarity error in a greedy conditioning lemma, but this was later found to be a PDF text extraction artifact that removed an overbar from a symbol. The error was withdrawn, and no confirmed mathematical error remains in the examined lemma. This case highlights a critical methodological hazard in auditing AI-generated mathematics: errors can arise not only in reasoning but also in the document-processing pipeline used by human reviewers. It underscores the need for careful document handling to avoid false error detection, which is essential for maintaining trust in AI-generated proofs. The audit focused on Chapter 6 of OpenAI's 'Ten Advances in Mathematics and Theoretical Computer Science', specifically the 'quantitative greedy conditioning' lemma. The initial diagnosis of a polarity error was traced to automatic PDF text extraction that removed an overbar from a mathematical symbol; with the correct rendering restored, the alleged error was withdrawn.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Greedy algorithms make locally optimal choices at each step and are common in combinatorial optimization. OpenAI's 'Ten Advances' presents AI-generated results on long-standing open problems in mathematics and theoretical computer science. PDF text extraction is a known source of artifacts that can corrupt mathematical symbols, leading to false conclusions in automated or human review processes.

## [Slot-Selective Masking Boosts Compositional Generalization in Shared-Genome LM Societies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20054) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a slot-selective evidence-masking regime for multi-module neural systems, where each module only sees its own evidence span. In experiments with shared-genome language-model societies, restricted-visibility models outperformed globally visible counterparts by at least 20 percentage points in 9 of 10 matched pairs. This finding suggests that restricting information flow between modules can significantly improve compositional generalization, potentially influencing future neural architecture design. It challenges the common practice of exposing every module to the full input and could lead to more efficient and capable multi-module AI systems. The study used four-cell societies sharing a frozen pretrained language model and a low-rank adapter, communicating via two model-width continuous vectors. The preregistered battery formally failed because the restricted-arm median depth-three accuracy was 0.6988, below the 0.70 floor, and an earlier qualification cohort yielded 0/10 complete passes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Compositional generalization refers to a model's ability to understand or generate novel combinations of known concepts. Multi-module neural systems often expose every module to the full input, which may lead to interference and hinder generalization. This paper explores whether restricting each module's input via attention masking can alter the solutions found by gradient-based training.

SPAR-Hate introduces an auditor-guided multi-perspective role-reasoning framework for bilingual hate speech parsing, decomposing documents into local focus units and generating evidence-grounded candidates from Victim, Moderator, and Cultural Bystander perspectives. It improves joint target-argument-label predictions on STATE-ToxiCN and a controlled TBO split. This framework addresses the complexity of structured hate speech parsing, which is crucial for content moderation and social media safety. By incorporating multiple perspectives and auditor guidance, it offers a more nuanced and culturally aware approach, potentially improving automated moderation systems in multilingual contexts. The framework uses clause-level segmentation, multi-role candidate generation, dynamic beacon arbitration, and sample-level reassembly. Experiments show gains across local and API backbones, with ablations and diagnostics confirming the contribution of separated perspective generation and arbitration; structured teacher traces also support training a smaller student model.

SPAR-Hate addresses these challenges by decomposing documents into local focus units and eliciting evidence-grounded candidates from multiple perspectives, then resolving conflicts under grounding and schema constraints.

## [GenCoord: Turning Private Facts into Executable Skill-Path Commitments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GenCoord is a new framework that converts distributed private information in multi-agent teams into executable skill-path commitments, using a small local Qwen3.5-0.8B model and counterfactual interventions to ensure correct task execution. It improves correct capability feedback from 50% to 100% across three seeds and reduces peer traffic by 92.8% with a Short DSL. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in embodied AI and multi-agent systems: coordinating agents when each holds private information that is essential for joint tasks. By making task consequences the coordination unit, GenCoord could enable more robust and efficient collaboration in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous systems. The framework uses a SELF plan and peer REQ mechanism, with bounded feedback conditions for routing revisions when the deciding capability is peer-local. It achieves a 6.9-point improvement in held-out-template success while reducing model decisions by 32%, and at matched closed-loop quality, Short DSL reduces peer traffic by 92.8% and median time-to-commitment by 68.2% compared to free-form communication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent coordination, agents often have private information that is not shared with others, making it difficult to determine who should act and how to continue a joint task. Traditional approaches rely on full information sharing or centralized planning, which may be impractical or inefficient. GenCoord introduces a novel approach where the consequence of private facts is turned into an executable commitment, allowing agents to coordinate without revealing all private details.

This paper introduces MSIBench, a new benchmark for evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) under situational illusions, along with a where-what-how taxonomy. Evaluations of 27 model configurations reveal that current MLLMs are highly vulnerable to these illusions, exhibiting six typical failure modes. This work addresses a critical reliability issue in MLLMs, which are increasingly deployed in real-world applications like autonomous driving and robotics. By identifying vulnerabilities and proposing mitigation strategies, it provides a practical path toward more trustworthy multimodal perception and reasoning. The proposed mitigation methods include prompting for closed-source models and supervised fine-tuning for open-source models, improving performance by up to 20%. The benchmark and taxonomy provide a structured framework for analyzing situational illusions, which are deviations between appearance and physical state.

## [SA-RSQ: Sparse Residual Soft Quantization for Multimodal Recommenders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SA-RSQ introduces a sparse activation-based residual soft quantization framework that uses Top-K sparse routing and softmax weights to store compact (Index, Probability) tuples, decoupling per-item storage from codebook dimensionality. Experiments on a proprietary food-delivery advertising dataset show favorable reconstruction-performance and CTR trade-offs across storage budgets of 8-48 bytes per item, with a one-week online A/B test achieving relative lifts of +2.51% in CTR and +3.66% in CPM. This work addresses the critical storage and latency overhead of deploying high-dimensional multimodal features in industrial recommender systems, offering a practical solution that balances compression and performance. The positive online A/B test results suggest real-world applicability, potentially influencing future recommender system designs and quantization research. The method avoids the straight-through estimator by allowing gradients to propagate through routing weights and weighted reconstruction. A preliminary Next-Distribution Prediction study and a one-week online A/B test demonstrate practical potential, with relative lifts of +2.51% in CTR and +3.66% in CPM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal recommender systems integrate features from various modalities like text, images, and audio, which are often high-dimensional and cause storage and latency issues. Quantization techniques reduce storage by mapping features to compact codes, but hard quantization introduces boundary distortion, while dense soft quantization couples representation quality to the storage budget. Sparse activation and Top-K routing are techniques used in neural networks to enforce sparsity and improve efficiency, and the straight-through estimator is a common heuristic for training models with non-differentiable operations.

HiQA introduces a hierarchical contextual augmentation framework that enriches text chunks with cascading document metadata (titles, section paths) and uses a multi-route retriever combining semantic, lexical, and keyword/entity signals. The paper also presents MasQA, a new benchmark for evaluating multi-document QA in realistic similar-document settings. This work addresses a critical limitation of RAG systems: retrieval degradation when the knowledge base contains many semantically and structurally similar documents. By improving retrieval and answer quality in such settings, HiQA could enhance the reliability of document-based QA systems in domains like legal, medical, or technical documentation, and MasQA provides a standardized evaluation for future research. HiQA's framework includes a document parsing and conversion methodology, metadata-based augmentation to enhance chunk distinguishability, and a multi-route retrieval mechanism. Experiments show that HiQA improves retrieval and answer quality on MasQA and remains competitive on public MDQA benchmarks, with the strongest benefits observed for structured, domain-specific, highly similar document collections.

## [Intrinsic PAPR: Fixing Misattribution in 3D Intrinsic Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Intrinsic PAPR introduces a novel framework that leverages Proximity Attention Point Rendering (PAPR) to enable direct per-point supervision in intrinsic decomposition, correcting the misattribution issue where individual primitives learn incorrect appearance features. The method outperforms existing point-based inverse rendering, NeRF-based intrinsic decomposition, and diffusion-based PBR methods in novel view synthesis and albedo estimation. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in point-based intrinsic decomposition and inverse rendering, which is crucial for applications like relighting, editing, and material recovery in 3D scenes. By enabling accurate per-point supervision, it improves the reliability and usability of decomposition outputs, potentially advancing 3D scene understanding and rendering pipelines. The method incorporates a 2D albedo prior adapted with conditional Implicit Maximum Likelihood Estimation (cIMLE) to handle monocular ambiguities, and employs a space carving loss to ensure multi-view consistency. Extensive evaluations on synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate its effectiveness in resolving the misattribution issue.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Intrinsic decomposition aims to separate a scene's appearance into albedo (surface color) and shading (lighting effects). Point-based rendering methods, such as PAPR, represent scenes as point clouds and render images by aggregating point features, but volume rendering approaches often only supervise the final color, leading to misattribution where individual points learn incorrect features. PAPR, introduced in 2023, directly predicts appearance at ray-surface intersections, enabling per-point supervision and avoiding the need for translucent primitives.
