## [Generative Learner for Causal Effect Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a multi-head neural network with quantile embeddings to estimate heterogeneous treatment effects and the full distribution of causal effects in a Neyman-Rubin causal model. The method outperforms existing approaches by over 70% in out-of-sample mean squared error in many cases, especially in small samples. This work advances causal inference by providing a flexible, distributional approach to treatment effects, which is crucial for personalized decision-making in fields like medicine and economics. The significant performance gains over established methods suggest a new state-of-the-art for heterogeneous effect estimation. The architecture combines three subnetworks for propensity score, baseline outcome, and treatment effects, with the latter using cosine quantile embeddings and element-wise multiplication. The method recovers the Stefan-Boltzmann law from Gaia DR3 stellar data, demonstrating its practical applicability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: The Neyman-Rubin causal model, also known as the potential outcomes framework, is a standard approach for estimating causal effects from observational or experimental data. Heterogeneous treatment effects (HTEs) refer to how treatment effects vary across individuals, and quantile regression is a technique for estimating conditional quantiles of a response variable. This paper integrates these concepts with modern deep learning to estimate the entire distribution of causal effects.

## [UPP: Balancing Safety and Optimality in Robot Path Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23197) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Unified Path Planner (UPP), a graph-search algorithm that dynamically balances safety and optimality via adaptive heuristic weighting, along with the OptiSafe metric for evaluating this trade-off. UPP achieves a 0.94 OptiSafe score in cluttered environments, outperforming existing methods (0.22-0.85) with only 0.5-1% path-length overhead and a 100% success rate in simulation. This work addresses a fundamental trade-off in robot path planning, offering a practical solution that improves both safety and efficiency in cluttered environments. It could benefit autonomous robots in real-world applications, such as warehouse robots and drones, where navigating safely without sacrificing too much path length is critical. UPP uses a local inverse-distance safety field and auto-tunes its parameters based on real-time search progress, maintaining bounded heuristic behavior. Hardware validation on TurtleBot confirms practical advantages despite sim-to-real gaps, and the OptiSafe metric penalizes imbalance between safety and optimality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Path planning for autonomous robots often involves a trade-off between path length (optimality) and obstacle clearance (safety). Traditional algorithms like A* prioritize optimality, while others focus on safety, but few address both simultaneously. The OptiSafe index provides a normalized metric to evaluate this trade-off, and UPP builds on graph-search techniques with adaptive heuristic weighting to balance the two objectives.

This paper introduces RegressionBug4APR, a benchmark of 200 real-world Java and Python regression bugs, and empirically evaluates traditional and LLM-based automated program repair (APR) techniques on them. The study shows that LLM-based APR, when augmented with bug-inducing change context, achieves 1.6x more successful repairs than without such context. The findings highlight the potential of context-aware LLM-based APR and provide a valuable resource for future research and tool development. The benchmark includes 200 regression bugs from widely used GitHub repositories, and the study evaluates both classical APR tools and advanced LLM-based approaches. The ablation study disaggregates the contribution of each contextual element within the bug-inducing change information, and the findings are consistent across Java and Python benchmarks.

## [LLMs as Assistive Agents in Discrete Choice Modelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21790) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically evaluates twelve versions of seven leading LLMs (ChatGPT, Claude, DeepSeek, Gemini, Gemma, Llama, and Mistral) in specifying and estimating Multinomial Logit (MNL) models, testing various prompting strategies and information availability. Notably, GPT o3 in an agentic setting was the only model capable of correctly estimating its own specifications by executing self-generated code. This research provides practical guidance for integrating LLMs into choice modellers' workflows, potentially improving efficiency and accessibility of discrete choice modelling. It also highlights the current capabilities and limitations of LLMs in a niche but growing application area, informing future developments in AI-assisted econometrics. The experimental framework varied along three dimensions: modelling goal (suggesting vs. suggesting and estimating), prompting strategy (Zero-Shot vs. Chain-of-Thoughts), and information availability (full dataset vs. data dictionary). Proprietary LLMs performed well with structured prompts, while open-weight models like Llama and Gemma struggled; some LLMs performed better with only a data dictionary, suggesting that limiting raw data access may enhance reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Discrete choice models, such as the Multinomial Logit (MNL) model, are widely used in economics and transportation to describe and predict choices among discrete alternatives. Traditionally, specifying and estimating these models requires significant expertise and manual effort. Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promise in automating various tasks, and this paper explores their potential as assistive agents in this domain. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that guides LLMs to reason step-by-step, which has been shown to improve performance on complex tasks.

## [NeuronTune: Fine-Grained Neuron Modulation for Balanced LLM Safety and Utility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeuronTune introduces a fine-grained framework that pinpoints and modulates sparse safety-critical and utility-preserving neurons in LLMs, using attack-aware attribution and meta-learning. It dynamically controls intervention scope via neuron inclusion thresholds, achieving superior safety while maintaining utility. This addresses the critical challenge of balancing safety and utility in LLMs, which current coarse-grained methods fail to achieve. It could influence future safety alignment research by demonstrating the effectiveness of neuron-level interventions. The method first identifies safety-critical and utility-preserving neurons via attack-aware attribution, then applies meta-learning to adaptively modulate their activations. The intervention scope is dynamically controlled by neuron inclusion thresholds, allowing flexible prioritization of safety or utility requirements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) require safety alignment to prevent harmful outputs, but current techniques often degrade utility or fail against attacks. Existing methods typically apply coarse-grained layer-wise interventions, which are insufficient. NeuronTune leverages the concept of knowledge neurons, assuming safety and utility features are stored in specific neurons, and uses meta-learning for adaptive modulation.

## [Review of Transparency and HMI for Autonomous Ships](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15959) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a systematic review of 100 studies on automation transparency for Maritime Autonomous Surface Ships (MASS), mapping the Guidance-Navigation-Control stack to shore-based operational modes and identifying where human unsafe control actions (Human-UCAs) concentrate. It synthesizes evidence that transparency features improve understanding and trust calibration, and proposes an adaptive transparency framework coupling operator state estimation with explainable decision support. This review addresses a critical safety issue in the adoption of autonomous ships, where opaque decision-making and poor human-automation interaction are key barriers. The findings and framework provide actionable guidance for researchers and practitioners in human factors and autonomous systems, potentially improving safety and trust in high-stakes maritime operations. The review identifies that transparency features such as decision rationales, alternatives, confidence/uncertainty, and rule-compliance indicators improve understanding, though reliability and predictability often dominate trust. It also distills design strategies at three layers: sensor/SA acquisition and fusion, HMI presentation, and engineer-facing processes, and integrates methods like STPA-Cog + IDAC for Human-UCA identification and quantitative trust/SA assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Maritime Autonomous Surface Ships (MASS) rely on advanced AI, sensing, and connectivity for autonomous navigation, but opaque decision-making and poorly calibrated human-automation interaction hinder safe adoption. Automation transparency is a design principle that aims to provide operators with insight into the automation's capabilities, goals, and effects, which is crucial for trust calibration. The Guidance-Navigation-Control (GNC) stack is a standard architecture for autonomous ship systems, and shore-based operational modes include remote supervision (RSM) and remote control (RCM).

## [VGGT-DP: Integrating 3D Vision and Proprioception for Robot Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18778) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VGGT-DP, a visuomotor policy framework that uses the pretrained 3D perception model VGGT as its visual encoder, integrates proprioceptive feedback, and employs a frame-wise token reuse mechanism to reduce inference latency. Experiments on MetaWorld tasks show it outperforms baselines like DP and DP3, especially in precision-critical and long-horizon scenarios. This work addresses a key limitation in visual imitation learning by improving spatial understanding and generalization through 3D geometric priors and proprioceptive feedback. It offers a practical latency reduction technique, making advanced vision foundation models more feasible for real-time robot control, which could benefit the broader robotics and AI community. The framework uses VGGT as the visual encoder and introduces a proprioception-guided visual learning strategy to align perception with internal robot states. The frame-wise token reuse (FTR) mechanism reuses cached VGGT aggregator tokens from overlapping frames, computing features only for the latest frame, and random token pruning is applied to enhance robustness and reduce overfitting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Visual imitation learning enables robots to learn manipulation skills from demonstrations, but existing approaches often neglect the visual encoder's structure, limiting spatial understanding. VGGT is a feed-forward neural network that infers 3D scene attributes like camera parameters, depth maps, and point maps from images. Proprioceptive feedback provides internal sensory data about the robot's own state, which is crucial for robust control.

## [Monotone and Separating Set Functions: Theory and Neural Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Monotone and Separating (MAS) set functions, which preserve the subset partial order in vector embeddings. It provides dimension bounds for finite ground sets, proves impossibility for infinite ground sets, and proposes a weakly MAS model with Hölder stability and universal approximation. This work addresses a fundamental problem in set-to-vector learning, offering theoretical guarantees that could improve models for set containment tasks. The proposed weakly MAS model provides a practical alternative for infinite ground sets, potentially benefiting applications in machine learning and combinatorial optimization. The paper establishes lower and upper bounds on the vector dimension required for MAS functions, depending on the cardinality of multisets and the ground set. For infinite ground sets, MAS functions are shown to be impossible, but the weakly MAS model is proven to be Hölder continuous and capable of universal approximation of monotone set functions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Monotonic functions preserve order between sets, and in machine learning, set functions often need to be permutation-invariant. Universal approximation theorems guarantee that certain neural network families can approximate any continuous function on sets, but incorporating order constraints like monotonicity is challenging. This paper bridges order theory and neural set models.

This paper introduces RoPAR, a part-aware masked autoregression model that leverages credible part-level data from web videos to generate human motion, handling occlusions and off-screen captures. It also contributes K700-M, a benchmark with about 200k real-world motion sequences. This work addresses a critical data quality issue in motion generation from web videos, enabling scalable use of noisy real-world data. It improves motion quality, semantic consistency, and diversity, benefiting character animation and related applications. The model decomposes the human body into five parts, detects credible parts in each frame, and encodes them with a part-aware variational autoencoder. A masked autoregressive generator predicts missing parts, and a diffusion head refines the full-body sequence; ablations show robustness even with up to 70% missing data.

## [Quantization's Impact on LLM Self-Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00282) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study investigates how quantization affects the quality and faithfulness of large language models' self-explanations, finding moderate declines. Specifically, quantization leads to up to 4.4% degradation in explanation quality and up to 3.9% in faithfulness, with user-perceived coherence and trustworthiness dropping by up to 8.5%. As quantization is widely used to deploy LLMs on resource-constrained devices, understanding its effect on self-explanations is crucial for maintaining transparency in high-stakes applications. This research provides valuable insights for practitioners who rely on LLM explanations for decision-making, highlighting the need to validate explanation quality after quantization. The study examines two types of self-explanations: natural language explanations (NLEs) and counterfactual examples, using three common quantization techniques at different bit widths. It finds that larger models show limited resilience in explanation quality but maintain faithfulness better than smaller models, and no quantization technique consistently excels across all metrics.

**Background**: Quantization reduces the precision of model weights, decreasing memory and computational requirements, which is essential for deploying LLMs on edge devices. This study builds on prior work on self-explanation faithfulness, extending it to the context of quantization.

Researchers introduced TangramPuzzle, a benchmark with 668 validated configurations and 1,336 instances for evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on compositional spatial reasoning under geometric constraints. It includes a Tangram Construction Expression (TCE) for machine-verifiable specifications and a multi-solution-aware verifier. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing MLLM evaluation by focusing on precise compositional spatial reasoning with rigorous geometric verification, which is crucial for applications like robotics and autonomous systems. It reveals that current MLLMs often prioritize silhouette matching over geometric constraints, highlighting a key limitation. The verifier evaluates geometric validity and silhouette fidelity using IoU and Hausdorff distance, without relying on a single reference assembly.

## [Framework for Ad Insertion in LLM Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel framework for inserting ads into LLM-generated responses, decoupling ad placement from generation and bidding from specific queries via 'genres'. It applies the VCG auction mechanism to ensure incentive compatibility and efficiency, with experiments showing clearance in ~1.25 seconds for 10^5 advertisers. This addresses the critical challenge of monetizing LLMs sustainably, balancing contextual coherence, efficiency, and ethics. It could enable practical ad-supported LLM services while protecting user privacy and ensuring transparency, impacting the AI industry and advertising ecosystem. The framework uses 'genres' as high-level semantic clusters for bidding, reducing privacy risks and computational burden. The VCG auction provides approximate DSIC, IR, and social welfare guarantees. An 'LLM-as-a-Judge' metric for contextual coherence correlates with human ratings at Spearman's ρ≈0.66.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM advertising is an emerging field where ads are inserted into conversational responses, unlike traditional search ads that rely on static keywords. Recent works have explored token-level and query-level bidding, but they struggle with contextual coherence, latency, and privacy. This paper introduces a genre-based approach to decouple bidding from sensitive real-time queries, and uses VCG auction for efficiency and fairness.

## [ICA: Information-Aware Credit Assignment for Long-Horizon Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10863) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Information-Aware Credit Assignment (ICA), a post hoc reward propagation method that estimates turn-level utility scores from rollout success rates and assigns dense rewards to intermediate steps that introduced high-utility information. It also proposes representing fetched webpages as rendered snapshots to preserve layout and multimodal content as stable observations. This work addresses a critical challenge in long-horizon reinforcement learning for web agents: sparse terminal rewards do not indicate which acquired information led to success. By providing dense, information-aware rewards, ICA can significantly improve learning efficiency and performance in open-ended web environments, potentially advancing the capabilities of autonomous information-seeking agents. The method is integrated with GSPO and consistently improves performance on benchmarks including BrowseComp, GAIA, Xbench-DS, and Seal-0. The approach uses rendered snapshots to avoid issues from text-based parsers that discard layout semantics and introduce noise. Code and datasets will be released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon reinforcement learning for information-seeking agents is difficult because terminal rewards only indicate whether the final answer is correct, not which information was useful. Traditional text-derived webpage observations often suffer from parsing, truncation, and summarization issues, leading to incomplete and unstable representations. The proposed evidence-centric framework treats acquired information as identifiable units, and ICA estimates the utility of each unit via posterior analysis, enabling dense reward assignment to key search turns.

PatientHub is a new unified and modular framework that standardizes the creation, simulation, and evaluation of LLM-based patients. It provides 16 patient simulators, a graph-based orchestrator for multi-turn, multi-session interactions, and a configurable LLM-as-a-judge evaluator. This framework addresses the fragmentation in LLM-based patient simulation by providing a reproducible pipeline, which could accelerate research and development in AI training and therapeutic assessment. It enables fair comparison and reuse across different simulators, potentially improving the quality and consistency of counselor training. The framework includes a command-line interface for generating patient profiles, running simulations, and applying rubric-driven evaluation at the turn and session level. The authors demonstrate extensibility by prototyping a new simulator variant with minimal additional code, and the code and data are publicly available on GitHub.

PatientHub aims to consolidate these efforts into a single, standardized pipeline. The framework leverages techniques like LLM-as-a-judge for evaluation and graph-based orchestration for complex multi-turn interactions.

This paper presents a systematic study of continued pretraining (CPT) and post-training to enhance Estonian capabilities in multilingual LLMs, using Llama 3.1 8B and Apertus 8B as base models. The approach includes Estonian-enriched multilingual replay, English supervised fine-tuning, preference optimization, and chat vector merging, yielding significant gains for the English-centric Llama model. This work addresses the critical gap in multilingual LLM performance for low-resource languages like Estonian, offering a practical recipe for improving single-language capabilities without catastrophic forgetting. The findings could guide future efforts to adapt LLMs for other underrepresented languages, benefiting both research and real-world applications. The study uses Llama 3.1 8B and Apertus 8B, applying CPT with Estonian-enriched multilingual replay, followed by mostly English supervised fine-tuning, preference optimization, and chat vector merging. Evaluations on Estonian benchmarks, pairwise human evaluation, and an Estonian Chatbot Arena-style setup show consistent improvements, though some English capabilities regress, which chat vector merging substantially restores.

## [msData: Millisecond-Resolution 5G Dataset for Time Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16497) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced msData, a novel dataset capturing millisecond-resolution wireless and traffic conditions from an operational 5G deployment, expanding time series foundation models (TSFMs) to high-frequency domains. The dataset includes short-term forecasting use cases with prediction horizons from 1 to 96 milliseconds. This dataset addresses a critical gap in high-frequency time series data, which existing large-scale datasets largely lack, potentially improving TSFM performance in real-world applications like network management. It also introduces wireless networks as a new domain, complementing existing domains like energy and finance. The dataset is sourced from an operational 5G Radio Access Network (RAN) and includes traffic classes like YouTube and Web Browsing with static and train mobility patterns. Benchmarks show that most TSFM configurations perform poorly in both zero-shot and fine-tuned settings, underscoring the need for high-frequency data in pre-training.

## [Thesis on Ollivier-Ricci Curvature Extends to Directed Graphs for GNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14211) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This thesis presents a comprehensive exposition of Ollivier-Ricci curvature, including novel extensions to directed graphs and applications to graph neural networks. It bridges theoretical results from Riemannian geometry with practical algorithms in network science and graph machine learning. This work is significant because it extends Ollivier-Ricci curvature to directed graphs, which are common in real-world networks, and demonstrates its utility in graph neural networks. It provides a theoretical foundation for using geometric concepts to improve graph-based machine learning models. The thesis covers the 1-Wasserstein distance and optimal transport theory, and presents proofs connecting Ollivier-Ricci curvature to classical Ricci curvature, including extensions of Bonnet-Myers and Levy-Gromov theorems. It also discusses Lin-Lu-Yau graph curvature and Jost-Liu combinatorial bounds, before introducing novel directed graph extensions.

**Background**: Ollivier-Ricci curvature is a discrete adaptation of classical Ricci curvature that measures the overlap of vertex neighborhoods using the Wasserstein distance. It connects optimal transport with Riemannian geometry and has been applied to graph analysis. Lin-Lu-Yau curvature is a variant defined on graph edges via lazy random walks, and both have been used to analyze graph structure and improve graph neural networks.

## [RA-CMF: Region-Adaptive Conditional MeanFlow for CT Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00901) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces RA-CMF, a novel CT image reconstruction method that combines a conditional MeanFlow network with a reinforcement learning-driven policy network for spatially adaptive refinement. The approach achieves high accuracy in tumor regions, with an average radiomic feature CCC of 0.93, PSNR of 31.94, and SSIM of 0.97. This work addresses the challenge of inconsistent CT image quality across different imaging protocols and scanners, which is critical for accurate diagnosis and treatment planning. By integrating reinforcement learning for adaptive refinement, RA-CMF could improve the reliability of CT-based radiomics and enhance clinical decision-making. The policy network predicts tile-wise refinement budgets, stopping criteria, and total budget allocation, trained via policy gradient to maximize reconstruction quality while minimizing computation. The method shows overall image quality improvements with average PSNR of 34.23 and SSIM of 0.95, indicating effective global enhancement alongside regional focus.

## [Robust Diffusion Solver for Inverse Problems with Outliers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09477) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel diffusion model-based solver for inverse problems that explicitly estimates noise and uses an iteratively reweighted least squares objective with Huber loss to handle outliers, employing conjugate gradient for efficiency. The method outperforms recent DM-based approaches in most cases across linear and nonlinear tasks. Outliers are common in real-world measurements and can severely degrade the performance of existing diffusion model-based inverse problem solvers. This work addresses a practical gap by providing a robust solution, which could improve reliability in applications like image reconstruction and medical imaging. The method first refines measurements via explicit noise estimation, then formulates an iteratively reweighted least squares objective based on Huber loss. To avoid delicate learning rate tuning, it employs the conjugate gradient method with an efficient updating strategy. Code is available at the provided GitHub repository.

**Background**: Diffusion models have shown remarkable performance in solving inverse problems, but they typically assume clean measurements and struggle with outliers. Robust statistics techniques like Huber loss and iteratively reweighted least squares (IRLS) are designed to mitigate the influence of outliers. The conjugate gradient method is an efficient iterative algorithm for solving large linear systems and optimization problems, often used to avoid manual tuning of step sizes.

## [CoWorld-VLA: Multi-Expert World Model for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10426) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoWorld-VLA introduces a multi-expert world reasoning framework for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models in autonomous driving, using explicit world representations as conditions for action planning. It constructs four types of expert tokens—semantic interaction, geometric structure, dynamic evolution, and ego trajectory—and employs a diffusion-based hierarchical multi-expert fusion planner. This work addresses limitations of existing reasoning mechanisms in VLA models, such as textual Chain-of-Thought (CoT) failing to preserve continuous spatiotemporal structure and latent world reasoning being difficult to use directly for action generation. By providing planner-accessible conditioning signals, CoWorld-VLA could improve end-to-end autonomous driving performance, particularly in collision avoidance and trajectory accuracy. The framework extracts complementary world information through multi-source supervision and encodes it into expert tokens within the VLA. Experiments on NAVSIM v1 & v2 demonstrate future-scene modeling capability and strong planning performance, with ablation studies validating the complementarity of expert tokens and their effectiveness as planning conditions.

World models aim to predict future states of the environment, providing a structured understanding that can guide planning. CoWorld-VLA builds on these concepts by using multiple expert world representations to condition action generation.

ForceFlow is a new force-aware reactive framework that uses flow matching and asymmetric multimodal fusion to improve contact-rich manipulation policies. It achieves a 37% success rate improvement over the strong baseline ForceVLA across six real-world tasks, with lower cost. This work addresses a significant challenge in robotics: contact-rich manipulation, which requires precise force feedback and control. By demonstrating robust generalization and self-regulation, ForceFlow could advance imitation learning and robot control, making robots more capable in real-world physical interactions. ForceFlow uses an asymmetric multimodal fusion architecture that treats force as a global regulatory signal, combined with a joint prediction paradigm. It also introduces a Vision-to-Force (V2F) handover mechanism that decouples spatial generalization from contact regulation, and demonstrates zero-shot out-of-distribution generalization.

Asymmetric multimodal fusion treats different modalities (e.g., vision and force) as having different roles or reliability, which can improve policy learning.

## [SGSD: Skill-Conditioned Gated Self-Distillation Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Skill-Conditioned Gated Self-Distillation (SGSD), a novel method that uses an experience-derived skill bank as teacher-side privileged information for on-policy self-distillation. SGSD validates teacher hypotheses via a gated objective, improving LLM reasoning on mathematical benchmarks, e.g., outperforming GRPO by 6.2% and OPSD by 1.7% on Qwen3-1.7B across AIME24, AIME25, and HMMT25. This work addresses a key limitation of existing self-distillation methods that assume trusted privileged information, such as reference answers. By allowing PI to come from a skill bank that may contain irrelevant or misleading skills, SGSD makes self-distillation more practical and robust, potentially improving LLM reasoning in real-world scenarios where perfect supervision is unavailable. SGSD retrieves skill-mistake pairs to construct a multi-teacher pool, where each skill-conditioned teacher scores the same plain-prompt student rollout. The verifier validates each teacher's polarity, and a robust gated objective distills informative teacher-student disagreements while suppressing uncertain or extreme signals. Experiments show consistent improvements over GRPO and competitiveness with answer-conditioned OPSD under a weaker PI assumption.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (SD) improves LLM reasoning by using teacher-side privileged information (PI) to convert sparse verifier outcomes into dense token-level supervision. Traditional methods rely on trusted PI like reference answers or successful traces, but this paper explores using a skill bank derived from experience, which may contain irrelevant or misleading skills. The proposed SGSD formulates skill-based SD as teacher hypothesis validation rather than unconditional imitation, using a gated objective to handle noisy supervision.

## [TW-LegalBench: New Benchmark for Taiwanese Legal AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TW-LegalBench is a new benchmark for evaluating large language models on Taiwanese legal tasks, featuring over 16,000 multiple-choice questions, 117 essay questions, and 14,000+ legal judgment prediction instances. The study evaluates 13 LLMs and finds that top models pass the lawyer qualification exam threshold but struggle with exact legal article citation. This benchmark fills a critical gap in jurisdiction-specific legal AI evaluation, as most existing legal benchmarks focus on English or Simplified Chinese sources. It provides a comprehensive testbed for assessing LLMs in a civil-law context, which is essential for developing reliable legal AI applications in Taiwan and similar jurisdictions. The benchmark includes three task types: MCQs from five years of official exams across 18 professional domains, essay questions with official scoring rubrics, and legal judgment prediction covering hundreds of crime categories. Evaluation uses accuracy for MCQs, a decomposed LLM-as-Judge framework for essays, and metrics for sentencing accuracy and statute citation for LJP.

## [ReCARE Framework Preserves Benign Co-occurring Concepts in Diffusion Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the CARE score, a metric to quantify preservation of benign co-occurring concepts during diffusion model unlearning, and proposes the ReCARE framework that automatically constructs a CARE-set to safeguard these concepts while erasing only the target concept. This addresses a critical flaw in existing unlearning methods that often suppress benign concepts like 'person' when erasing 'nudity', limiting practical deployment. By balancing robust erasure, utility, and concept preservation, ReCARE improves the safety and usability of diffusion models in real-world applications. ReCARE automatically builds a CARE-set of benign co-occurring tokens from target images and uses it during training. Experiments across target concepts like Nudity, Van Gogh style, and Tench object show state-of-the-art performance in balancing erasure, utility, and CARE preservation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative AI systems that can create images from text prompts, but they can also generate harmful content. Unlearning is a technique to remove specific concepts from these models, yet it often inadvertently removes associated benign concepts. The CARE score and ReCARE framework aim to solve this by explicitly measuring and preserving co-occurring concepts.

<a id="item-349"></a>
## [Baseline Capture: When Incumbent Becomes the Specification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13393) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of 'baseline capture,' a pathology where an incumbent implementation becomes the de facto specification, hindering computational reformulation for modern accelerators. It separates the question of whether a demand can be met from whether a replacement is acceptable. This work provides a clear lens for evaluating computational reformulations, which is crucial for achieving speed and energy gains on modern accelerators. It helps researchers and practitioners avoid the trap of using an incumbent's output as the sole acceptance criterion, thereby enabling more innovative and efficient designs. The paper illustrates the pattern with several case studies, including shortest-path routing, learnable audio frontends, ZIP-215 for Ed25519, and CESM-ECT for climate models. A single-GEMM audio frontend achieved 1.64x-3.29x speedup and up to 3.03x less energy, demonstrating the practical benefits of avoiding baseline capture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: The test-oracle problem in software testing refers to the challenge of determining correct expected outputs for test cases. Implementation bias in requirements engineering occurs when requirements are expressed in terms of a specific implementation rather than the underlying need. This paper builds on these concepts to define 'baseline capture' and proposes 'buying a verifier'—making a demand explicit, operational, and independent of the incumbent—as a solution.

TabDPT-Turbo introduces a faster tabular foundation model that achieves performance comparable to TabDPT v1.1 on TabArena-Lite, CC18, and CTR23 benchmarks, but at orders of magnitude faster inference. The model is released as TabDPT v1.2. This efficiency improvement addresses a key practical limitation of tabular foundation models, making them more viable for compute-constrained or latency-sensitive applications. It could accelerate adoption of in-context learning for tabular data in real-world scenarios. The model eliminates the need for retrieval by using long-context pre-training, combined with architectural improvements and SSL pre-training on a newly-sourced, larger corpus of real data. In experiments, TabDPT-Turbo is the fastest model overall among leading foundation models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular foundation models use in-context learning to make predictions on new datasets without fine-tuning, by conditioning on labeled examples at inference time. However, recent approaches with cell-based architectures or retrieval have sacrificed efficiency for raw performance, limiting their use in compute-limited or speed-critical settings. TabDPT-Turbo takes an alternative approach, sticking with row-based attention and using long-context pre-training to avoid retrieval, achieving both speed and comparable accuracy.

## [New Framework Measures Epistemic Transfer in AI-Assisted Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework and evaluation protocol for measuring epistemic transfer in AI-assisted verification, defining the Epistemic Transfer Effect (ETE) and Tool-Removal Cost (TRC) metrics. It distinguishes epistemic transfer from related concepts and proposes a diagnostic space to categorize outcomes. This work addresses a gap in current evaluation methods by focusing on what users can do after AI tool removal, which is crucial for understanding long-term skill development and independence. It provides practical tools for researchers in HCI and AI evaluation to assess whether AI tools build capabilities or merely provide temporary assistance. The protocol combines answer-first and evidence-first AI conditions with active-practice and no-practice controls, delayed tests on held-out claims, and behavioral measures. The diagnostic space separates capability building, capability plus tool advantage, epistemic inertness or de-skilling, and verification on loan.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: AI-assisted verification tools are typically evaluated while the tool is present, but this paper asks what users can do on their own afterward. The paper introduces ETE and TRC as simple quantities to study this effect and provides a practical evaluation protocol for experiments or field studies.

## [ER-KAN: Robust KAN for Noisy, Data-Scarce Scientific ML](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14773) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ER-KAN, a Kolmogorov-Arnold Network variant that combines shared Gaussian RBF bases, curriculum noise injection, and entropy-weighted regularization. It demonstrates superior robustness to noise and data scarcity compared to existing KAN variants and MLPs. This work addresses a critical gap in the efficient-KAN literature, which has largely ignored noisy, data-scarce conditions. By introducing a robust architecture and a new evaluation metric (noise degradation ratio), it encourages more realistic benchmarking and could improve the reliability of scientific machine learning models in practical settings. ER-KAN uses a 595-parameter network that matches MLP accuracy at moderate noise and degrades more gracefully as noise increases. The paper evaluates on eight analytic functions, a damped harmonic oscillator PINN (4.2x lower solution MSE than MLP), and a Burgers' equation PINN where all models fail to converge, a limitation the authors report transparently.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) are a neural architecture inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem, replacing fixed activation functions with learnable univariate functions. Efficient-KAN variants (e.g., ChebyKAN, wavelet KAN) have been benchmarked mostly on clean data, which may conceal performance differences under noise. Gaussian radial basis functions (RBFs) are commonly used in neural networks for their locality and smoothness, and noise injection is a known technique to improve generalization.

## [FreqDiff: Frequency-Aware Diffusion for Temporal Knowledge Graph Extrapolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20804) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FreqDiff, a frequency-aware diffusion framework for temporal knowledge graph (TKG) extrapolation, which uses context-aware spectral calibration to better distinguish query-specific evidence from historical facts. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on four public TKG benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation of existing diffusion-based TKG extrapolation methods, which often dilute target-discriminative signals by aggregating conditioning on subject histories. By improving the accuracy of future fact prediction, FreqDiff could enhance applications such as event forecasting and decision support in dynamic environments. FreqDiff formulates future object prediction as query-slot denoising and develops a dual-stream denoiser that integrates temporal dependency modeling with context-aware spectral calibration. It also introduces a frequency-domain regularizer to align the denoised target with the gold object in spectral space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal knowledge graphs (TKGs) extend traditional knowledge graphs by incorporating time, allowing the representation of how facts evolve over time. TKG extrapolation aims to infer future facts from historical relational data, which is crucial for predictive tasks. Diffusion models are generative frameworks that learn to denoise noisy data, and they have been recently applied to TKG extrapolation to model uncertainty. However, existing methods often fail to distinguish query-specific evidence from irrelevant historical facts, leading to diluted signals.

The paper introduces DELE-w0.5, a method that infers robot actions directly from compact future latent states, bypassing video generation. In 480 real-robot trials across four long-horizon tasks, it achieved 62.5% full-task success and 81.3% macro ordered-stage progress, outperforming the strongest baseline by 47.5 and 30.7 percentage points respectively. This challenges the common practice of using video generation in World-Action Models (WAMs) for robotic manipulation, potentially reducing computational cost and latency. It could lead to more efficient and effective robot control systems, impacting both research and industrial applications. DELE-w0.5 models how the physical world changes under robot actions rather than visual appearance frame by frame, removing high-dimensional visual redundancy. The future latent state serves as an explicit bridge between world modeling and action generation, enabling cheaper training and low-latency inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: World-Action Models (WAMs) typically build robot control on video-generation backbones, jointly predicting dense future visual trajectories and robot actions. However, the paper argues that intermediate frames only describe visual transitions and consume substantial capacity without directly specifying physical outcomes. DELE-w0.5 instead focuses on predicting the state the world will reach after an action, which is more aligned with the goal of manipulation.

## [SANE Stabilizes Delta-Rule Models Under Extreme Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22354) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces State Anomaly Neutralization (SANE), a method that applies adaptive tanh compression at chunk boundaries to stabilize Delta-Rule recurrent models like RWKV-7 during extreme-context extrapolation. SANE matches baseline performance on 11 short-context benchmarks while retaining functional reasoning after a 100M-token prefix, where the baseline fails due to numerical overflow. This work addresses a critical stability issue in recurrent models for long-context AI, which is essential for scaling to ultra-long sequences. By providing a simple, effective solution without significant benchmark degradation, SANE could enable more reliable deployment of efficient recurrent architectures in real-world applications. SANE operates within a safe threshold range (3 ≤ α ≤ 5) and preserves the intra-chunk parallel structure. The paper also reveals a capacity-stability trade-off: overly permissive thresholds (α ≥ 8) remain numerically stable but lose reasoning capability entirely.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Delta-Rule recurrent models maintain a fixed-size state, enabling O(1) inference memory, but can become unstable under extreme-context extrapolation. RWKV-7 is a recent example of such models, using a generalized delta rule with vector-valued gating. The paper identifies a failure pattern of localized norm explosion on a sparse substrate, distinct from global state saturation.

## [Update Masking in Federated Learning: How Much Regularization Survives Averaging?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23286) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides the first theoretical analysis of how update masking, a technique that randomly masks parameter updates, affects implicit regularization in federated learning. It proves that client-specific masks weaken the regularization effect by the cohort size, while shared masks can restore it depending on gradient diversity. This work addresses the important problem of non-IID data in federated learning by explaining why certain methods that rely on implicit regularization fail. The insights could guide the design of more effective federated optimization algorithms, potentially improving generalization across heterogeneous clients. In experiments on CIFAR-10, the restoration factor for shared masks was 1.19 out of a possible 10, but turning off minibatch sampling raised it to 8.96. Changing data heterogeneity a thousandfold left the factor between 1.17 and 1.50, and configurations that preserve regularization train too poorly to be practical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains a shared model across clients without pooling data, and non-IID data distributions can hurt generalization. Implicit regularization, such as that from noise in parameter updates, helps find flat minima that generalize better, but its interaction with federated averaging and update masking was previously unclear.

<a id="item-362"></a>
## [RAG vs. Deterministic Tax Engine in Multi-Agent Financial Advisory: A 2x2 Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23908) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A 2x2 factorial experiment on a multi-agent financial advisory system found that enabling a deterministic tax optimization engine significantly reduced tax savings by about 55 percentage points, while RAG context had no significant effect. The RAG-only condition achieved the highest mean tax savings at 47.7%. This study challenges the assumption that adding domain-specific computation engines to LLM-based agents always improves performance, showing they can introduce conflicting signals. It highlights the need for careful evaluation of tool integration in AI systems, particularly in fintech applications. The experiment used a repeated-measures ANOVA, revealing a significant main effect of the tax engine (F(1,29)=9.17, p=.005, η²p=.240), but no significant RAG effect (p=.841) or interaction (p=.553). The baseline condition (no RAG, no engine) performed second-best with 30.6% tax savings, suggesting the LLM's internal knowledge may suffice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Tax-loss harvesting is a strategy where investors sell losing securities to offset capital gains, reducing tax liability. Multi-agent systems use multiple AI agents to handle complex tasks like financial advisory, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by retrieving relevant external information. A 2x2 factorial experiment tests two factors (RAG and tax engine) at two levels each (on/off), and repeated-measures ANOVA analyzes data from the same subjects under all conditions.

## [Personalized Sustainable Diet Recommendation via Constraint-Aware Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24274) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a personalized sustainable diet recommendation model that incorporates sustainability as learnable constraints rather than user preferences, and presents a new dataset, SusDiet, containing about 150k recipes with broad sustainability indicators. This work bridges the gap between population-level sustainability modeling and individual-level adoption, offering a framework that aligns personal dietary choices with planetary health. It could inform future sustainable diet interventions and policy-making, and its dataset supports further research in this domain. The model uses a constraint-aware decision-making mechanism, where sustainability constraints are learned and integrated into the recommendation process. Experiments on SusDiet show that the method promotes more sustainable choices without compromising individual preference, though the paper does not provide detailed performance metrics or comparisons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Sustainable diets require balancing nutrition, affordability, cultural acceptability, and environmental impact. Traditional recommendation systems often model user preferences, but sustainability is a global constraint that must be satisfied. Constraint-based recommender systems use predefined constraints to guide recommendations, and this work extends that idea by learning sustainability constraints from data. The SusDiet dataset, with 150k recipes, provides a resource for evaluating such approaches.

## [Preference Optimization for Non-Verbal Vocalization Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24163) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper systematically studies preference optimization for non-verbal vocalization (NV) synthesis in TTS, proposing an NV-aware character error rate (NV-CER) metric and evaluating DPO-based methods on a new benchmark (Emilia-NV and augmented NV-Bench covering 18 NV types). This work addresses a gap in expressive TTS by providing a systematic framework for optimizing NV generation, which is crucial for natural and emotionally expressive speech. The proposed NV-CER metric enables controllable optimization without altering the underlying algorithm, offering practical insights for post-training in expressive TTS systems. The study focuses on preference signals, preference-pair construction, and DPO-based optimization objectives. NV-CER treats NV tags as distinct output symbols and computes a weighted pinyin-based CER over both verbal and non-verbal content, enabling controllable optimization of NV realization. Experiments include objective, LLM-based, and human evaluations, providing converging evidence for the findings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Non-verbal vocalizations (NVs) such as laughter, coughs, and sighs are essential for expressive text-to-speech (TTS) synthesis. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a method used to align TTS models with human preferences by comparing preferred and less preferred outputs. Character Error Rate (CER) is a common metric for evaluating speech recognition and synthesis accuracy, typically measuring character-level discrepancies between generated and reference transcripts.

## [Multimodal Framework Predicts Student Engagement in Online Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24340) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a multimodal framework that integrates spatiotemporal features from pretrained video, audio, and image encoders with structured behavioral cues like head pose, gaze, facial action units, emotion, and wavelet-based audio features to predict student engagement in online learning videos. The framework uses a Perceiver IO latent bottleneck, models student and instructor personalities as variational posteriors, and employs evidential regression and spectral-normalized Gaussian process classification heads for uncertainty-aware prediction. Accurate engagement prediction is crucial for improving online education, as it can enable real-time interventions and personalized learning experiences. This work addresses the challenges of inter-person variability and annotation subjectivity, and its emphasis on well-calibrated uncertainty metrics is essential for deploying engagement models in real-world educational tools. The framework was benchmarked on the CASED challenge test set, where all participating methods converged near random-chance performance, highlighting the dataset's difficulty. Despite this, the framework achieved competitive performance while uniquely offering well-calibrated uncertainty metrics, demonstrating the importance of reliable risk-quantification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Student engagement is a multidimensional construct comprising behavioral, emotional, and cognitive states, making its prediction from online tutoring videos challenging. The CASED dataset reveals high inter-person variability and subjectivity in engagement annotations, complicating the task. Multimodal learning integrates various data types, such as video, audio, and behavioral signals, to improve prediction accuracy. Uncertainty-aware prediction methods, like evidential regression and Gaussian processes, provide confidence estimates that are crucial for reliable deployment in educational settings.

## [Boundary Strategies for Constrained Hyperparameter Optimization in Streaming Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24712) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces four boundary constraint-handling strategies for constrained hyperparameter optimization in online learning scenarios, and empirically demonstrates that these strategies outperform the traditional 'boundary' strategy on established datasets. This work addresses the underexplored problem of hyperparameter optimization in streaming data, where models must self-adjust in real-time. Improved constraint handling can enhance the performance and reliability of online learning systems, which are increasingly important in dynamic environments. The proposed strategies are designed for constrained numerical optimization problems where hyperparameters are bounded. The paper compares these strategies against the standard 'boundary' approach, which simply clamps out-of-bound values to the limits, and shows superior performance on benchmark datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperparameter optimization is crucial for model performance, but most research focuses on batch learning. Streaming data requires online learning, where models must update continuously, making self-adjusting hyperparameters essential. Many hyperparameters are constrained within bounded search spaces, and optimization operators may produce invalid solutions, necessitating boundary constraint-handling techniques.

## [Heuristic Algorithm for Fuzzy Text Segmentation Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.13427) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a heuristic algorithm for fuzzy segmentation of text into segments matching a fuzzy pattern, using a prefix structure to track the mapping of text segments to fuzzy properties. It proves that in the special case of fuzzy string matching (unit-length segments), the algorithm finds all matching segments, and it also solves the best segmentation problem via dynamic programming. This work contributes to text processing and pattern matching by addressing the niche problem of fuzzy segmentation, which has applications in data clustering and approximate string matching. The proven special case adds theoretical value, potentially inspiring further research in fuzzy matching algorithms. The algorithm leverages a prefix structure (similar to a trie) to efficiently track the mapping process. The paper also addresses the best segmentation problem using dynamic programming, and includes a proof that the heuristic is exact for the fuzzy string matching case.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Fuzzy string matching, also known as approximate string matching, is a technique to find strings that match a pattern approximately rather than exactly, often using edit distance measures. Text segmentation is the process of dividing text into meaningful units, and prefix structures like tries are used for efficient string storage and lookup. This paper combines these concepts to address a specific clustering problem.

## [Olapa-MCoT Boosts Chinese Math Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.17535) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Olapa-MCoT, a method combining SimRRHF and IDRL to enhance Chinese mathematical reasoning in Llama-2-13B, achieving a 36% accuracy improvement (up to 50%) without degrading original capabilities, and even a 4% gain in English reasoning. This work addresses the challenge of efficiently improving LLMs' performance in new languages under limited data and compute, which is crucial for multilingual AI deployment. It offers a cost-effective approach that could be applied to other language-specific enhancements, benefiting the broader NLP community. The method introduces SimRRHF, which adds a similarity loss to RRHF to constrain optimization direction, and IDRL (Incorrect Data Relearning) to improve learning from difficult examples. Experiments show significant gains on Chinese math reasoning while maintaining or improving other capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models like Llama-2 often excel in English but underperform in other languages due to training data imbalance. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting and fine-tuning methods like RRHF (Rank Responses to align Human Feedback) are used to improve reasoning, but adapting to new languages typically requires extensive resources. This paper proposes a resource-efficient approach to bridge that gap.

This study applies the YOLOv26s model, typically used for object detection, to classify autism spectrum disorder (ASD) in children from facial images, achieving 92.86% accuracy and an F1-score of 0.9291. This work highlights the potential of deep learning to provide accessible, non-invasive ASD screening, which could enable earlier intervention and improve outcomes for children. It also demonstrates the versatility of YOLO architectures beyond object detection, potentially broadening their application in medical imaging and diagnostics. The study reports a high F1-score of 0.9291, indicating a good balance between precision and recall, which is crucial in medical screening to minimize false negatives. However, the paper is an arXiv preprint and lacks details on dataset size, demographics, and comparison with other classification-specific models, which are necessary to assess generalizability.

## [STA-Net: Lightweight Plant Disease Classification with Decoupled Shape-Texture Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03754) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces STA-Net, a lightweight plant disease classification model that uses a decoupled shape and texture attention module (STAM) and a training-free neural architecture search method (DeepMAD) to achieve 89.00% accuracy and an F1 score of 88.96% on the CCMT dataset with only 401K parameters and 51.1M FLOPs. This work addresses the challenge of deploying high-precision plant disease diagnosis models on edge devices, which is crucial for precision agriculture and food security. By integrating domain knowledge through decoupled attention, STA-Net offers a promising path for efficient and accurate edge-deployed AI in agriculture. STAM splits attention into two branches: one using deformable convolutions (DCNv4) for shape awareness and the other using a Gabor filter bank for texture awareness. Ablation studies confirm that STAM significantly improves performance over baseline and standard attention models, and the source code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 26, 04:00

**Background**: Plant disease classification is vital for food security, but high-precision models are often too large for edge devices. Lightweight networks typically use generic attention mechanisms that fail to capture subtle pathological features like irregular lesion shapes and complex textures. DeepMAD is a training-free neural architecture search method that designs efficient CNN backbones, and DCNv4 is an efficient deformable convolution operator that enhances dynamic property and expressive power.
